\begin{split}{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}{}^{x}\big({\mathrm{Res}}^{\mu\delta}_{(x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu)\delta}{\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V\big)&\simeq{}^{x}({\mathpzc F}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}{\mathrm{Res}}^{\mu\delta}_{(x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu)\delta}{\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)\\
&\simeq{}^{x}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{\mu}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}V),\end{split}

where we have used (
4.2.26
) for the second isomorphism.

For
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, we set

(4.2.33)

b_{w}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}_{d}\psi_{\rho_{d}(w)}{f}_{d}\in C_{d}.

Let
V\in C_{\mu}\!\operatorname{-mod}
. Note that
for any
x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}
, we have

{f}_{\lambda}b_{x}{f}_{\mu}\otimes V\subseteq{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}V.

Let
\leq
be a total order refining the Bruhat order on
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.
For
x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}
, we consider the submodules

\displaystyle\Phi_{\leq x}(V)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\lambda}b_{y}{f}_{\mu}\otimes V\subseteq{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}V,

\displaystyle\Phi_{<x}(V)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y<x}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\lambda}b_{y}{f}_{\mu}\otimes V\subseteq{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}V.

Theorem 4.2.34
.

(Gelfand-Graev Mackey Theorem)

Let
\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(d)
, and
V\in C_{\mu}\!\operatorname{-mod}
.
Then we have the filtration

(\Phi_{\leq x}(V))_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}}

of

{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}V
.
Moreover, for the sub-quotients of the filtration we have

\Phi_{\leq x}(V)/\Phi_{<x}(V)\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}^{\lambda}{}^{x}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{\mu}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}V).

Proof.

We identify

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}V

\displaystyle={f}_{\lambda}C_{d}{f}_{\mu}\otimes_{C_{\mu}}V={f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{d}1_{\mu\delta}{f}_{\mu}V

\displaystyle={f}_{\lambda}1_{\lambda\delta}\hat{C}_{d}1_{\mu\delta}\otimes_{\hat{C}_{\mu}}\hat{C}_{\mu}{f}_{\mu}\otimes_{C_{\mu}}V

\displaystyle={f}_{\lambda}{\mathrm{Res}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda\delta}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{d\delta}_{\mu\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V.

By Theorem
4.1.16
, this has a filtration

({f}_{\lambda}\bar{F}_{\leq x}({\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V))_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}}

where

\displaystyle{f}_{\lambda}\bar{F}_{\leq x}({\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}{f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{\lambda}1_{\lambda\delta}b_{y}1_{\mu\delta}\otimes_{\hat{C}_{\mu}}({\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}{f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{\lambda}1_{\lambda\delta}b_{y}1_{\mu\delta}\otimes_{\hat{C}_{\mu}}\hat{C}_{\mu}{f}_{\mu}\otimes_{C_{\mu}}V

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}{f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{\lambda}1_{\lambda\delta}b_{y}1_{\mu\delta}{f}_{\mu}\otimes_{C_{\mu}}V

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}{f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{\lambda}1_{\lambda\delta}b_{y}{f}_{\mu}\otimes_{C_{\mu}}V

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}{f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{\lambda}{f}_{\lambda}b_{y}{f}_{\mu}\otimes_{C_{\mu}}V

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\lambda}b_{y}{f}_{\mu}\otimes_{C_{\mu}}V

\displaystyle=\Phi_{\leq x}(V),

where we have used Lemma
4.2.31
for the fifth equality.
Now, using Theorem
4.1.16
, we also have

\displaystyle\Phi_{\leq x}(V)/\Phi_{<x}(V)

\displaystyle={f}_{\lambda}\bar{F}_{\leq x}({\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)\,/\,{f}_{\lambda}\bar{F}_{<x}({\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)

\displaystyle\simeq{f}_{\lambda}\big(\bar{F}_{\leq x}({\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)/\bar{F}_{<x}({\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{f}_{\lambda}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}_{(\lambda\cap x\mu)\delta}^{\lambda\delta}\,{}^{x}({\mathrm{Res}}^{\mu\delta}_{(x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu)\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)

We may assume that
\lambda
has more than one non-zero part since otherwise there is nothing to prove. It follows that the composition
\lambda\cap x\mu
also has more than one non-zero part.
So

\displaystyle{f}_{\lambda}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}_{(\lambda\cap x\mu)\delta}^{\lambda\delta}\,{}^{x}({\mathrm{Res}}^{\mu\delta}_{(x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu)\delta}{\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)

\displaystyle\simeq\,

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}_{(\lambda\cap x\mu)\delta}^{\lambda\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}{}^{x}({\mathrm{Res}}^{\mu\delta}_{(x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu)\delta}{\mathpzc G}_{\mu}V)

\displaystyle\simeq\,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}^{\lambda}\,{}^{x}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{\mu}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}V),

where we have used Lemma
4.2.22
for the first isomorphism and (
4.2.32
), (
4.2.26
) for the second isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 4.2.35
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
f_{\lambda}C_{d}f_{\omega_{d}}=C_{\lambda}f_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}f_{\omega_{d}}
.

Proof.

We have
C_{\lambda}f_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}f_{\omega_{d}}=f_{\lambda}C_{\lambda}f_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}f_{\omega_{d}}\subseteq f_{\lambda}C_{d}f_{\omega_{d}}
. On the other hand, by the Gelfand-Graev Mackey, we have

f_{\lambda}C_{d}f_{\omega_{d}}\simeq{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}C_{\omega_{d}}=\sum_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\lambda}b_{x}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes C_{\omega_{d}}

So

f_{\lambda}C_{d}f_{\omega_{d}}=\sum_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\lambda}b_{x}{f}_{\omega_{d}}=\sum_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}}C_{\lambda}b_{x}{f}_{\omega_{d}}

and it remains to note, using Lemma
4.2.31
with
\lambda=\mu=\omega_{d}
, that
b_{x}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\in f_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}f_{\omega_{d}}
for any
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.
∎

4.2g.
The isomorphism
{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\cong H_{d}(A_{\ell})

Following
[
33
, §4.2c]
, we have the following elements of
C_{1}
:

(4.2.36)

\displaystyle\dot{u}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}^{0}y_{p}{f}^{0},

(4.2.37)

\displaystyle\dot{z}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\textstyle\sum_{j\in J}{f}^{j}y_{p-j-1}y_{p-j}{f}^{j},

(4.2.38)

\displaystyle\dot{a}^{[j,j-1]}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}^{j}\psi_{p-1}\cdots\psi_{p-2j}{f}^{j-1}\qquad(j=1,\dots,\ell-1),

(4.2.39)

\displaystyle\dot{a}^{[j-1,j]}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=(-1)^{j}{f}^{j-1}\psi_{p-2j-1}\cdots\psi_{p-1}{f}^{j}\qquad(j=1,\dots,\ell-1).

We have the parabolic subalgebra

C_{1}^{\otimes d}=C_{\omega_{d}}\subseteq{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}.

Given an element
x\in C_{1}
and
1\leq r\leq d
and recalling the notation (
2.2.4
), we have the element

x_{r}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes x\otimes{f}_{1}^{\otimes(d-r)}\in C_{1}^{\otimes d}\subseteq{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}.

So we now also have the elements

(4.2.40)

\dot{u}_{r},\,\dot{z}_{r},\,\dot{a}^{[j,j-1]}_{r},\,\dot{a}^{[j-1,j]}_{r}\in{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\qquad(1\leq r\leq d).

Further, following
[
33
, (4.3.28)]
, we denote
\psi(r)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\psi_{r}\psi_{r+1}\cdots\psi_{r+p-1}
for
r=1,\dots,p
, and
set

(4.2.41)

\upsigma\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}_{\omega_{2}}\psi(p)\psi(p-1)\cdots\psi(1){f}_{\omega_{2}}\in{f}_{\omega_{2}}C_{2}{f}_{\omega_{2}}

Now, following
[
33
, (4.4.11)]
, we define
\dot{s}\in{f}_{\omega_{2}}C_{2}{f}_{\omega_{2}}
via:

(4.2.42)

\dot{s}{f}^{ij}=(\upsigma+(-1)^{i}\delta_{i,j}){f}^{ij}\qquad(i,j\in J).

For
1\leq r<d
, we define

\displaystyle\dot{s}_{r}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=

\displaystyle{f}_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes\dot{s}\otimes{f}_{1}^{\otimes(d-r-1)}\in C_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes{f}_{\omega_{2}}C_{2}{f}_{\omega_{2}}\otimes C_{1}^{\otimes(d-r-1)}\subseteq{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}},

\displaystyle\upsigma_{r}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=

\displaystyle{f}_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes\upsigma\otimes{f}_{1}^{\otimes(d-r-1)}\in C_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes{f}_{\omega_{2}}C_{2}{f}_{\omega_{2}}\otimes C_{1}^{\otimes(d-r-1)}\subseteq{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}.

Recalling (
4.1.14
) and (
4.2.33
), we observe that

(4.2.43)

\upsigma_{r}=\psi_{\rho_{d}(s_{r})}{f}_{\omega_{d}}=b_{s_{r}}{f}_{\omega_{d}}.

For
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
with an arbitrarily chosen reduced decomposition
w=s_{t_{1}}\cdots s_{t_{l}}
, we define

(4.2.44)

\dot{w}=\dot{s}_{t_{1}}\cdots\dot{s}_{t_{l}}\quad\text{and}\quad\upsigma_{w}=\upsigma_{t_{1}}\cdots\upsigma_{t_{l}}.

We point out that in general
\upsigma_{w}
depends on the choice of a reduced decomposition of
w
, while
\dot{w}
does not, as Theorem
4.2.47
below shows. We note that the element
\dot{w}
was denoted
\uptau_{w}
in
[
33
]
.
In view of (
4.2.43
) and (
4.2.33
), we may assume that

(4.2.45)

\upsigma_{w}=\psi_{\rho_{d}(w)}{f}_{\omega_{d}}=b_{w}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\qquad(w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}).

Now
[
33
, Lemma 4.4.29]
gives:

Lemma 4.2.46
.

Let
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
. Then
\dot{w}=\upsigma_{w}+\sum_{u<w}c_{u}\upsigma_{u}
for some coefficients
c_{u}\in\Bbbk
.

Recalling now the notation (
3.2.9
), we have

Theorem 4.2.47
.

There exists an isomorphism of graded superalgebras

\displaystyle F_{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}H_{d}(A_{\ell})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}},

\displaystyle e^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle\mapsto{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle(\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}),

\displaystyle u_{r}

\displaystyle\mapsto\dot{u}_{r},\ z_{r}\mapsto\dot{z}_{r},\ a^{[j,j-1]}_{r}\mapsto\dot{a}^{[j,j-1]}_{r},\ a^{[j-1,j]}_{r}\mapsto\dot{a}^{[j-1,j]}_{r}

\displaystyle(1\leq r\leq d),

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle\mapsto\dot{w}

\displaystyle(w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}).

Proof.

In
[
33
, Theorems 4.5.8]
this is proved over any field. The result for
\Bbbk=\mathcal{O}
follows.
∎

From now on, we identify the graded
\Bbbk
-superalgebras
{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
and
H_{d}(A_{\ell})
via the isomorphism of Theorem
4.2.47
.

Corollary 4.2.48
.

As a left/right graded
C_{\omega_{d}}
-supermodule,
{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
is free with basis
\{\dot{w}\mid w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}
.

Proof.

In view of Theorem
3.2.18
, as a left/right
graded
A_{\ell}[z]^{\otimes d}
-supermodule,
H_{d}(A_{\ell})
is free with basis given by the elements
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
. Now the claim follows from Theorem
4.2.47
.
∎

4.2h.
The graded supermodules
L_{j}
and
\hat{L}_{j}

For
j\in J
, we recall from §
3.2b
the graded supermodule
L_{j}=\Bbbk\cdot v_{j}
over
H_{1}(A_{\ell})
. But we have identified
H_{1}(A_{\ell})={f}_{1}C_{1}{f}_{1}=C_{1}
. So we will also consider
L_{j}
as graded
C_{1}
-supermodule. Note that

(4.2.49)

{f}^{i}v_{j}=\delta_{i,j}\hat{v}_{j}\qquad(i,j\in J).

Define the graded
\hat{C}_{1}
-supermodules

(4.2.50)

\hat{L}_{j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathpzc G}_{1}(L_{j})=\hat{C}_{1}{f}_{1}\otimes_{C_{1}}L_{j}\qquad(j\in J).

Also set

(4.2.51)

\hat{v}_{j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}_{1}\otimes v_{j}\in\hat{L}_{j}.

Note that

(4.2.52)

{f}^{i}\hat{v}_{j}=\delta_{i,j}\hat{v}_{j}\qquad(i,j\in J).

Recalling (
3.2.7
), we have

(4.2.53)

\operatorname{bideg}(v_{j})=\operatorname{bideg}(\hat{v}_{j})=(1+2j-2\ell,\,j\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,).

Using the Morita equivalence of Corollary
4.2.20
, if
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
, then
\hat{L}_{j}
is irreducible. Moreover, suppose the ground field
\mathbb{F}
is an
\mathcal{O}
-module.
Writing
\hat{L}_{j,\Bbbk}
when we want to specify the ground ring
\Bbbk
, and applying (
2.2.19
), we get

(4.2.54)

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\hat{L}_{j,\mathcal{O}}\simeq\hat{L}_{j,\mathbb{F}}.

Remark 4.2.55
.

It is easy to see that as a
\Bbbk
-module,
\hat{L}_{j}
is free of finite rank. The claim follows from (
4.2.54
) if we can show that
\dim\hat{L}_{j,\mathbb{F}}
does not depend on the characteristic of
\mathbb{F}
. This can be seen using for example the argument of
[
31
, Remark 5.5]
.

Chapter 5
Regrading

Let us identify the idempotent truncation
{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
with the affine zigzag algebra
H_{d}(A_{\ell})
as graded superalgebras via the isomorphism
F_{d}
of Theorem
5.1.16
.
Recall from Proposition
3.2.28
that
H_{d}(A_{\ell})
has an important regrading
H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})
, so we can also regrade
{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
accordingly. In this chapter we will upgrade this regrading to a regrading
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
of the whole algebra
C_{d}
. This regrading is important in particular because
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
is non-negatively graded, see Theorem
5.2.23
below. We will also describe a basis

\{\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\}
the degree zero component
(\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}})^{0}
, see Lemma
5.2.34
. The special elements
\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
and their counterparts
g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\in C_{d}
introduced in §
5.2b
below, will play an important role later on.

5.1.
Regrading the truncated cuspidal algebra and modules over it.

5.1a.
Regrading
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
of
C_{d}
.

Let
(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(m,d)
.
Recalling (
2.1.1
), we set

(5.1.1)

t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=dp+\sum_{s=1}^{m}\mu_{s}^{2}(2j_{s}-4\ell)\quad\text{and}\quad{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\sum_{s=1}^{m}\mu_{s}j_{s}\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,.

As usual, in some special cases we use a simplified notation:
if
\mu=(d)
and
\text{\boldmath$j$}=j
, we write
t_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=t_{(d),j}
and
{\varepsilon}_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\varepsilon}_{(d),j}
;
if
j_{s}=j
for all
s=1,\dots,m
, we write
t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=t_{\mu,j}
and
{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\varepsilon}_{\mu,j}
;
if
\mu=\omega_{d}
, we write
t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=t_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}
and
{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\varepsilon}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}
which agrees with (
3.2.20
).

Recalling the general setting of (
2.2.24
), the parameters (
5.1.1
) will be taken as grading supershift parameters which correspond to the orthogonal decomposition (
4.2.12
) in
C_{d}
. This yields the new graded superalgebra

(5.1.2)

{\mathsf{C}}_{d}=\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}),(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\Uppi^{{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}-{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}.

We denote by
{\mathsf{c}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
the element corresponding to an element
c\in C_{d}
; for example, we have the idempotents
\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
and
\mathsf{f}_{\nu}
in
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
corresponding to the idempotents
{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
and
{f}_{\nu}
in
C_{d}
, respectively.

For
j\in J
, recalling (
2.1.6
), we define

m_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=[d]^{!}_{\ell}[2d]^{!}_{0}\bigg(\prod_{i=j+1}^{\ell-1}[2d]^{!}_{i}\bigg)\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{j}([d]^{!}_{i})^{2}\bigg)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\pi}[q,q^{-1}],

and then for
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
, we define

m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=m_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\cdots m_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\pi}[q,q^{-1}].

Lemma 5.1.3
.

For any
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
, we have
\overline{m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}=\pi^{d}m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
.

Proof.

Using (
2.1.7
) and (
2.1.8
), we get

\overline{m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}=\overline{m_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}}\cdots\overline{m_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}}=(\pi^{\lambda_{1}}m_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}})\cdots(\pi^{\lambda_{n}}m_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})=\pi^{d}m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}},

as required.
∎

Recall the notation (
3.3.42
).

Lemma 5.1.4
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
.
Then

\text{\boldmath$l$}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}!=m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
and
\langle\text{\boldmath$l$}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\rangle=-t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
n=1
, i.e.
\text{\boldmath$l$}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}=\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}
. That
\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}!=m_{d,j}
follows immediately from the definitions. Moreover, taking into account that
(\alpha_{\ell}|\alpha_{\ell})=8
,
(\alpha_{0}|\alpha_{0})=2
, and
(\alpha_{i}|\alpha_{i})=4
for all other
i
, we get

\displaystyle\langle\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}\rangle

\displaystyle=8\frac{d(d-1)}{4}+\sum_{i=j+1}^{\ell-1}4\frac{2d(2d-1)}{4}+2\sum_{i=1}^{j}4\frac{d(d-1)}{4}+2\frac{2d(2d-1)}{4}

\displaystyle=-dp-d^{2}(2j-4\ell),

which is
-t_{d,j}
as required.
∎

Recall the notation (
2.2.5
).

Lemma 5.1.5
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(d)
, and
V
(resp.
W
) be a left (resp. right) graded
\hat{C}_{d}
-supermodule.
Then

\hat{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}V\simeq({f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}V)^{\oplus q^{-t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\quad\text{and}\quad W\hat{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq(W{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{\oplus q^{t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\overline{m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}}.

Proof.

Let

\text{\boldmath$k$}=\text{\boldmath$l$}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
. Then by definition we have
{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}=1_{\text{\boldmath$k$}}
and
\hat{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}=1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$k$}}}
. Now, by Lemmas
3.3.43
and
5.1.4
, we have

\hat{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}V=1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$k$}}}V\simeq(1_{\text{\boldmath$k$}}V)^{\oplus q^{\langle\text{\boldmath$k$}\rangle}\text{\boldmath$k$}!}=({f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}V)^{\oplus q^{-t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}.

The second isomorphism is proved similarly.
∎

Lemma 5.1.6
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}),(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(d)
. We have isomorphisms of graded superspaces:

(i)

\hat{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\hat{C}_{d}\hat{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq(\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{\oplus\pi^{d+{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}-{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}m_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}};

(ii)

\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}
.

Proof.

(i) By Lemmas
5.1.5
,
5.1.3
and (
2.2.6
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\hat{C}_{d}\hat{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq({f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{\oplus q^{t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\overline{m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}m_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\simeq(\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{\oplus\pi^{d+{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}-{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}m_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}m_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}.

(ii) Note applying
\tau
from (
3.3.15
) that
\hat{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\hat{C}_{d}\hat{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq\hat{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\hat{C}_{d}\hat{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}
as superspaces. So (ii)
follows form (i) and the uniqueness theorem for finitely generated modules over a PID applied to each graded component.
∎

5.1b.
Functors for regraded algebras

As in (
2.2.25
), we have mutually quasi-inverse equivalence functors

(5.1.7)

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}C_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod},\ V\mapsto\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{-t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\Uppi^{-{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}V,

(5.1.8)

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{g}}_{d}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to C_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod},\ {\mathsf{V}}\mapsto\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\Uppi^{{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{V}}

Since
\sum_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
is the identity in
C_{d}
, we can identify
V
and
{\mathsf{V}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathpzc{h}}_{d}V
as
\Bbbk
-modules; in other words
{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}V
is
V
with a different grading. So for
v\in V
we can speak of
the element of
{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}V
corresponding to
v
; we usually denote this element
{\mathsf{v}}
. Then, recalling the notation of §
5.1a
, we have for any
c\in C_{d}
and
v\in V
:

(5.1.9)

{\mathsf{c}}{\mathsf{v}}
is the element of
{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}V
corresponding to
cv
.

Let
\nu\in\Uplambda(n,d)
and recall from
(
4.2.13
), (
4.2.14
)
the parabolic subalgebra

C_{\nu_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes C_{\nu_{n}}=C_{\nu}={f}_{\nu}\hat{C}_{\nu}{f}_{\nu}\subseteq{f}_{\nu}C_{d}{f}_{\nu}.

We denote

\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(\nu)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)\mid\text{$\lambda$ is a refinement of $\nu$}\}.

Then

{f}_{\nu}=\sum_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(\nu)}{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}},

and we have a parabolic subalgebra

(5.1.10)

{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}),(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(\nu)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\Uppi^{{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}-{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{\nu}{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\subseteq\mathsf{f}_{\nu}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\nu}

which can again be identified with
{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu_{n}}
via the same isomorphism as for
C_{\nu}
(one needs to make the obvious observation that the grading shifts match):

(5.1.11)

{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}={\mathsf{C}}_{\nu_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu_{n}}.

Again, we have mutually inverse equivalence functors

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{h}}_{\nu}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}C_{\nu}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}\!\operatorname{-mod},\ V\mapsto\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(\nu)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{-t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\Uppi^{-{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}V,

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{g}}_{\nu}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to C_{\nu}\!\operatorname{-mod},\ {\mathsf{V}}\mapsto\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(\nu)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\Uppi^{{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{V}}

5.1c.
Regraded Gelfand-Graev induction and restriction

We now also have the
shifted Gelfand-Graev induction and restriction
functors

(5.1.12)

\begin{split}{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\nu}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\nu}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}}-\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod},\\
{\mathsf{GGR}}_{\nu}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\mathsf{f}_{\nu}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{d}}-\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}\!\operatorname{-mod}.\end{split}

Lemma 5.1.13
.

Let
\nu\in\Uplambda(d)
. For
{\mathsf{V}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}\!\operatorname{-mod}
and
{\mathsf{W}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we have functorial isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\nu}^{d}\,{\mathsf{V}}\simeq{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}\,{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\nu}^{d}\,{\mathpzc{g}}_{\nu}\,{\mathsf{V}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathsf{GGR}}_{\nu}^{d}\,{\mathsf{V}}\simeq{\mathpzc{h}}_{\nu}\,{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\nu}^{d}\,{\mathpzc{g}}_{d}\,{\mathsf{W}}.

Proof.

For the first isomorphism we have

\displaystyle\,{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}\,{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\nu}^{d}\,{\mathpzc{g}}_{\nu}\,{\mathsf{V}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\Uppi^{-{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}_{\nu}\otimes_{C_{\nu}}\Big(\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(\nu)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\Uppi^{{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{V}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(\nu)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\Uppi^{{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}-{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}_{\nu}{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}}\,{\mathsf{V}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}\bigoplus_{(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\Uppi^{{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}-{\varepsilon}_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{f}_{\nu}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}}\,{\mathsf{V}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\nu}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}}\,{\mathsf{V}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\nu}^{d}\,{\mathsf{V}}.

The second isomorphism is proved similarly.
∎

Corollary 5.1.14
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
and

\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}\in I^{\lambda_{1}\delta}_{\operatorname{div}},\dots,\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(n)}\in I^{\lambda_{n}\delta}_{\operatorname{div}}
be Gelfand-Graev words. Then

{\mathsf{C}}_{d}1_{\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}\cdots\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(n)}}\simeq{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\big({\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}1_{\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}}\boxtimes\,\cdots\,\boxtimes\,{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{n}}1_{\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(n)}}\big)

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
4.2.30

using Lemma
5.1.13
.
∎

Let
{\mathsf{V}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu}\!\operatorname{-mod}
. Recall (
4.2.33
). Note that
for any
x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}
, we have

\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}{\mathsf{b}}_{x}\mathsf{f}_{\mu}\otimes{\mathsf{V}}\subseteq{\mathsf{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}{\mathsf{V}}.

Let
\leq
be a total order refining the Bruhat order on
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.
For
x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}
, we consider the submodules

\displaystyle\Phi_{\leq x}({\mathsf{V}})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}{\mathsf{b}}_{y}\mathsf{f}_{\mu}\otimes{\mathsf{V}}\subseteq{\mathsf{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}{\mathsf{V}},

\displaystyle\Phi_{<x}({\mathsf{V}})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\,\text{with}\,y<x}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}{\mathsf{b}}_{y}\mathsf{f}_{\mu}\otimes{\mathsf{V}}\subseteq{\mathsf{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}V.

Theorem 5.1.15
.

(Shifted Gelfand-Graev Mackey Theorem)

Let
\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(d)
, and
{\mathsf{V}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu}\!\operatorname{-mod}
.
Then we have a filtration

(\Phi_{\leq x}({\mathsf{V}}))_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}}

of

{\mathsf{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}\,{\mathsf{V}}

with sub-quotients

\Phi_{\leq x}({\mathsf{V}})/\Phi_{<x}({\mathsf{V}})\simeq{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}^{\lambda}{}^{x}({\mathsf{GGR}}^{\mu}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}{\mathsf{V}}).

Proof.

By Lemma
5.1.13
, we have

{\mathsf{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}\,{\mathsf{V}}\simeq{\mathpzc{h}}_{\lambda}\,{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}\,{\mathpzc{g}}_{\mu}{\mathsf{V}}
. The result follows by applying Theorem
4.2.34
to
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}(\,{\mathpzc{g}}_{\mu}{\mathsf{V}})
and Lemma
5.1.13
again.
∎

5.1d.
The idempotent truncation
\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}

We recall from (
4.2.40
) and (
4.2.44
) the explicit elements
\dot{u}_{r},\dot{z}_{r},\dot{a}^{[j,j-1]}_{r},\dot{a}^{[j-1,j]}_{r},\dot{w}\in{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}

According to our general convention the corresponding elements of the regraded algebra
\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}
are denoted
\dot{\mathsf{u}}_{r},\dot{\mathsf{z}}_{r},\dot{\mathsf{a}}^{[j,j-1]}_{r},\dot{\mathsf{a}}^{[j-1,j]}_{r},\dot{\mathsf{w}}
.
By Theorem
4.2.47
, there is an explicit isomorphism
F_{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}H_{d}(A_{\ell})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
. Combining with Proposition
3.2.28
, we obtain:

Theorem 5.1.16
.

There is an isomorphism of graded superalgebras

\displaystyle{\mathsf{F}}_{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{e}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{u}}_{r}

\displaystyle\mapsto\dot{\mathsf{u}}_{r}\sum_{\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}}(-1)^{d+j_{1}+\dots+j_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{z}}_{r}

\displaystyle\mapsto\dot{\mathsf{z}}_{r}\sum_{\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}}(-1)^{j_{r}+1}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{a}}^{[i,j]}_{r}

\displaystyle\mapsto\dot{\mathsf{a}}^{[i,j]}_{r}\sum_{\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}}(-1)^{j_{1}+\dots+j_{r-1}+r-1}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}},

\displaystyle s_{r}

\displaystyle\mapsto\dot{\mathsf{s}}_{r}\sum_{\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}}(-1)^{(j_{r}+1)(j_{r+1}+1)}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}.

From Corollary
4.2.48
, we get:

Corollary 5.1.17
.

As a left/right
{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}
-module,
\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}
is free with basis
\{\dot{\mathsf{w}}\mid w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}
.

Note that under the isomorphism of Theorem
5.1.16
,
the subalgebra
{\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[{\mathsf{z}}]^{\otimes d}\subseteq H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})

corresponds to the parabolic subalgebra

{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\subseteq\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}.

In particular,
{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}
is generated by the

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}},\dot{\mathsf{u}}_{r},\dot{\mathsf{z}}_{r},\dot{\mathsf{a}}^{[i,j]}_{r}
.

Lemma 5.1.18
.

The algebra
\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}
is non-negatively graded with the degree zero component
(\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}})^{0}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}
generated by all
\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}
and
\dot{\mathsf{w}}
. Moreover,

(\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}})^{>0}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{>0}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}.

Proof.

The equalities
(\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}})^{0}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}
and
(\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}})^{>0}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{>0}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}
are obvious, and
the first claim follows from Theorem
5.1.16
and Corollary
3.2.19
(i). The equality
\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}
follows from
{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}
.

Recalling the isomorphism
{\mathsf{F}}_{d}
of Theorem
5.1.16
, we have
(\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}})^{>0}={\mathsf{F}}_{d}(H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})^{>0})
. By Corollary
3.2.19
(ii), we have
H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})^{>0}=H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[z]^{\otimes d})^{>0}
, and
{\mathsf{F}}_{d}(H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[z]^{\otimes d})^{>0})=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}
.
So
(\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}})^{>0}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}
.
∎

Lemma 5.1.19
.

In
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
, for any
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, we have
{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}\dot{\mathsf{w}}=\dot{\mathsf{w}}\,{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}
.

Proof.

We have
{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}={\mathsf{F}}_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[{\mathsf{z}}]^{\otimes d})
.
It is easy to see using the relations in
H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})
and the fact that
s_{r}
has degree
0
that
({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[{\mathsf{z}}]^{\otimes d})^{>0}s_{r}=s_{r}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[{\mathsf{z}}]^{\otimes d})^{>0}
for all
1\leq r<d
. So

({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[{\mathsf{z}}]^{\otimes d})^{>0}w=w({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[{\mathsf{z}}]^{\otimes d})^{>0}
for all
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.
The result follows on application of
{\mathsf{F}}_{d}
.
∎

Lemma 5.1.20
.

For any
\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}
we have
\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}=\Bbbk\cdot\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}
.

Proof.

We have
\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}={\mathsf{F}}_{d}({\mathsf{e}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[{\mathsf{z}}]^{\otimes d})^{0}{\mathsf{e}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})={\mathsf{F}}_{d}(\Bbbk\cdot{\mathsf{e}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})=\Bbbk\cdot\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}
.
∎

5.2.
Non-negativity of grading and the elements
g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}

5.2a.
Special segments

Let
j\in J
and
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Define the composition

(5.2.1)

\nu^{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=(d,(2d)^{\ell-1-j},d^{j},2d,d^{j})\in\Uplambda(dp).

Corresponding to the composition
\nu^{d,j}
we have the decomposition of the segment
[1,dp]
as an increasing disjoint union of the segments (this is a special notation replacing the segments
{\tt P}^{\nu^{d,j}}_{r}
from (
2.1.12
)):

(5.2.2)

[1,dp]={\tt X}_{\ell}^{d,j}\sqcup{\tt X}_{\ell-1}^{d,j}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt X}_{j+1}^{d,j}\sqcup{\tt Y}^{d,j}_{j}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt Y}^{d,j}_{1}\sqcup{\tt X}^{d,j}_{0}\sqcup{\tt Z}^{d,j}_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt Z}^{d,j}_{j},

such that

\displaystyle{\tt X}^{d,j}_{i}

\displaystyle=\{r\in[1,dp]\mid\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}_{r}=i\}\qquad(\text{for}\ j<i\leq\ell\ \text{or}\ i=0),

\displaystyle{\tt Y}^{d,j}_{i}\sqcup{\tt Z}^{d,j}_{i}

\displaystyle=\{r\in[1,dp]\mid\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}_{r}=i\}\qquad(\text{for}\ 1\leq i\leq j).

For
1\leq i\leq j
, define

{\tt X}^{d,j}_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\tt Y}^{d,j}_{i}\sqcup{\tt Z}^{d,j}_{i}

so that now
for all

i\in I
we have

(5.2.3)

{\tt X}^{d,j}_{i}=\{r\in[1,dp]\mid\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}_{r}=i\}.

More generally, let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. Recalling (
2.1.12
), (
2.1.11
),
we have the decomposition of the segment
[1,dp]
as an increasing disjoint union of the segments

[1,dp]={\tt P}^{p\lambda}_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt P}^{p\lambda}_{n}.

For each
1\leq r\leq n
, we have the decomposition of the segment
{\tt P}^{p\lambda}_{r}
as an increasing disjoint union of the segments:

(5.2.4)

{\tt P}_{r}^{p\lambda}={\tt X}_{r,\ell}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\sqcup{\tt X}_{r,\ell-1}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt X}_{r,i_{r}+1}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\sqcup{\tt Y}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,i_{r}}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt Y}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{1,i_{r}}\sqcup{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,0}\sqcup{\tt Z}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt Z}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,i_{r}},

where

\displaystyle{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,i}

\displaystyle=\{r\in{\tt P}^{p\lambda}_{r}\mid\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r}=i\}\qquad(\text{for}\ i_{r}<i\leq\ell\ \text{or}\ i=0),

\displaystyle{\tt Y}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,i}\sqcup{\tt Z}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,i}

\displaystyle=\{r\in{\tt P}^{p\lambda}_{r}\mid\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r}=i\}\qquad(\text{for}\ 1\leq i\leq i_{r}).

For
1\leq i\leq i_{r}
we define

{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\tt Y}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,i}\sqcup{\tt Z}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,i}

so that now

{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r,i}=\{r\in{\tt P}_{r}^{p\lambda}\mid\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{r}=i\}

for all
i\in I
.

Example 5.2.5
.

(i) Let
\ell=3
,
j=1
, and
d=2
. Then

\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}=3\,3\,2\,2\,2\,2\,1\,1\,0\,0\,0\,0\,1\,1

and the decomposition (
5.2.2
) is illustrated by the following picture:

\overbrace{3\,3}^{{\tt X}^{d,j}_{3}}\,\overbrace{2\,2\,2\,2}^{{\tt X}^{d,j}_{2}}\,\overbrace{1\,1}^{{\tt Y}^{d,j}_{1}}\,\overbrace{0\,0\,0\,0}^{{\tt X}^{d,j}_{0}}\,\overbrace{1\,1}^{{\tt Z}^{d,j}_{1}}.

In other words, the decomposition (
5.2.2
) is
[1,14]=[1,2]\sqcup[3,6]\sqcup[7,8]\sqcup[9,12]\sqcup[13,14]
.

(ii) Let
\ell=3
,
d=3
,
\lambda=(2,1)
and
\text{\boldmath$i$}=11
. Then

\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}=3\,\,3\,\,2\,\,2\,\,2\,\,2\,\,1\,\,1\,\,0\,\,0\,\,0\,\,0\,\,1\,\,1\,\,3\,\,2\,\,2\,\,1\,\,0\,\,0\,\,1

and the decomposition (
5.2.4
) is illustrated by the following picture:

\underbrace{\overbrace{3\,\,3}^{{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{1,3}}\,\,\overbrace{2\,\,2\,\,2\,\,2}^{{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{1,2}}\,\,\overbrace{1\,\,1}^{{\tt Y}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{1,1}}\,\,\overbrace{0\,\,0\,\,0\,\,0}^{{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{1,0}}\,\,\overbrace{1\,\,1}^{{\tt Z}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{1,1}}}_{{\tt P}^{p\lambda}_{1}}\,\,\underbrace{\overbrace{3}^{{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{2,3}}\,\,\overbrace{2\,\,2}^{{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{2,2}}\,\,\overbrace{1}^{{\tt Y}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{2,1}}\,\,\overbrace{0\,\,0}^{{\tt X}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{2,0}}\,\,\overbrace{1}^{{\tt Z}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}_{2,1}}}_{{\tt P}^{p\lambda}_{2}}.

5.2b.
The elements
g_{d,j}
and
h_{d,j}

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
j\in J
.
Note that the set

\{x\in{\mathscr{D}}^{p^{d}}_{dp}\mid x\cdot(\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{j})^{d}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}\}

has a unique longest element which we denote
\gamma_{d,j}
and the unique shortest element which we denote
\chi_{d,j}
.
We denote

(5.2.6)

g_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\psi_{\gamma_{d,j}}{f}^{j^{d}}\in\hat{C}_{d}\quad\text{and}\quad h_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}{f}^{j^{d}}\in\hat{C}_{d}.

For
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
, we denote

(5.2.7)

g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=g_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes g_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}\in\hat{C}_{\lambda_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\hat{C}_{\lambda_{n}}=\hat{C}_{\lambda}.

An easy computation shows that

(5.2.8)

\begin{split}\operatorname{bideg}(g_{d,j})&=(d(d-1)(2j-4\ell),{\bar{0}}),\\
\operatorname{bideg}(g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})&=\big(\sum_{r=1}^{n}\lambda_{r}(\lambda_{r}-1)(2i_{r}-4\ell)\,,\ {\bar{0}}\big).\end{split}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{234.23236pt}{49.20847pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

It is easy to see that

(5.2.10)

\gamma_{d,j}=\kappa_{d,j}\chi_{d,j}

for some
\kappa_{d,j}\in\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{d,j}}
such that

{\mathtt{l}}(\gamma_{d,j})={\mathtt{l}}(\kappa_{d,j})+{\mathtt{l}}(\chi_{d,j})
.
Letting
k_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\psi_{\kappa_{d,j}}
, we may assume that

(5.2.11)

g_{d,j}=k_{d,j}h_{d,j}.

Lemma 5.2.12
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. Then
g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}={f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}g_{d,j}{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots\,i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}\in C_{\lambda}
.
In particular,
g_{d,j}={f}^{d,j}g_{d,j}{f}^{j^{d}}\in C_{d}
.

Proof.

It suffices to establish the special case
g_{d,j}={f}^{d,j}g_{d,j}{f}^{j^{d}}\in C_{d}
.
As
{f}^{j^{d}}
is an idempotent, we have
g_{d,j}=g_{d,j}{f}^{j^{d}}
by definition. To prove that
g_{d,j}={f}^{d,j}g_{d,j}
, recall the notation (
5.2.1
). Observe, using the braid relations (
3.3.12
) with no error term, that we can write
g_{d,j}=\psi_{w_{0}}\psi_{x}{f}^{j^{d}}
, where
w_{0}
is the longest element in the parabolic subgroup
\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{d,j}}
. Now we can apply (
3.3.37
).
∎

Lemma 5.2.13
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. Then
g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\neq 0
. In particular, in
\hat{C}_{d}
, we have that
g_{d,j}\neq 0
and hence
h_{d,j}\neq 0
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
g_{d,j}\neq 0
.
Recalling the
\hat{C}_{1}
-module
\hat{L}_{j}
from (
4.2.50
),
the module
{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}\neq 0
is cuspidal by Lemma
4.1.9
(i), and it suffices to prove that
g_{d,j}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}\neq 0
. In fact we show that

g_{d,j}\otimes\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}=g_{d,j}1_{\delta^{d}}\otimes\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}\subseteq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

is non-zero.
By definition, we have
{f}^{j}L_{j}\neq 0
, so
{f}^{j^{d}}\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}\neq 0
. Now, since
\gamma_{d,j}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{pd}^{p^{d}}
, we have by Lemma
3.3.23
that
g_{d,j}\otimes(\hat{L}_{j})^{\boxtimes d}=\psi_{\gamma_{d,j}}1_{\delta^{d}}\otimes{f}^{j^{d}}(\hat{L}_{j})^{\boxtimes d}\neq 0
.
∎

5.2c.
The idempotent truncation
{f}^{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}

In this subsection we establish the equality
{f}^{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}=g_{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
.

Lemma 5.2.14
.

Let
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)},\dots,\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(d)}\in I^{\delta}_{\operatorname{cus}}
,
\text{\boldmath$i$}=\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}\cdots\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(d)}
, and
w\in{}^{\nu^{d,j}}{\mathscr{D}}^{p^{d}}_{dp}
satisy
w\cdot\text{\boldmath$i$}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}
. Then

\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}=\dots=\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(d)}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{j}
and
w=\chi_{d,j}
.

Proof.

Write
\text{\boldmath$i$}=i_{1}\cdots i_{dp}
.
We use the notation of §
5.2a
, in particular the sets
{\tt X}^{d,j}_{i},{\tt Y}^{d,j}_{i},{\tt Z}^{d,j}_{i}
.
We simplify the notation

{\tt P}_{r}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\tt P}^{p\omega_{d}}_{r}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=[p(r-1)+1,pr]
for
1\leq r\leq d
, and also
denote
{\tt X}_{i}^{(r)}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{t\in{\tt P}_{r}\mid i_{t}=i\}
for
i\in I
.
The assumption
w\in{}^{\nu^{d,j}}{\mathscr{D}}^{p^{d}}_{dp}
means that
w
is increasing on each
{\tt P}_{r}
, and
w^{-1}
is increasing on each
{\tt X}^{d,j}_{i}
for
j<i\leq\ell
or
i=0
and on each
{\tt Y}^{d,j}_{i}
,
{\tt Z}^{d,j}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq j
. The assumption that
w\cdot\text{\boldmath$i$}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}
means that
w
maps
\bigsqcup_{r=1}^{d}{\tt X}_{i}^{(r)}
onto
{\tt X}^{d,j}_{i}
for all
i\in I
. Thus it suffices to prove that
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}=\text{\boldmath$l$}^{j}
for all
r=1,\dots,d
, and then it automatically follows that
w=\chi_{d,j}
.

We now claim that for
r=1,\dots,d
the word
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}
begins with
\ell(\ell-1)^{2}\cdots(j+1)^{2}
(i.e. every
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}
agrees with
\text{\boldmath$l$}^{j}
in the first
2(\ell-j)-1
positions). We apply inverse induction on
i=\ell,\ell-1,\dots,j
to prove that
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}
begins with
\ell(\ell-1)^{2}\cdots(i+1)^{2}

for all
r
.
As each
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}
is cuspidal, we have that
i^{(r)}
begins with
\ell
for all
r
. This gives the induction base. The inductive step follows using the fact that each
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}
is cupsidal, contains only two letters equal to
i+1
, and the properties of
w
listed in the previous paragraph.

Now, we certainly have that
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}
begins with
\ell(\ell-1)^{2}\cdots(j+1)^{2}j
for all
r
since
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}\in I^{\delta}
are cuspidal. On the other hand we now cannot have
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}
beginning with
\ell(\ell-1)^{2}\cdots(j+1)^{2}j^{2}
for any
r
since
|{\tt Y}^{d,j}_{j}|=d
. So it follows that each
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}
begins with
\ell(\ell-1)^{2}\cdots(j+1)^{2}j(j-1)
. Continuing like this, we deduce that each
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}
begins with
\ell(\ell-1)^{2}\cdots(j+1)^{2}j(j-1)\cdots 1
and then even with
\ell(\ell-1)^{2}\cdots(j+1)^{2}j(j-1)\cdots 10^{2}
. Now it is easy to see that in fact

\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(r)}=\ell(\ell-1)^{2}\cdots(j+1)^{2}j(j-1)\cdots 10^{2}1\cdots j=\text{\boldmath$l$}^{j}

as required.
∎

Lemma 5.2.15
.

Let
\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}
,
j\in J
, and
u\in\mathfrak{S}_{dp}
satisfy
u\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}
. If
\psi_{u}\hat{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\neq 0
in
\hat{C}_{d}
then

u=x\chi_{d,j}y
for some
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{d,j}}
and
y\in\mathfrak{S}_{p^{d}}
such that
y\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{j^{d}}
.

Proof.

We can write
u=xwy
for
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{d,j}}
,
w\in{}^{\nu^{d,j}}{\mathscr{D}}^{p^{d}}_{dp}
and
y\in\mathfrak{S}_{p^{d}}
with
{\mathtt{l}}(u)={\mathtt{l}}(x)+{\mathtt{l}}(w)+{\mathtt{l}}(y)
. We may assume that
\psi_{u}=\psi_{x}\psi_{w}\psi_{y}
. Now
\psi_{u}\hat{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\neq 0
implies
\psi_{y}\hat{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\neq 0
, so
y\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}=\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}\cdots\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(d)}
for some

\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)},\dots,\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(d)}\in I^{\delta}_{\operatorname{cus}}
. Now, since
x\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}
, we can apply Lemma
5.2.14
to deduce
w=\chi_{d,j}
and
y\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}=\text{\boldmath$l$}^{j^{d}}
.
∎

Lemma 5.2.16
.

Let
Y\in\Bbbk[y_{1},\dots,y_{dp}]
. Then there exists
Y^{\prime}\in\Bbbk[y_{1},\dots,y_{dp}]
such that
Y\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}=\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}Y^{\prime}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}
in
\hat{C}_{d}
.

Proof.

We consecutively move the dots corresponding to the elements
y_{t}
appearing in
Y
past the crossings of the Khovanov-Lauda diagram for
\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}
using the relations (
3.3.9
). The error terms appearing on each step are of the form
Y^{\prime\prime}\psi_{u}Y^{\prime\prime\prime}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}
with
{\mathtt{l}}(u)<{\mathtt{l}}(\chi_{d,j})
and
u\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{j^{d}}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}
. By Lemma
5.2.15
, such error terms are zero.
∎

Proposition 5.2.17
.

We have
{f}^{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}=g_{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.2.12
we have
{f}^{d,j}g_{d,i}=g_{d,i}
so
{f}^{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\supseteq g_{d,i}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
.

For the opposite inclusion, let
0\neq c\in{f}^{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
.
As
{f}_{\omega_{d}}=\sum_{\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}}{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}
, by Theorem
3.3.13
, we may assume that
c={f}^{d,j}\psi_{u}Y{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}={f}^{d,j}\psi_{u}\hat{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}Y{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}
for some
\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}
, some
Y\in\Bbbk[y_{1},\dots,y_{dp}]
, and some
u\in\mathfrak{S}_{dp}
satisfying
u\cdot(\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}
.
By Lemma
5.2.15
, we may assume that

\psi_{u}=\psi_{x}\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}\psi_{y}
for some
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{d,j}}
and
y\in\mathfrak{S}_{p^{d}}
with
y\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{j^{d}}
.

Let
w_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=w_{\nu^{d,j}}
be the longest element of the parabolic subgroup
\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{d,j}}
. By Lemma
3.3.38
, we have

{f}^{d,j}\psi_{x}=\psi_{w_{d,j}}Y^{\prime}

for some
Y^{\prime}\in\Bbbk[y_{1},\dots,y_{dp}]
.
So

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle={f}^{d,j}\psi_{x}\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}\psi_{y}Y{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle={f}^{d,j}{f}^{d,j}\psi_{x}\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}\psi_{y}Y{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle={f}^{d,j}\psi_{w_{d,j}}Y^{\prime}\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}\psi_{y}Y{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle={f}^{d,j}\psi_{w_{d,j}}\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}Y^{\prime\prime}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}\psi_{y}Y{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

for some
Y^{\prime\prime}\in\Bbbk[y_{1},\dots,y_{dp}]
, where we have used Lemma
5.2.16
for the last equality.
Recalling the notation (
5.2.10
), we note

w_{d,j}=\kappa_{d,j}z

for some element
z\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
such that
{\mathtt{l}}(w_{d,j})={\mathtt{l}}(\kappa_{d,j})+{\mathtt{l}}(z)
. Moreover,
z\chi_{d,j}=\chi_{d,j}z^{\prime}
where
z^{\prime}=(w_{1,j},\dots,w_{1,j})\in\mathfrak{S}_{p}\times\dots\times\mathfrak{S}_{p}=\mathfrak{S}_{p^{d}}\leq\mathfrak{S}_{dp}
and
{\mathtt{l}}(\chi_{d,j}z^{\prime})={\mathtt{l}}(\chi_{d,j})+{\mathtt{l}}(z^{\prime})
. Note by Lemma
3.3.38
that

\psi_{z^{\prime}}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}\hat{C}_{d}={f}^{j^{d}}\hat{C}_{d}
. So, using (
5.2.11
) and Lemma
5.2.12
, we get

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle={f}^{d,j}\psi_{w_{d,j}}\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}Y^{\prime\prime}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}\psi_{y}Y{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle={f}^{d,j}\psi_{\kappa_{d,j}}\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}\psi_{z^{\prime}}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}Y^{\prime\prime}\hat{f}^{j^{d}}\psi_{y}Y{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle\subseteq{f}^{d,j}k_{d,j}\psi_{\chi_{d,j}}{f}^{j^{d}}\hat{C}_{d}{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle={f}^{d,j}k_{d,j}h_{d,j}C_{d}{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle={f}^{d,j}g_{d,j}C_{d}{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

\displaystyle=g_{d,j}C_{d}{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}},

which completes the proof since
C_{d}{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\subseteq C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
.
∎

Lemma 5.2.18
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. Then

{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}=g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}=g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}=g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}.

Proof.

Since
g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}={f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}={f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
it suffices to prove the inclusion
{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\subseteq g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
.
By Corollary
4.2.35
, we have

{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}={f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}.

Now, the required inclusion follows from

\displaystyle{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\omega_{d}}

\displaystyle={f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}C_{\lambda_{1}}{f}_{\omega_{\lambda_{1}}}\otimes\dots\otimes{f}^{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}C_{\lambda_{n}}{f}_{\omega_{\lambda_{n}}}

\displaystyle=g_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}C_{\lambda_{1}}{f}_{\omega_{\lambda_{1}}}\otimes\dots\otimes g_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}C_{\lambda_{n}}{f}_{\omega_{\lambda_{n}}}

\displaystyle=g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\omega_{d}},

where we have used Proposition
5.2.17
for the second equality.
∎

5.2d.
The algebra
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
is non-negatively graded

In this subsection we prove that
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
is non-negatively graded, i.e.
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{<0}=0
, see the notation (
2.2.1
).

Lemma 5.2.19
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
.
The words in
I^{d\delta}
beginning in the following patterns are not cuspidal:

\displaystyle\ell^{d}(\ell-1)^{2d}\cdots(i+1)^{2d}i^{d}\cdots(k+1)^{d}k^{d-1}(k-1)^{d}\cdots

\displaystyle(\text{for $1<k\leq i<\ell$}),

\displaystyle\ell^{d}(\ell-1)^{2d}\cdots(i+1)^{2d}i^{d}\cdots 2^{d}1^{d-1}0^{2d}\cdots

\displaystyle(\text{for $1\leq i<\ell$}),

\displaystyle\ell^{d}(\ell-1)^{2d}\cdots(j+1)^{2d}j^{d}(j-1)^{2d}\cdots

\displaystyle(\text{for $2\leq j<\ell$}),

\displaystyle\ell^{d}(\ell-1)^{2d}\cdots(j+1)^{2d}j^{a_{j}}\cdots 1^{a_{1}}0^{2d}\cdots\cdots

\displaystyle(\text{for $1\leq j<\ell$ and $a_{j}<d$}).

Proof.

Checked explicitly using the definition of cuspidality and (
2.1.34
).
∎

Lemma 5.2.20
.

Let
i,j\in J
. We have:

(i)

\mathsf{f}^{d,i}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i}=(\mathsf{f}^{d,i}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i})^{\geq 0}
and
(\mathsf{f}^{d,i}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i})^{0}
is spanned by
\mathsf{f}^{d,i}
.

(ii)

If
|i-j|=1
then
\mathsf{f}^{d,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i}=(\mathsf{f}^{d,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i})^{>0}
.

(iii)

If
|i-j|>1
then
\hat{f}^{d,j}C_{d}{f}^{d,i}=0
; in particular,

\mathsf{f}^{d,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i}=0
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we will use the notation (
5.2.2
), (
5.2.3
), (
2.1.17
).

By Lemma
5.1.6
(ii), we may assume that
i\leq j
.
Recall that
{f}^{d,i}=1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}
and
{f}^{d,j}=1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}}
.
As
t_{d,i}-t_{d,j}=2d^{2}(i-j)
, it suffices to prove the following claims:

(a)
(1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}C_{d}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}})^{<0}=0
and
(1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}C_{d}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}})^{0}
is spanned by
1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}
,

(c)
(1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i+1}}C_{d}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}})^{\leq 2d^{2}}=0
,

(d)
1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}}C_{d}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}=0
if
j-i>1
.

By Theorem
3.3.13
, we have that

1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}}C_{d}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}
is spanned by the elements of the form
1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}}F(y_{1},\dots,y_{dp})\psi_{w}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}
, where
F(y_{1},\dots,y_{dp})
is a polynomial and
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{dp}
satisfies
w\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,i}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}
, which is equivalent to
w({\tt X}^{d,i}_{k})={\tt X}^{d,j}_{k}
for all
k\in I
.
Moreover, since
\psi_{x}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}=0
for any
x
in the parabolic subgroup
\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{d,i}}
, we may assume that
w\in{\mathscr{D}}^{\nu^{d,i}}_{dp}
, i.e.
w
is increasing on all
{\tt X}^{(i)}_{k}
with
i<k\leq\ell
or
k=0
and on all
{\tt Y}^{(i)}_{k},{\tt Z}^{(i)}_{k}
with
1\leq k\leq i
. Since
i\leq j
, we have
that
w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt X}^{(i)}_{k}\nearrow{\tt X}^{(j)}_{k}
for
j<k\leq\ell
and
k=0
, and
w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt X}^{(i)}_{k}\nearrow{\tt Y}^{(j)}_{k}\sqcup{\tt Z}_{k}^{(j)}
for
i<k\leq j
.
Since all
y_{r}
’s have positive degrees it now suffices to prove the following claims:

(a
′
)
1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}\psi_{w}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}\in C_{d}
is non-zero only if
w=1
,

(b
′
) a non-zero
1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i+1}}\psi_{w}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}\in C_{d}
has degree
>2d^{2}

(c
′
)
1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}}\psi_{w}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}=0
in
C_{d}
for
|i-j|>1
.

To prove (a
′
), suppose
1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}\psi_{w}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}\in C_{d}
is non-zero. To prove that
w=1
, it suffices to prove that
w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Y}_{k}^{(i)}\nearrow{\tt Y}_{k}^{(j)}
and
w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Z}_{k}^{(i)}\nearrow{\tt Z}_{k}^{(j)}
for all
k=1,\dots,i
. We apply induction on
k
. Suppose
k\geq 1
and we already know that
w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Y}_{l}^{(i)}\nearrow{\tt Y}_{l}^{(j)}
and
w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Z}_{l}^{(i)}\nearrow{\tt Z}_{l}^{(j)}
for all
1\leq l<k
. Let
t=\max({\tt Y}_{k}^{(i)})
. To prove that
w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Y}_{k}^{(i)}\nearrow{\tt Y}_{k}^{(j)}
and
w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Z}_{k}^{(i)}\nearrow{\tt Z}_{k}^{(j)}
, it suffices to show that
w(t)\in{\tt Y}_{k}^{(j)}
. If
w(t)\in{\tt Z}_{k}^{(j)}
, we can write
w=yx
with
{\mathtt{l}}(w)={\mathtt{l}}(y)+{\mathtt{l}}(x)
,
x(r)=r
for all
r\in{\tt Y}_{k}^{(i)}\setminus\{t\}
and
x(r)=r-1
for all
r\in{\tt Y}_{k-1}^{(i)}
(if
k=1
this should be interpreted as
x(r)=r-1
for all
r\in{\tt X}^{(i)}_{0}
). So we may assume that
\psi_{w}=\psi_{y}\psi_{x}
, and then
\psi_{x}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}=0
since

x\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\ell^{d}(\ell-1)^{2d}\cdots(i+1)^{2d}i^{d}\cdots(k+1)^{d}k^{d-1}(k-1)^{d}&\hbox{if $k>1$,}\\
\ell^{d}(\ell-1)^{2d}\cdots(i+1)^{2d}i^{d}\cdots 2^{d}1^{d-1}0^{2d}&\hbox{if $k=1$,}\end{array}\right.

which is not cuspidal by Lemma
5.2.19
.

To prove (b
′
), as in the case
j=i
we prove by induction on
k
that
1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i+1}}\psi_{w}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}
is non-zero only if

w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Y}_{k}^{(i)}\nearrow{\tt Y}_{k}^{(i+1)}
and
w\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Z}_{k}^{(i)}\nearrow{\tt Z}_{k}^{(i+1)}
for all
k=1,\dots,i
. Therefore

\deg(1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i+1}}\psi_{w}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}})=4d^{2}>2d^{2}
as required.

To prove (c
′
), let
j>i+1
. We can write
w=yx
with
{\mathtt{l}}(w)={\mathtt{l}}(y)+{\mathtt{l}}(x)
and

x\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,i}=\ell^{d}(\ell-1)^{2d}\cdots(j+1)^{2d}j^{d}(j-1)^{2d}\cdots

which is not cuspidal by Lemma
5.2.19
. So
1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}}C_{d}1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,i}}=0
.
∎

Recall the notation of §
5.2a
.
For
\mu\in\Uplambda_{0}(n,d)
and
i\in I
, we define the permutation

q_{\mu,i}\in\mathfrak{S}_{dp}
by the following conditions:

(1)
q_{\mu,i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt X}^{d,i}_{k}\nearrow\bigsqcup_{s=1}^{n}{\tt X}_{s,k}^{\mu,i}
for
i<k\leq\ell
or
k=0
;

(2)
q_{\mu,i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Y}^{d,i}_{k}\nearrow\bigsqcup_{s=1}^{n}{\tt Y}_{s,k}^{\mu,i}

and
q_{\mu,i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\tt Z}^{d,i}_{k}\nearrow\bigsqcup_{s=1}^{n}{\tt Z}_{s,k}^{\mu,i}

for
1\leq k\leq i
;

Note that
q_{\mu,i}\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,i}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\mu,i}
and
w_{(d),i}=1
. Define
a_{\mu,i}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}^{\mu,i}\psi_{q_{\mu,i}}{f}^{d,i}\in{f}^{\mu,i}C_{d}{f}^{d,i}
. We have the corresponding element
{\mathsf{a}}_{\mu,i}\in\mathsf{f}^{\mu,i}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i}
.

Lemma 5.2.21
.

Let
i\in J
and
(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. Then we have

\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i}=(\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i})^{\geq 0}
. Moreover:

(i)

(\mathsf{f}^{\mu,i}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i})^{0}
is spanned by
{\mathsf{a}}_{\mu,i}
;

(ii)

\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i}=(\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i})^{>0}
unless
j_{s}=i
for all
s=1,\dots,n
;

(iii)

\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,i}=0
unless
|j_{s}-i|\leq 1
for all
s=1,\dots,n
.

Proof.

We apply induction on
n
, the base case
n=1
being Lemma
5.2.20
. Let
n>1
. It suffices to prove the following claims:

(a)
{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}^{d,i}=({f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}^{d,i})^{\geq t_{d,i}-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}
;

(b)
{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}^{d,i}=({f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}^{d,i})^{>t_{d,i}-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}}
unless
j_{s}=i
for all
s=1,\dots,n
, in which case we have that
({f}^{\mu,i}C_{d}{f}^{d,i})^{t_{\mu,i}-t_{d,i}}
is spanned by
a_{\mu,i}
;

(c)
\hat{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}^{d,i}=0
if
|i-j_{s}|>1
for some
s\in\{1,\dots,n\}
.

We have increasing disjoint unions of the segments

\displaystyle[1,dp]={\tt X}_{\ell}^{d,i}\sqcup{\tt X}_{\ell-1}^{d,i}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt X}_{i+1}^{d,i}\sqcup{\tt Y}^{d,i}_{i}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt Y}^{d,i}_{1}\sqcup{\tt X}^{d,i}_{0}\sqcup{\tt Z}^{d,i}_{1}\sqcup\dots{\tt Z}^{d,i}_{i},

\displaystyle[1,p\mu_{1}]={\tt X}_{1,\ell}^{\mu,j_{1}}\sqcup{\tt X}_{1,\ell-1}^{\mu,j_{1}}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt X}_{1,{j_{1}}+1}^{\mu,j_{1}}\sqcup{\tt Y}^{\mu,j_{1}}_{1,j_{1}}\sqcup\dots\sqcup{\tt Y}^{\mu,j_{1}}_{1,1}\sqcup{\tt X}^{\mu,j_{1}}_{1,0}\sqcup{\tt Z}^{\mu,j_{1}}_{1,1}\sqcup\dots{\tt Z}^{\mu,j_{1}}_{1,j_{1}}.

By Theorem
3.3.13
, we have that

\hat{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}C_{d}{f}^{d,i}
is spanned by the elements of the form
\hat{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}F(y_{1},\dots,y_{dp})\psi_{w}{f}^{d,i}
, where
F(y_{1},\dots,y_{dp})
is a polynomial and
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{dp}
satisfies
w\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,i}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}
.
Moreover, since
\psi_{x}{f}^{d,i}=0
for any
x
in the parabolic subgroup
\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{d,i}}
, we may assume that
w\in{\mathscr{D}}^{\nu^{d,i}}_{dp}
, i.e.
w
is increasing on all
{\tt X}^{d,i}_{k}
with
i<k\leq\ell
or
k=0
and on all
{\tt Y}^{d,i}_{k},{\tt Z}^{d,i}_{k}
with
1\leq k\leq i
.
Since all
y_{r}
’s have positive degrees it suffices to prove that for
a non-zero
\hat{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\psi_{w}{f}^{d,i}\in C_{d}
we have:

(1)
\deg({f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\psi_{w}{f}^{d,i})\geq t_{d,i}-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}
,

(2)
\deg({f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\psi_{w}{f}^{d,i})>t_{d,i}-t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}
, unless
w=q_{\mu,i}
and
j_{s}=i
for all
s=1,\dots,n
,

(3)
|j_{s}-i|\leq 1
for all
s=1,\dots,n
.

For segments
{\tt X}
and
{\tt Y}
we will use the notation
{\tt X}<_{l}{\tt Y}
if we can write
{\tt Y}
as an increasing disjoint union of the segments
{\tt Y}={\tt X}\sqcup{\tt Z}
.

Since
w
is increasing on all
{\tt X}^{d,i}_{k}
with
i<k\leq\ell
or
k=0
, we have for such
k
that
\tilde{\tt X}_{k}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=w^{-1}({\tt X}_{1,k}^{\mu,j_{1}})<_{l}{\tt X}_{k}^{d,i}
. Since
w
is increasing on
{\tt Y}^{d,i}_{k},{\tt Z}^{d,i}_{k}
with
1\leq k\leq i
, we have for such
k
that
\tilde{\tt X}_{k}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=w^{-1}({\tt X}_{1,k}^{\mu,j_{1}})=\tilde{\tt Y}_{k}\sqcup\tilde{\tt Z}_{k}
with
\tilde{\tt Y}_{k}<_{l}{\tt Y}_{k}^{d,i}
and
\tilde{\tt Z}_{k}<_{l}{\tt Z}_{k}^{d,i}
.
Set

\tilde{\tt X}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\tilde{\tt X}_{\ell}\sqcup\tilde{\tt X}_{\ell-1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\tilde{\tt X}_{i+1}\sqcup\tilde{\tt Y}_{i}\sqcup\dots\sqcup\tilde{\tt Y}_{1}\sqcup\tilde{\tt X}_{0}\sqcup\tilde{\tt Z}_{1}\sqcup\dots\tilde{\tt Z}_{i}\subseteq[1,dp].

Denote
{\mathtt{x}}_{k}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=|\tilde{\tt X}_{k}|
,
{\mathtt{y}}_{k}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=|\tilde{\tt Y}_{k}|
and
{\mathtt{z}}_{k}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=|\tilde{\tt Z}_{k}|
for all admissible
k
.

Let
u\in\mathfrak{S}_{dp}
be the permutation which maps
\tilde{X}
increasingly onto
[1,\mu_{1}p]
and
[1,dp]\setminus\tilde{X}
increasingly onto
[\mu_{1}p+1,dp]
. Then we can write
w=xyu
with
{\mathtt{l}}(w)={\mathtt{l}}(x)+{\mathtt{l}}(y)+{\mathtt{l}}(u)
and
(x,y)\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mu_{1}p}\times\mathfrak{S}_{dp-\mu_{1}p}<\mathfrak{S}_{dp}
. Note that
u\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,i}=\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(2)}
, where

\displaystyle\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\ell^{\,{\mathtt{x}}_{\ell}}(\ell-1)^{\,{\mathtt{x}}_{\ell-1}}\cdots(i+1)^{\,{\mathtt{x}}_{i+1}}i^{\,{\mathtt{y}}_{i}}\cdots 1^{\,{\mathtt{y}}_{1}}0^{\,{\mathtt{x}}_{0}}1^{\,{\mathtt{z}}_{1}}\cdots i^{\,{\mathtt{z}}_{i}},

\displaystyle\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\ell^{d\ell-{\mathtt{x}}_{\ell}}(\ell-1)^{2d\ell-{\mathtt{x}}_{\ell-1}}\cdots(i+1)^{2d\ell-{\mathtt{x}}_{i+1}}i^{d\ell-{\mathtt{y}}_{i}}\cdots 1^{d\ell-{\mathtt{y}}_{1}}0^{2d\ell{\mathtt{x}}_{0}}1^{d\ell-{\mathtt{z}}_{1}}\cdots i^{d\ell-{\mathtt{z}}_{i}}.

So, by Lemma
5.2.19
,
u\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,i}
is non-cuspidal and
\psi_{u}{f}^{d,i}
is zero in
C_{d}
, unless
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\mu_{1},i}
and
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(2)}=\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d-\mu_{1},i}
. So we may assume that
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(1)}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\mu_{1},i}
and
\text{\boldmath$i$}^{(2)}=\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d-\mu_{1},i}
. Then we also have
x\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\mu_{1},i}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\mu_{1},j_{1}}
and
y\cdot\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d-\mu_{1},i}=\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}
, where we have set

\bar{\mu}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=(\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n})
and
\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=j_{2}\cdots j_{n}
.

An elementary computation shows that

\deg(\psi_{u}{f}^{d,i})=4\mu_{1}(2\ell-i)(d-\mu_{1}).

Now, by the inductive assumption, we have that:

(1
′
)
\deg({f}^{\mu_{1},j_{1}}\psi_{x}{f}^{\mu_{1},i})\geq t_{(\mu_{1}),j_{1}}-t_{(\mu_{1}),i}
and
\deg({f}^{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\psi_{y}{f}^{d-\mu_{1},i})\geq t_{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}-t_{(d-\mu_{1}),i}
;

(2
′
)
\deg({f}^{\mu_{1},j_{1}}\psi_{x}{f}^{\mu_{1},i})>t_{(\mu_{1}),j_{1}}-t_{(\mu_{1}),i}
unless
j_{1}=i
and
x=1
; moreover,

\deg({f}^{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\psi_{y}{f}^{d-\mu_{1},i})>t_{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}-t_{(d-\mu_{1}),i}
unless
j_{s}=i
for all
s=2,\dots,n
and
y=q_{\bar{\mu},i}
.

(3
′
)
\hat{f}^{\mu_{1},j_{1}}\psi_{x}{f}^{\mu_{1},i}=0
unless
|j_{1}-i|\leq 1
and
\hat{f}^{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}\psi_{y}{f}^{d-\mu_{1},i}=0
unless
|j_{s}-i|\leq 1
for all
s=2,\dots,n
.

Another elementary computation shows that

4\mu_{1}(2\ell-i)(d-\mu_{1})+t_{(\mu_{1}),j_{1}}-t_{(\mu_{1}),i}+t_{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}-t_{(d-\mu_{1}),i}=t_{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}-t_{d,i}

and
q_{\mu,i}=q_{\bar{\mu},i}u
,
so (1),(2),(3) above follow from (1
′
),(2
′
),(3
′
), respectively.
∎

For
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(d)
and
j\in J
, we define

d_{j}(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\sum_{r\,\,\text{with}\,\,i_{r}=j}\lambda_{r},

and, recalling the notation (
2.1.13
), set

(5.2.22)

{\underline{d}}(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=(d_{0}(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}),\dots,d_{\ell-1}(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}))\in\Uplambda(J,d).

Theorem 5.2.23
.

The algebra
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
is non-negatively graded. Also,
\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}=0
unless
{\underline{d}}(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})={\underline{d}}(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{m})
and
\mu=(\mu_{1},\dots,\mu_{n})
.
If
\min(m,n)=1
then by Lemma
5.1.6
(ii), we may assume that
m=1
, in which case the theorem follows from Lemma
5.2.21
. So we may assume that
m,n\geq 2
.

Let
\bar{\lambda}=(\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{m})
,
\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}}=i_{2}\cdots i_{m}
, and
\bar{\mu}=(\mu_{2},\dots,\mu_{n})
,
\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}=j_{2}\cdots j_{n}
. Note that

{\underline{d}}(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})={\underline{d}}((\lambda_{1}),i_{1})+{\underline{d}}(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}})
and
{\underline{d}}(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})={\underline{d}}((\mu_{1}),j_{1})+{\underline{d}}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}})
.

By Corollary
5.1.14
, we have

{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda_{1},d-\lambda_{1}}^{d}({\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}}}).

By the inductive assumption,
{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}
and
{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\lambda_{1}}
are non-negatively graded, which now implies that so is
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
. This implies the first claim.

To prove the second claim, from the Mackey Theorem
5.1.15
, we have that

\mathsf{f}_{\mu_{1},d-\mu_{1}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq{\mathsf{GGR}}^{d}_{\mu_{1},d-\mu_{1}}{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda_{1},d-\lambda_{1}}^{d}({\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}}})

has a filtration with subquotients of the form

{\mathsf{GGI}}^{\mu_{1},d-\mu_{1}}_{r,s;t,u}\,\,{}^{x}\big(({\mathsf{GGR}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{r,t}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}})\boxtimes({\mathsf{GGR}}^{d-\lambda_{1}}_{s,u}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}}})\big).

It follows that
\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
has a filtration with subquotients of the form

\big((\mathsf{f}^{\mu_{1},j_{1}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}_{r,s})\boxtimes(\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}_{t,u})\big)\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{r,s;t,u}}{}^{x}\big((\mathsf{f}_{r,t}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}})\boxtimes(\mathsf{f}_{s,u}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}}})\big).

Recalling the concatenation notation (
2.1.15
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}^{\mu_{1},j_{1}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}_{r,s}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{(\rho,\text{\boldmath$r$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(r),\,(\sigma,\text{\boldmath$s$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(s)}\mathsf{f}^{\mu_{1},j_{1}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\rho\sigma,\text{\boldmath$r$}\text{\boldmath$s$}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}_{t,u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{(\tau,\text{\boldmath$t$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(t),\,(\upsilon,\text{\boldmath$u$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(u)}\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\tau\upsilon,\text{\boldmath$t$}\text{\boldmath$u$}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{r,t}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{(\rho^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$r$}^{\prime})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(r),\,(\tau^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$t$}^{\prime})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(t)}\mathsf{f}^{\rho^{\prime}\tau^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$r$}^{\prime}\text{\boldmath$t$}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}_{s,u}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{(\sigma^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$s$}^{\prime})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(s),\,(\upsilon^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$u$}^{\prime})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(u)}\mathsf{f}^{\sigma^{\prime}\upsilon^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$s$}^{\prime}\text{\boldmath$u$}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}}}.

By the inductive assumption, we have for all
(\rho,\text{\boldmath$r$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(r)
,
(\sigma,\text{\boldmath$s$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(s)
,
(\tau,\text{\boldmath$t$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(t)
and
(\upsilon,\text{\boldmath$u$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(u)
:

\bullet

(\mathsf{f}^{\mu_{1},j_{1}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\rho\sigma,\text{\boldmath$r$}\text{\boldmath$s$}})^{0}=0
unless
{\underline{d}}(\rho,\text{\boldmath$r$})+{\underline{d}}(\sigma,\text{\boldmath$s$})={\underline{d}}((\mu_{1}),j_{1})
;

\bullet

(\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\tau\upsilon,\text{\boldmath$t$}\text{\boldmath$u$}})^{0}=0
unless
{\underline{d}}(\tau,\text{\boldmath$t$})+{\underline{d}}(\upsilon,\text{\boldmath$u$})={\underline{d}}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}})
;

\bullet

(\mathsf{f}^{\rho^{\prime}\tau^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$r$}^{\prime}\text{\boldmath$t$}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}})^{0}=0
unless
{\underline{d}}(\rho^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$r$}^{\prime})+{\underline{d}}(\tau^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$t$}^{\prime})={\underline{d}}((\lambda_{1}),i_{1})
;

\bullet

(\mathsf{f}^{\sigma^{\prime}\upsilon^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$s$}^{\prime}\text{\boldmath$u$}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}}})^{0}=0
unless
{\underline{d}}(\sigma^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$s$}^{\prime})+{\underline{d}}(\upsilon^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$u$}^{\prime})={\underline{d}}(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}})
.

Finally, note that

\displaystyle\big((\mathsf{f}^{\mu_{1},j_{1}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\rho\sigma,\text{\boldmath$r$}\text{\boldmath$s$}})\boxtimes(\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\mu_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\tau\upsilon,\text{\boldmath$t$}\text{\boldmath$u$}})\big)

\displaystyle\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{r,s;t,u}}\hskip-2.84526pt{}^{x}\big((\mathsf{f}^{\rho^{\prime}\tau^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$r$}^{\prime}\text{\boldmath$t$}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}})\boxtimes(\mathsf{f}^{\sigma^{\prime}\upsilon^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$s$}^{\prime}\text{\boldmath$u$}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d-\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}}})\big)=0

unless
(\rho,\text{\boldmath$r$})=(\rho^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$r$}^{\prime})
,
(\sigma,\text{\boldmath$s$})=(\sigma^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$s$}^{\prime})
,
(\tau,\text{\boldmath$t$})=(\tau^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$t$}^{\prime})
and
(\upsilon,\text{\boldmath$u$})=(\upsilon^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$u$}^{\prime})
.
So
(\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{0}=0
unless

\displaystyle{\underline{d}}(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})

\displaystyle={\underline{d}}((\mu_{1}),j_{1})+{\underline{d}}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\text{\boldmath$j$}})

\displaystyle={\underline{d}}(\rho,\text{\boldmath$r$})+{\underline{d}}(\sigma,\text{\boldmath$s$})+{\underline{d}}(\tau,\text{\boldmath$t$})+{\underline{d}}(\upsilon,\text{\boldmath$u$})

\displaystyle={\underline{d}}(\rho^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$r$}^{\prime})+{\underline{d}}(\sigma^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$s$}^{\prime})+{\underline{d}}(\tau^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$t$}^{\prime})+{\underline{d}}(\upsilon^{\prime},\text{\boldmath$u$}^{\prime})

\displaystyle={\underline{d}}((\lambda_{1}),i_{1})+{\underline{d}}(\bar{\lambda},\bar{\text{\boldmath$i$}})

\displaystyle={\underline{d}}(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})

as required.
∎

For
j\in J
, we have the idempotent

(5.2.24)

\mathsf{f}_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\sum_{\lambda\in\Uplambda_{0}(d)}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d}

(this is not to be confused with the idempotents
{f}^{d,j}\in C_{d}
or
\mathsf{f}^{d,j}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
, cf. (
4.2.11
)).

Corollary 5.2.25
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
and
j\in J
. Then

{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}=\mathsf{f}_{d,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}
and
\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}=\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}_{d,j}

Proof.

By (
4.2.12
), we have

{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}=\sum_{(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}.

By Theorem
5.2.23
, we have
\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}=0
unless
j
is of the form
j^{k}
, which implies the first equality. The second equality is proved similarly.
∎

5.2e.
On weight spaces
\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}

Recall from (
2.1.16
) that we consider the multicompositions from
\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)
as elements of
\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n\ell,d)
via the embedding
{\mathtt{b}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)\to\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n\ell,d)
. So, for
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)
, we use the notation

(5.2.26)

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\mathsf{f}^{{\mathtt{b}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})}=\mathsf{f}^{{\mathtt{a}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}),0^{n}\cdots(\ell-1)^{n}},\ {\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\underline{d}}({\mathtt{b}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})),\ \text{etc.}

Note that
{\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})=(|\lambda^{(0)}|,\dots,|\lambda^{(\ell-1)}|).

The second statement of Theorem
5.2.23
yields:

Lemma 5.2.27
.

If
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$},\text{\boldmath$\mu$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)
and
{\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})\neq{\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})
then
\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}=0
.

So we concentrate on the weight spaces
\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}=0
for
{\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})={\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})
. The next lemma gives an upper bound on the size of these weight spaces when both
\lambda
and
\mu
concentrated in one color
j
. We will later prove that this upper bound is sharp, i.e.
\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}
is free of rank
|{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}|
.

Lemma 5.2.28
.

Let
\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(d)
and
j\in J
. Then the graded
\Bbbk
-superpsace
\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}
is spanned by
|{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}|
elements.

Proof.

By the Mackey Theorem
5.1.15
,

\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}=\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}

has filtration with sub-quotients

\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{GGI}}^{\lambda}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}{}^{x}({\mathsf{GGR}}^{\mu}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j})=\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}}{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}).

Since all algebras are non-negatively graded by Theorem
5.2.23
, we deduce that the graded
\Bbbk
-supermodule
\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}
has filtration with sub-quotients
