\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}^{0}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}^{0}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}}{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}{\mathsf{C}}^{0}_{\mu}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j})\qquad(x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}).

By the second claim of Theorem
5.2.23
, for each
x
we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}^{0}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}=\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}^{0}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda\cap x\mu,j}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{f}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}{\mathsf{C}}_{\mu}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}=\mathsf{f}^{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu,j}{\mathsf{C}}^{0}_{\mu}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}.

By Lemma
5.2.21
,
\mathsf{f}^{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu,j}{\mathsf{C}}^{0}_{\mu}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}
is spanned by one element. The same is true for
\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{C}}^{0}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda\cap x\mu,j}
by Lemmas
5.2.21
and
5.1.6
(ii).
The result follows.
∎

Our next goal is to prove Proposition
5.2.29
generalizing Lemma
5.2.28
. Recall from (
5.1.10
) and (
5.1.11
) that for a composition
{\underline{d}}=(d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1})\in\Uplambda(J,d)
, we have the parabolic subalgebra
{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}={\mathsf{C}}_{d_{0}}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{d_{\ell-1}}\subseteq\mathsf{f}_{\underline{d}}C_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\underline{d}}
.

Proposition 5.2.29
.

Let
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$},\text{\boldmath$\mu$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)
with

{\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})={\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})=\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\underline{d}}=(d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}).

Then

(5.2.30)

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}=\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}\simeq(\mathsf{f}^{\lambda^{(0)},0}{\mathsf{C}}_{d_{0}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\mu^{(0)},0})\otimes\dots\otimes(\mathsf{f}^{\lambda^{(\ell-1)},\ell-1}{\mathsf{C}}_{d_{\ell-1}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\mu^{(\ell-1)},\ell-1}).

In particular, the graded
\Bbbk
-superpsace
\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}
is spanned by

\prod_{j\in J}|{}^{\lambda^{(j)}}{\mathscr{D}}_{d_{j}}^{\,\mu^{(j)}}|=|{}^{{\mathtt{a}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})}{\mathscr{D}}_{\underline{d}}^{\,{\mathtt{a}}(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})}|

elements.

Proof.

The isomorphism in (
5.2.30
) is clear from the identification
{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}={\mathsf{C}}_{d_{0}}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{d_{\ell-1}}
and the fact that all algebras are positively graded by Theorem
5.2.23
. So,
once the equality
\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}=\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}
is proved, everything else follows from Lemma
5.2.28
. To prove the equality, we apply the Mackey Theorem
5.1.15
to

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}=\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{GGR}}^{d}_{{\underline{d}}}{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}

Theorem
5.1.15
yields a filtration with sub-quotients isomorphic to

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{GGI}}^{\underline{d}}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}{}^{x}({\mathsf{GGR}}^{\underline{d}}_{x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})\simeq\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{GGI}}^{\underline{d}}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}_{x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})\qquad(x\in{}^{\underline{d}}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\underline{d}}).

Since all algebras are non-negatively graded by Theorem
5.2.23
, we deduce that the graded
\Bbbk
-supermodule
\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}
has filtration with sub-quotients

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}^{0}}{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}_{x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})\qquad(x\in{}^{\underline{d}}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\underline{d}}).

By Theorem
5.1.15
, the case
x=1
corresponds to the bottom level of the filtration and contributes the
\Bbbk
-subspace

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}_{{\underline{d}}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{{\underline{d}}}^{0}}\mathsf{f}_{{\underline{d}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}=\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{{\underline{d}}}^{0}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}\simeq\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}.

It remains to prove that for
1\neq x\in{}^{\underline{d}}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\underline{d}}
, we have

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}^{0}}{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}_{x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})=0.

We denote

{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}=(d_{0,0},\dots,d_{0,\ell-1};\dots;d_{\ell-1,0},\dots,d_{\ell-1,\ell-1})

so that

x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}=(d_{0,0},\dots,d_{\ell-1,0};\dots;d_{0,\ell-1},\dots,d_{\ell-1,\ell-1})

and
\sum_{i\in J}d_{i,j}=d_{j}=\sum_{i\in J}d_{j,i}
.
Define the idempotents

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\mathsf{f}_{d_{0,0},0}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{f}_{d_{0,\ell-1},0}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{f}_{d_{\ell-1,0},\ell-1}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{f}_{d_{\ell-1,\ell-1},\ell-1},

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}^{\prime}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\mathsf{f}_{d_{0,0},0}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{f}_{d_{0,\ell-1},\ell-1}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{f}_{d_{\ell-1,0},0}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{f}_{d_{\ell-1,\ell-1},\ell-1}

in the parabolic subalgebra
{\mathsf{C}}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}
,
and

\mathsf{f}(x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}})\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\mathsf{f}_{d_{0,0},0}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{f}_{d_{\ell-1,0},0}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{f}_{d_{0,\ell-1},\ell-1}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{f}_{d_{\ell-1,\ell-1},\ell-1}

in the parabolic subalgebra
{\mathsf{C}}_{x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}}
.

By Corollary
5.2.25
, we have

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}=\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})
and
\mathsf{f}_{x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}=\mathsf{f}(x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}){\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}},

and so

{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}_{x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})={}^{x}(\mathsf{f}(x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}){\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})=\mathsf{f}^{\prime}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})\,{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}(x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}){\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})

For
x\neq 1
, we must have that for some
j\in J
and distinct
i_{1},i_{2}\in J
, we have
d_{j,i_{1}}\neq 0\neq d_{j,i_{2}}
, and so
\mathsf{f}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})\mathsf{f}^{\prime}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})=0
. So in this case, we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}^{0}}{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}_{x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})

\displaystyle=\,\,

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}^{0}}\mathsf{f}^{\prime}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})\,{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}(x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}){\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})

\displaystyle=\,\,

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})\mathsf{f}^{\prime}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{{\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}}}^{0}}\mathsf{f}^{\prime}({\underline{d}}\cap x{\underline{d}})\,{}^{x}(\mathsf{f}(x^{-1}{\underline{d}}\cap{\underline{d}}){\mathsf{C}}_{\underline{d}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}})

equals
0
, as desired.
∎

5.2f.
More on the elements
g_{d,j}

Let
j\in J
,
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. Recalling the elements
g_{d,j}\in C_{d}
and
g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\in C_{\lambda}
from (
5.2.6
) and (
5.2.7
), we have the corresponding elements

(5.2.31)

\mathsf{g}_{d,j}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\mathsf{g}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\otimes\dots\mathsf{g}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\subseteq\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}.

By Lemmas
5.2.12
and
5.2.13
, we have

(5.2.32)

0\neq\mathsf{g}_{d,j}=\mathsf{f}^{d,j}\mathsf{g}_{d,j}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}\quad\text{and}\quad 0\neq\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}=\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}.

Lemma 5.2.33
.

We have
\operatorname{bideg}(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})=(0,{\bar{0}})
.

Proof.

Using (
5.1.2
), (
5.1.1
) and (
5.2.8
), we get

\displaystyle\operatorname{bideg}(\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})=

\displaystyle\,\operatorname{bideg}(g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})+(t_{\omega_{d},i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}},{\varepsilon}_{\omega_{d},i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}})-(t_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}},{\varepsilon}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\big(\textstyle\sum_{r=1}^{n}\lambda_{r}(\lambda_{r}-1)(2i_{r}-4\ell)\,,\,{\bar{0}}\big)

\displaystyle+(dp+\textstyle\sum_{r=1}^{n}\lambda_{r}(2i_{r}-4\ell)\,,\,\sum_{r=1}^{n}\lambda_{r}i_{r}\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,)

\displaystyle-\big(dp+\textstyle\sum_{r=1}^{n}\lambda_{r}^{2}(2i_{r}-4\ell)\,,\,\sum_{r=1}^{n}\lambda_{r}i_{r}\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(0,{\bar{0}}),

as desired.
∎

Lemma 5.2.34
.

We have that
\{\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\}
is a
\Bbbk
-basis of
(\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}})^{0}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.2.23
, each
{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{r}}

is non-negatively graded, so it suffices to prove the special case that
\{\mathsf{g}_{d,j}\}
is the basis of
(\mathsf{f}^{d,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}})^{0}
. By Lemmas
5.1.6
(ii) and
5.2.21
(i), the
\Bbbk
-module
(\mathsf{f}^{d,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}})^{0}
is generated by one element.
Moreover, by Lemmas
5.2.13
and
5.2.33
, we have that
\mathsf{g}_{d,j}\in(\mathsf{f}^{d,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}})^{0}
is non-zero when working over
\mathcal{O}
and over any field, so
\{\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\}
must be the basis of this
\Bbbk
-module.
∎

Corollary 5.2.35
.

We have that
\{g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\}
is a
\Bbbk
-basis of the lowest degree component of
{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}C_{\lambda}{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.2.23
,
{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}
is non-negatively graded. So Lemma
5.2.34
implies that
\{\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\}
is a
\Bbbk
-basis of the lowest degree component of
\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
. The result follows since
C_{\lambda}
is obtained from
{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}
be regrading.
∎

Recall from (
4.2.44
) the elements
\dot{w}\in{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
.

Proposition 5.2.36
.

Let
j\in J
and
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Then in
C_{d}
we have:

(i)

g_{d,j}\dot{w}=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}g_{d,j}
for all
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
;

(ii)

for any
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
we have that
g_{\lambda,j}\dot{w}=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}g_{\lambda,j}
for all
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

Note that (ii) follows from (i) since

g_{\lambda,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=g_{\lambda_{1},j}\otimes\dots\otimes g_{\lambda_{n},j}\in C_{\lambda_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes C_{\lambda_{n}}=C_{\lambda}.

To prove (i), it suffices to show that
g_{d,j}\dot{s}_{r}=(-1)^{j+1}g_{d,j}
for all
1\leq r<d
.
By Corollary
5.2.35
,
\{g_{d,j}\}
is a basis of the lowest degree component of
{f}^{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
. By Theorem
4.2.47
,
\dot{s}_{r}
is a degree zero element of order
2
. So

g_{d,j}\dot{s}_{r}=cg_{d,j}
for
c=\pm 1
. To see that actually
c=(-1)^{j+1}
,
recall that

\dot{s}_{r}={f}_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes\dot{s}\otimes{f}_{1}^{\otimes(d-r-1)}\in C_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes{f}_{\omega_{2}}C_{2}{f}_{\omega_{2}}\otimes C_{1}^{\otimes(d-r-1)}\subseteq{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}},

and note that
we can write

g_{d,j}=yx
for some
y\in C_{d}
and

x={f}_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes g_{2,j}\otimes{f}_{1}^{\otimes(d-r-1)}\in C_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes{f}^{2,j}C_{2}{f}_{\omega_{2}}\otimes C_{1}^{\otimes(d-r-1)}\subseteq{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}},

so the computation reduced to the case
d=2
, in which case we need to prove
g_{2,j}\dot{s}=(-1)^{j+1}g_{2,j}
, knowing that
g_{2,j}\dot{s}=cg_{2,j}
for
c=\pm 1
.

Recalling the element
\upsigma
from (
4.2.41
), observe that
\upsigma{f}^{j,j}=zg_{2,j}
for
some
z\in C_{2}
. Hence we have
zg_{2,j}\dot{s}{f}^{j,j}=czg_{2,j}{f}^{j,j}
or

(5.2.37)

\upsigma\dot{s}{f}^{j,j}=c\upsigma{f}^{j,j}.

On the other hand, by (
4.2.42
), we have
\dot{s}{f}^{j,j}=(\upsigma+(-1)^{j}){f}^{j,j}
, and by Theorem
4.2.47
, we have
\dot{s}^{2}{f}^{j,j}={f}^{j,j}
, so

(\upsigma+(-1)^{j})\dot{s}{f}^{j,j}={f}^{j,j},

whence we have

\upsigma\dot{s}{f}^{j,j}=(-1)^{j+1}\dot{s}{f}^{j,j}+{f}^{j,j}=(-1)^{j+1}(\upsigma+(-1)^{j}){f}^{j,j}+{f}^{j,j}=(-1)^{j+1}\upsigma{f}^{j,j}.

Combining with (
5.2.37
), we deduce that
c=(-1)^{j+1}
as required.
∎

Chapter 6
Imaginary Schur-Weyl duality

Fix
j\in J
. Recall from §
4.2h
the graded
C_{1}
-supermodule
L_{j}=\Bbbk\cdot v_{j}
and the graded
\hat{C}_{1}
-supermodule
\hat{L}_{j}
generated by the vector
\hat{v}_{j}
such that
{f}^{i}v_{j}=\delta_{i,j}v_{j}
and
{f}^{i}\hat{v}_{j}=\delta_{i,j}\hat{v}_{j}

for all
i\in J
. Recall from (
4.2.53
) that

\operatorname{bideg}(v_{j})=\operatorname{bideg}(\hat{v}_{j})=(1+2j-2\ell,\,j\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,).

6.1.
Colored imaginary tensor space

6.1a.
The imaginary tensor space
M_{d,j}

For
d\in\mathbb{N}
, we define two versions of
(
d
th) imaginary tensor superspace (of color
j
)
which will correspond to each other under the functors
{\mathpzc F}_{d}
and
{\mathpzc G}_{d}
from (
4.2.15
) (at the moment we do not yet know that
{\mathpzc F}_{d}
and
{\mathpzc G}_{d}
are equivalences—this will be established in §
6.2
):

(6.1.1)

\displaystyle M_{d,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}=C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d},

(6.1.2)

\displaystyle\hat{M}_{d,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\,\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}=\hat{C}_{d}1_{\delta^{d}}\otimes_{\hat{C}_{\omega_{d}}}\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}.

(see Lemma
4.1.9
(i) for the last equality). Let

v_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes v_{j}^{\otimes d}\in M_{d,j}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{v}_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=1_{\delta^{d}}\otimes\hat{v}_{j}^{\otimes d}\in\hat{M}_{d,j},

so the
C_{d}
-module
M_{d,j}
is generated by

v_{d,j}

and
the
\hat{C}_{d}
-module

\hat{M}_{d,j}
is generated by

\hat{v}_{d,j}.

Note that

(6.1.3)

{f}^{j^{d}}v_{d,j}=v_{d,j}\quad\text{and}\quad{f}^{j^{d}}\hat{v}_{d,j}=\hat{v}_{d,j}.

Lemma 6.1.4
.

We have:

(i)

M_{d,j}\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}(\hat{M}_{d,j})
.

(ii)

\hat{M}_{d,j}\simeq{\mathpzc G}_{d}(M_{d,j})=\hat{C}_{d}{f}_{d}\otimes_{C_{d}}M_{d,j}
and the map
M_{d,j}\to\hat{M}_{d,j},\ m\mapsto{f}_{d}\otimes m
is injectve.

Proof.

As
M_{d,j}\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}({\mathpzc G}_{d}(M_{d,j}))

by (
4.2.18
), it suffices to prove (ii).
We have

\displaystyle{\mathpzc G}_{d}(M_{d,j})

\displaystyle=\hat{C}_{d}{f}_{d}\otimes_{C_{d}}M_{d,j}

\displaystyle=\hat{C}_{d}{f}_{d}\otimes_{C_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle\simeq\hat{C}_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle\simeq\hat{C}_{d}1_{\delta^{d}}\otimes_{\hat{C}_{\omega_{d}}}\hat{C}_{\omega_{d}}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle\simeq\hat{C}_{d}1_{\delta^{d}}\otimes_{\hat{C}_{\omega_{d}}}\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle=\hat{M}_{d,j}.

Finally, the map in (ii) is just the isomorphism between
M_{d,j}
and
{f}_{d}\hat{M}_{d,j}
.
∎

More generally, let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
. Recalling the parabolic subalgebra
C_{\lambda}=C_{\lambda_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes C_{\lambda_{n}}\subseteq{f}_{\lambda}C_{d}{f}_{\lambda}
, we have the graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule

M_{\lambda,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=M_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{\lambda_{n},j}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{\lambda}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

with generator
v_{\lambda,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=v_{\lambda_{1},j}\otimes\dots\otimes v_{\lambda_{n},j}
.
There are obvious analogous modules
\hat{M}_{\lambda,j}
over
\hat{C}_{\lambda}
.
Note using (
4.2.53
) that

(6.1.5)

\operatorname{bideg}(v_{\lambda,j})=\operatorname{bideg}(\hat{v}_{\lambda,j})=(d(1+2j-2\ell),dj\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,).

6.1b.
The space
{f}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}

Recall from (
4.2.44
) the elements
\dot{w}\in{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
.

Lemma 6.1.6
.

We have:

(i)

{f}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}={f}^{j^{d}}M_{d,j}
has
\Bbbk
-basis
\{\dot{w}v_{d,j}\mid w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}
.

(ii)

{f}_{\omega_{d}}\hat{M}_{d,j}={f}^{j^{d}}\hat{M}_{d,j}
has
\Bbbk
-basis
\{\dot{w}\hat{v}_{d,j}\mid w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
6.1.4
, we get
{f}_{\omega_{d}}\hat{M}_{d,j}\simeq{f}_{\omega_{d}}{f}_{d}\hat{M}_{d,j}\simeq{f}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}
, so (ii) follows from (i). To prove (i),
note that

{f}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}={\mathrm{GGR}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}M_{d,j}={\mathrm{GGR}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}.

By Theorem
4.2.34
, taking into account (
4.2.45
), this has a filtration

(\Phi_{\leq w})_{w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}},

where

\Phi_{\leq w}=\sum_{x\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\,\text{with}\,x\leq w}C_{\omega_{d}}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\upsigma_{x}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

and

\Phi_{\leq x}(V)/\Phi_{<x}(V)\simeq\,{}^{x}(L_{j}^{\boxtimes d})\simeq L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}.

Note that under the isomorphism above, we have that the coset
\upsigma_{x}v_{d,j}+\Phi_{<x}(V)
corresponds to
v_{j}^{\otimes d}
, and

{f}_{\omega_{d}}\upsigma_{x}v_{d,j}={f}^{j^{d}}\upsigma_{x}v_{d,j}=\upsigma_{x}v_{d,j}
, which implies that
{f}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}={f}^{j^{d}}M_{d,j}
has
\Bbbk
-basis
\{\upsigma_{w}v_{d,j}\mid w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}
. Now we can apply Lemma
4.2.46
.
∎

Lemma 6.1.7
.

We have:

(i)

For each
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, the vector
\dot{w}v_{d,j}
spans a graded
C_{\omega_{d}}
-subsupermodule of
{f}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}
isomorphic to
L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}
.

(ii)

{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}\simeq(L_{j}^{\boxtimes d})^{\oplus d!}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.1.6
, (ii) follows from (i). To prove (i), note that

{f}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}\simeq{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}={\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}={f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{1}^{\otimes d}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}.

Now using Theorem
4.2.47
, the statement (i) becomes a statement about
H_{d}(A_{\ell})
which is easily checked using the defining relations for
H_{d}(A_{\ell})
.
∎

6.1c.
Symmetric group action on
M_{d,j}

By adjointness of
{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}
and
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}
,
we have an explicit isomorphism

\operatorname{End}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})=\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}({\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d},{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\omega_{d}}}(L_{j}^{\boxtimes d},{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}).

It now follows from Lemma
6.1.7
that for every
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, there exists a unique
C_{d}
-endomorphism
{\varphi}_{w}
of
M_{d,j}
which is characterized by the property

(6.1.8)

{\varphi}_{w}(v_{d,j})=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}\dot{w}v_{d,j}.

The sign
(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}
is chosen for technical reasons—it makes some of the notions of §
7.2
, such as imaginary symmetric, exterior and divided powers, more natural. In fact, the convenience of the sign is already clear from
Proposition
5.2.36
.

Recall that we always consider
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
as concentrated in bidegree
(0,{\bar{0}})
.
Applying Lemma
6.1.7
one more time, we get:

Theorem 6.1.9
.

There is an isomorphism of graded superalgebras

\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{End}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}

which maps
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
to the unique endomorphism
{\varphi}_{w}
such that

{\varphi}_{w}(v_{d,j})=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}\dot{w}v_{d,j}.

By Theorem
6.1.9
, we can consider
M_{d,j}
as a graded
(C_{d},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bisupermodule with
mw={\varphi}_{w}(m)
for any
m\in M_{d,j}
and
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
; in particular,

(6.1.10)

(cv_{d,j})w=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}c\dot{w}v_{d,j}\qquad(c\in C_{d},\ w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}).

Corollary 6.1.11
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. There is an isomorphism of graded superalgebras

\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{End}_{C_{\lambda}}(M_{\lambda,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}

which maps
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
to the unique endomorphism
{\varphi}_{w}
with

{\varphi}_{w}(v_{\lambda,j})=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}\dot{w}v_{\lambda,j}.

By Corollary
6.1.11
, we can consider
M_{\lambda,j}
as a graded
(C_{\lambda},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda})
-bisupermodule with

(6.1.12)

mw={\varphi}_{w}(m)\qquad(m\in M_{\lambda,j},\,w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}).

In particular,

(6.1.13)

(cv_{\lambda,j})w=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}c\dot{w}v_{\lambda,j}\qquad(c\in C_{d},\ w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}).

6.1d.
Gelfand-Graev induction and restriction on imaginary tensor spaces

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. By transitivity of the Gelfand-Graev induction, we have

M_{d,j}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{\lambda,j},

and so we have a homomorphism
of graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodules

i_{\lambda}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}M_{\lambda,j}\to{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{d,j},\ v_{\lambda,j}\mapsto{f}_{\lambda}v_{d,j}

Lemma 6.1.14
.

For each
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
, the homomorphism
i_{\lambda}
injective. Identifying
M_{\lambda,j}
with
i_{\lambda}(M_{\lambda,j})
, we have

{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{d,j}=\bigoplus_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}}M_{\lambda,j}x.

Proof.

By (
4.2.45
), we have
\psi_{\rho_{d}(x)}v_{d,j}=\upsigma_{x}v_{d,j}
for all
x\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, so
by Theorem
4.2.34
,

{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}M_{d,j}={\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\omega_{d}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

has a filtration

(\Phi_{\leq x})_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}},

where

\Phi_{\leq x}=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\lambda}\upsigma_{y}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

and

\Phi_{\leq x}(V)/\Phi_{<x}(V)\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{\lambda}\,{}^{x}(L_{j}^{\boxtimes d})\simeq M_{\lambda,j}.

Note that under the isomorphism above, we have that the coset
\upsigma_{x}v_{d,j}+\Phi_{<x}(V)
corresponds to
v_{\lambda,j}
.

When
x=1
, we deduce that
i_{\lambda}
is injective. Moreover, using Lemma
4.2.46
, we have the filtration
(\Phi^{\prime}_{\leq x})_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}}
of
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}M_{d,j}
,
where

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}_{\leq x}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\lambda}\dot{y}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}C_{\lambda}{f}_{\lambda}v_{d,j}y

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{y\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}\,\text{with}\,y\leq x}M_{\lambda,j}y

which implies the lemma.
∎

By functoriality of
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
, we have that
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{\lambda,j}
becomes a graded
(C_{d},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda})
-bisupermodule. Similarly,
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{d,j}
becomes a graded
(C_{\lambda},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bisupermodule.

Lemma 6.1.15
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then:

(i)

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{\lambda,j}\simeq M_{d,j}
as graded
(C_{d},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda})
-bisupermodules, where the right action of
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
on
M_{d,j}
is via restriction from
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.

(ii)

{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{d,j}\simeq M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
as graded
(C_{\lambda},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bisupermodules.

Proof.

(i) By transitivity of Gelfand-Graev induction,
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{\lambda,j}\simeq M_{d,j}
as graded
C_{d}
-supermodules. By (
6.1.12
), the isomorphism is compatible with the
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
-action.

(ii) follows easily from Lemma
6.1.14
.
∎

Corollary 6.1.16
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
.
The following pairs of functors are isomorphic:

(i)

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\circ(M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\,-)
and
(M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\,-)\circ{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\,\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to C_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}
.

(ii)

{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}\circ(M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\,-)
and
(M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\,-)\circ{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\,\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\,\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to C_{\lambda}\!\operatorname{-mod}
.

Proof.

(i) Take
V\in\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\!\operatorname{-mod}
. Using Lemma
6.1.15
(i), we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}V

\displaystyle=M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}V

\displaystyle\simeq M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}V

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathrm{GGI}}^{n}_{\lambda}M_{\lambda,j})\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}V

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}^{n}_{\lambda}(M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}V),

as required.

(ii) Using Lemma
6.1.15
(ii), for an
V\in\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{n}(M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}V)

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{n}M_{d,j})\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}V

\displaystyle\simeq(M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}V

\displaystyle\simeq M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}{\mathrm{Res}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}V,

as required.
∎

6.1e.
Weight basis of
M_{d,j}

Recall the elements
g_{d,j}\in{f}^{d,j}C_{d}{f}^{j^{d}}
and

g_{\lambda,j}=g_{\lambda_{1},j}\otimes\dots\otimes g_{\lambda_{n},j}\in{f}^{\lambda,j}C_{\lambda}{f}^{j^{d}}
for
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
from §
5.2b
.

Lemma 6.1.17
.

For any
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, we have
g_{d,j}v_{d,j}w=g_{d,j}v_{d,j}
.
More generally, for
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
and
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
, we have
g_{\lambda,j}v_{d,j}w=g_{\lambda,j}v_{d,j}
.

Proof.

For
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
, we have

g_{\lambda,j}v_{d,j}w=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}g_{\lambda,j}\dot{w}v_{d,j}=g_{\lambda,j}v_{d,j},

thanks to Proposition
5.2.36
(ii).
∎

Lemma 6.1.18
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{d,j}
has
\Bbbk
-basis
\{g_{\lambda,j}v_{d,j}x\mid x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}\}
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
6.1.15
(ii), we get

{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{d,j}={f}^{\lambda,j}f_{\lambda}M_{d,j}={f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}M_{d,j}\simeq{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d},

with
{f}^{\lambda,j}v_{d,j}\in{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{d,j}
corresponding to

{f}^{\lambda,j}v_{\lambda,j}\otimes 1
.
So, it suffices to note that
{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{\lambda,j}
has basis
g_{\lambda,j}v_{\lambda,j}
. If
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})
then
{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{\lambda,j}\simeq{f}^{\lambda_{1},j}M_{\lambda_{1},j}\otimes\dots\otimes{f}^{\lambda_{n},j}M_{\lambda_{n},j}
, so the required claim reduces to the case where
\lambda
has only one part, i.e. to the claim that

{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}
has basis
g_{d,j}v_{d,j}
.

By Proposition
5.2.17
, we have

\displaystyle{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}

\displaystyle={f}^{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle=g_{d,j}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle=g_{d,j}{f}_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle=g_{d,j}{f}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j},

where we have used
g_{d,j}=g_{d,j}{f}_{\omega_{d}}
for the second equality. By Lemma
6.1.6
(i),
{f}_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}

has

\Bbbk
-basis
\{\dot{w}v_{d,j}\mid w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}=\{\pm v_{j,d}w\mid w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}
. Since
g_{d,j}v_{j,d}w=g_{d,j}v_{j,d}
by Lemma
6.1.17
, we deduce that
g_{d,j}v_{j,d}
spans
{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}
. So it suffices to prove that

{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}=1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}}M_{d,j}
is non-zero and free as a
\Bbbk
-module.
By Lemma
6.1.4
, we have

1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}}M_{d,j}\simeq 1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}}{f}_{d}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\,\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}=1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\,\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}.

By Lemma
3.3.23
, the
\Bbbk
-module
1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\,\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}
is free and non-zero, so
1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}}M_{d,j}
is also free and non-zero by Lemma
3.3.43
.
∎

Lemma 6.1.19
.

We have

M_{d,j}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda_{0}(d)}{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{d,j}
.

Proof.

We just need to prove that the orthogonal idempotents appearing in the sum (
4.2.12
) act on
M_{d,j}
as zero unless they are of the form
{f}^{\lambda,j}
. Using Lemma
6.1.14
and induction on
d
, this reduces to the case where
\lambda
has just one non-zero part, i.e. we need to prove that
{f}^{d,i}M_{d,j}=0
for
i\neq j
.

By definition,
g_{d,i}=g_{d,i}{f}^{i^{d}}=g_{d,i}{f}^{i^{d}}f_{\omega_{d}}
. So, using Proposition
5.2.17
, we get

\displaystyle{f}^{d,i}M_{d,j}

\displaystyle={f}^{d,i}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle=g_{d,i}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}

\displaystyle=g_{d,i}{f}^{i^{d}}f_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}.

But for
i\neq j
, we have

{f}^{i^{d}}f_{\omega_{d}}C_{d}{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{C_{\omega_{d}}}L_{j}^{\boxtimes d}={f}^{i^{d}}f_{\omega_{d}}M_{d,j}=0,

thanks to Lemma
6.1.7
(ii).
∎

Corollary 6.1.20
.

We have that
\{g_{\lambda,j}v_{d,j}x\mid\lambda\in\Uplambda_{0}(d),\,x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}\}
is a
\Bbbk
-basis of
M_{d,j}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemmas
6.1.19
and
6.1.18
.
∎

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. By (
5.2.8
) and (
6.1.5
), we can write

(6.1.21)

\begin{split}\operatorname{bideg}(g_{d,j}v_{d,j})&=(a_{d,j},\,dj\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,),\\
\operatorname{bideg}(g_{\lambda,j}v_{\lambda,j})&=(a_{\lambda,j},\,dj\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,)\end{split}

for

(6.1.22)

\begin{split}a_{d,j}&\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=d(1+2\ell)+d^{2}(2j-4\ell),\\
a_{\lambda,j}&\textstyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=d(1+2\ell)+(2j-4\ell)\sum_{r=1}^{n}\lambda_{r}^{2}.\end{split}

Lemma 6.1.23
.

For each
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
,
we have a bidegree
(0,{\bar{0}})
isomorphism of right
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-modules

{\mathpzc{Q}}^{-a_{\lambda,j}}\Uppi^{dj}{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{d,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}

which maps
g_{\lambda,j}v_{d,j}
to
1\otimes 1_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}
.

Proof.

We have

\operatorname{bideg}(g_{\lambda,j}v_{d,j})=\operatorname{bideg}(g_{\lambda,j}v_{\lambda,j})=(a_{\lambda,j},dj\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,).

Now the result follows from
Lemmas
6.1.18
and
6.1.17
.
∎

6.2.
Morita equivalence of
\hat{C}_{d}
and
C_{d}

We now pause to prove that the functors
{\mathpzc F}_{d}
and
{\mathpzc G}_{d}
from (
4.2.15
) are quasi-inverse equivalences, thus resolving Inductive Assumption
4.2.21
.

6.2a.
Counting the irreducibles

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
.
By Lemma
4.1.13
, we have
|{\mathrm{Irr}}(\hat{C}_{d})|=|{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)|
.
Since
C_{d}={f}_{d}\hat{C}_{d}{f}_{d}
is an idempotent truncation of
\hat{C}_{d}
, we have

(6.2.1)

{\mathrm{Irr}}(C_{d})\leq|{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)|.

Moreover,
in view of Lemmas
2.2.16
and
2.2.20
,
to prove that the functors
{\mathpzc F}_{d}
and
{\mathpzc G}_{d}
are quasi-inverse equivalences,
it suffices to prove that

|{\mathrm{Irr}}(C_{d})|=|{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)|.

Recall from (
4.2.12
) that
{f}_{d}
, which is the identity in
C_{d}
, decomposes as the sum of orthogonal idempotents
{f}_{d}=\sum_{(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}\in\hat{C}_{d}
.
In order to bound
|{\mathrm{Irr}}(C_{d})|
below, we introduce the
Gelfand-Graev formal character
of a finite dimensional graded
C_{d}
-supermodule
V
:

\operatorname{GGCh}(V)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\sum_{(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)}(\dim{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}V)\cdot(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\mathbb{Z}\cdot\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d).

where
\mathbb{Z}\cdot\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)
is the free
\mathbb{Z}
-module on the basis
\Uplambda_{0}^{\operatorname{col}}(d)
.

Recalling the notation (
2.1.21
) and (
4.2.6
) and the notation
\lambda^{\prime}
for transposed partition, a key observation is:

Lemma 6.2.2
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
,
j\in J
and
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
.
Then

\operatorname{GGCh}(M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda})=(\lambda^{\prime},j)+\sum_{\mu\lhd\lambda^{\prime}}c_{\mu,j}\cdot(\mu,j)

for some
c_{\mu,j}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Let
\mu\in\Uplambda_{0}(d)
.
By Lemma
6.1.23
, we have an isomorphism of vector spaces

{f}^{\mu,j}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}\cong\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mu}}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\mu}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}.

But by
[
22
, Lemma 4.6]
, we have
\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\mu}}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\mu}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}=0
unless
\mu\unlhd\lambda^{\prime}
, and
\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\prime}}}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\prime}}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}
is
1
-dimensional.
∎

Corollary 6.2.3
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
j\in J
. For every
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
there exists a unique up to isomorphism irreducible graded
C_{d}
-supermodule
L_{j}(\lambda)
such that
L_{j}(\lambda)
is a composition factor of
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
and

(6.2.4)

\operatorname{GGCh}(L_{j}(\lambda))=(\lambda,j)+\sum_{\mu\lhd\lambda}b_{\mu,j}\cdot(\mu,j)

for some
b_{\mu,j}\in\mathbb{N}
.
In particular, the number of non-isomorphic composition factors of
M_{d,j}
is at least
|{\mathcal{P}}(d)|
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
6.2.2
using the obvious fact that
the Gelfand-Graev formal character of a (finite dimensional) graded supermodule is the sum of the Gelfand-Graev formal characters of its composition factors.
∎

Corollary 6.2.5
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
,
j\in J
,
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
. Then
L_{j}(\lambda^{\prime})
appears as a composition factor of
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}
with multiplicity
1
, and
L_{j}(\mu)
is a composition factor of
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}
only if
\mu\unlhd\lambda^{\prime}
.

For
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}=(\lambda^{(0)},\dots,\lambda^{(\ell-1)})\in{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)
with
d_{j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=|\lambda^{(j)}|
for all
j\in J
, we define

(6.2.6)

L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}\big(L_{0}(\lambda^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\lambda^{(\ell-1)})\big).

We can now canonically parametrize all irreducible graded
C_{d}
-supermodules as well as the composition factors of each
M_{d,j}
.

Theorem 6.2.7
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
.
Then:

(i)

{\mathrm{Irr}}(C_{d})=\{L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})\mid\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)\}
;

(ii)

\{L_{j}(\lambda)\mid\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)\}
is a complete and non-redundant set of composition factors of
M_{d,j}
up to isomorphism.

Proof.

We prove the theorem by induction on
d
.
Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
and suppose the theorem is true for all
d^{\prime}<d
.

Claim 1.
Let
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$},\text{\boldmath$\mu$}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)
and set
d_{j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=|\lambda^{(j)}|
for all
j\in J
. If the irreducible graded
C_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}
-supermodule
L_{0}(\lambda^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\lambda^{(\ell-1)})
appears as a composition factor of
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}^{d}L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})
then
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}=\text{\boldmath$\mu$}
and the multiplicity of this composition factor is
1
.

To prove Claim 1, we set
m_{j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=|\mu^{(j)}|
for all
j\in J
.
We may assume that at least two of the
m_{j}
are non-zero or at least two of the
d_{j}
are non-zero, since otherwise the claim boils down to the proof that
L_{j}(\lambda)\cong L_{i}(\mu)
only if
j=i
and
\lambda=\mu
, which is clear by considering the Gelfand-Graev formal characters of
L_{j}(\lambda)
and
L_{i}(\mu)
, see (
6.2.4
).

By Theorem
4.2.34
,

{\mathrm{GGR}}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}^{d}L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})={\mathrm{GGR}}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}^{d}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{m_{0},\dots,m_{\ell-1}}^{d}\big(L_{0}(\mu^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\mu^{(\ell-1)})\big)

has filtration with subfactors of the form

(6.2.8)

{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d_{0}\,;\,\dots\,;\,d_{\ell-1}}_{n_{0,0},\dots,n_{\ell-1,0}\,;\,\dots\,;\,n_{0,\ell-1},\dots,n_{\ell-1,\ell-1}}\,V

where:

(a)
\sum_{i\in J}n_{i,j}=d_{j}
for all
j\in J
,

(b)
\sum_{j\in J}n_{i,j}=m_{i}
for all
i\in J
, and

(c)
V
is obtained by an appropriate twisting of the module

\big({\mathrm{GGR}}^{m_{0}}_{n_{0,0},\dots,n_{0,\ell-1}}L_{0}(\mu^{(0)})\big)\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\big({\mathrm{GGR}}^{m_{\ell-1}}_{n_{\ell-1,0},\dots,n_{\ell-1,\ell-1}}L_{\ell-1}(\mu^{(\ell-1)})\big).

Note by (a),(b) and the previous paragraph that all
n_{i,j}<d
.

Assume that the module in (
6.2.8
) is non-zero. By Lemma
6.1.15
(ii), if
L_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}
is a composition factor of
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{m_{i}}_{n_{i,0},\dots,n_{i,\ell-1}}L_{i}(\mu^{(i)})
then each
L_{j}
is a composition factors of
M_{n_{i,j},i}
, so by the inductive assumption
L_{j}
is of the form
L_{i}(\mu^{(i,j)})
for some
\mu^{(i,j)}\in{\mathcal{P}}(n_{i,j})
. Hence
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}^{d}L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})
has a filtration with factors of the form

X_{0}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes X_{\ell-1}

where for each
j\in J
we have set

X_{j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathrm{GGI}}^{d_{j}}_{n_{0,j},\dots,n_{\ell-1,j}}\big(L_{0}(\mu^{(0,j)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\mu^{(\ell-1,j)})\big).

By the inductive hypothesis, each
X_{j}
is irreducible. By considering Gelfand-Graev formal characters we deduce that
X_{j}\cong L_{j}(\lambda^{(j)})
if and only if
\mu^{(j,j)}=\lambda^{(j)}
and
\mu^{(i,i)}=\varnothing
for all
i\neq j
. Thus,
n_{j,j}=d_{j}
and
n_{i,j}=0
for all
i\neq j
. We conclude that
m_{j}=d_{j}
and
\mu^{(j)}=\lambda^{(j)}
for all
j
. This completes the proof of Claim 1.

Claim 2.
Let
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)
. The
C_{d}
-module
L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})
has simple head; denote it by
L^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}
. Then the multiplicity of
L^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}
in
L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})
is
1
.

To prove Claim 2, suppose that an irreducible
C_{d}
-module
L
appears in the head of
L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})
. Then, setting
d_{j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=|\lambda^{(j)}|
for all
j\in J
,
by adjointness of Gelfand-Graev induction and restriction, we have that the space

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}),L)=\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}}(L_{0}(\lambda^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\lambda^{(\ell-1)}),{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}\,L)

is non-zero.
So each irreducible
L
in the head of
L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})
contributes a composition factor
L_{0}(\lambda^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\lambda^{(\ell-1)})
into
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})
. But by Claim 1, the multiplicity of

L_{0}(\lambda^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\lambda^{(\ell-1)})
in
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})
is
1
, so Claim 2 follows.

Claim 3.
If
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$},\text{\boldmath$\mu$}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)
are distinct then
L^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}\not\cong L^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}
.

Indeed, if
L^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}\cong L^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}
then
L^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}
is a quotient of
L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})
. By the adjointness of Gelfand-Graev induction and restriction, we have that
L_{0}(\lambda^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\lambda^{(\ell-1)})
is a submodule of
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}L^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}
, hence
L_{0}(\lambda^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\lambda^{(\ell-1)})
is a composition factor of
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})
. Now
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}=\text{\boldmath$\mu$}
by Claim 1.

Now we can finish the proof of the theorem. By (
6.2.1
) and Claim 3, we have
{\mathrm{Irr}}(C_{d})=\{L^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}\mid\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)\}
. So to finish the proof of (i), it suffices to show that
L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})=L^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}
for all
\text{\boldmath$\mu$}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)
. If some
L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})
is not irreducible, then by Claim 2, it has a composition factor
L^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}
in its socle with
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\neq\text{\boldmath$\mu$}
. This yields a non-zero homomorphism
L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})\to L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})
, so by adjointness of Gelfand-Graev induction and restriction, we have that
L_{0}(\lambda^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{\ell-1}(\lambda^{(\ell-1)})
is a submodule of
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1}}L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})
. By Claim 1, we have
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}=\text{\boldmath$\mu$}
which is a contradiction. We have proved (i)

(ii) follows from (i) since
L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})
has
a Gelfand-Graev formal character compatible with being a composition factor of
M_{d,j}
only if
\lambda^{(i)}=\varnothing
for all
i\neq j
.
∎

Corollary 6.2.9
.

We have:

(i)

If
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
then
|{\mathrm{Irr}}(C_{d})|=|{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(d)|
.

(ii)

The functors
{\mathpzc F}_{d}
and
{\mathpzc G}_{d}
from (
4.2.15
) are quasi-inverse equivalences. Hence the functors
{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}
and
{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda}
from (
4.2.15
) are quasi-inverse equivalences for any
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
.

(iii)

If
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
then the functors
{\mathpzc F}_{d}
and
{\mathpzc G}_{d}
from (
4.2.15
) restrict to quasi-inverse equivalences of the categories of finite dimensional graded supermodules over
\hat{C}_{d}
and
C_{d}
.

Proof.

(i) follows from Theorem
6.2.7
(i).

(ii) follows from (i) in view of Lemmas
2.2.16
and
2.2.20
.

(iii) We just need to make sure that the functors preserve the finite dimensionality. This follows from the fact that the irreducible modules over
\hat{C}_{d}
are finite dimensional, see Lemma
3.3.19
.
∎

Corollary 6.2.10
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
the left graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule

{f}_{\lambda}C_{d}
is finitely generated projective and
the right graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule

C_{d}{f}_{\lambda}
is finitely generated projective.

Proof.

Note that
{f}_{\lambda}C_{d}={f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{d}{f}_{d}
is a direct summand of the left graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule
{f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{d}
, so it suffices to prove that
{f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{d}
is finitely generated projective. But
{f}_{\lambda}\hat{C}_{d}={f}_{\lambda}1_{\lambda\delta}\hat{C}_{d}\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}(1_{\lambda\delta}\hat{C}_{d})
, and since
{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}
is an equivalence by Corollary
6.2.9
(ii), it suffices to prove that the left graded
\hat{C}_{\lambda\delta}
-supermodule
1_{\lambda\delta}\hat{C}_{d}
is finitely generated projective. But this supermodule is in fact finitely generated free by Lemma
4.1.6
(iii). This completes the proof for left modules. For right modules the argument is similar. ∎

Corollary 6.2.11
.

Suppose that
P
is a finitely generated projective graded
C_{d}
-supermodule. Then for any
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
, we have that
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}P
is a finitely generated projective graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule.

Proof.

By Corollary
6.2.10
, the functor
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}
sends the regular graded
C_{d}
-supermodule
C_{d}
to a finitely generated projective graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule
f_{\lambda}C_{d}
. This implies the corollary since
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}
commutes with taking direct sums and direct summands, as well as with grading and parity shifts.
∎

Corollary 6.2.12
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
,
j\in J
and
c,d\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
L_{j}((c+d))
is a composition factor of
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{c,d}^{c+d}\ L_{j}((c))\boxtimes L_{j}((d))
.

Proof.

Since
(c+d)
is the most dominant partition of
c+d
, it suffices to prove that
(c+d,j)
appears in the Gelfand-Graev formal character of
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{c,d}^{c+d}\ L_{j}((c))\boxtimes L_{j}((d))
.
But by (
4.2.17
) and (
4.2.25
), we have

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{c,d}^{c+d}(L_{j}(c)\boxtimes L_{j}(d))\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{c+d}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}_{c\delta,d\delta}^{(c+d)\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{c}\big(L_{j}((c))\big)\boxtimes{\mathpzc G}_{d}\big(L_{j}((d))\big).

Since
{\mathpzc F}_{c}{\mathpzc G}_{c}\big(L_{j}((c))\big)\simeq L_{j}((c))
, we have that the inequality
{f}^{c,j}L_{j}((c))\neq 0
implies that

{f}^{c,j}{\mathpzc G}_{c}\big(L_{j}((c))\big)\neq 0
. So
1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{c,j}}{\mathpzc G}_{c}\big(L_{j}((c))\big)\neq 0
. Similarly
1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}}{\mathpzc G}_{d}\big(L_{j}((d))\big)\neq 0
. Since the word
\hat{g}^{c+d,j}
is a shuffle of the words
\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{c,j}
and
\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{d,j}
, it follows from Corollary
3.3.30
that

1_{\hat{g}^{c+d,j}}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{c\delta,d\delta}^{(c+d)\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{c}\big(L_{j}((c))\big)\boxtimes{\mathpzc G}_{d}\big(L_{j}((d))\big)\neq 0.

Byg Lemma
3.3.43
this implies

{f}^{c+d,j}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{c\delta,d\delta}^{(c+d)\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{c}\big(L_{j}((c))\big)\boxtimes{\mathpzc G}_{d}\big(L_{j}((d))\big)\neq 0.

Therefore

{f}^{c+d,j}{\mathpzc F}_{c+d}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{c\delta,d\delta}^{(c+d)\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{c}\big(L_{j}((c))\big)\boxtimes{\mathpzc G}_{d}\big(L_{j}((d))\big)\neq 0,

as required.
∎

6.2b.
Gelfand-Graev duality

Throughout the subsection we assume that
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and work with finite dimensional (graded super)modules.
Recall the duality
\circledast
from §§
3.3a
,
3.3c
. We will use
\circledast
to define a duality on finite dimensional graded
C_{d}
-supermodules which we denote by
\sharp
. Namely, given
V\in C_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we define

(6.2.13)

V^{\sharp}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathpzc F}_{d}\big(({\mathpzc G}_{d}V)^{\circledast}\big).

More generally,
for
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
and
V\in C_{\lambda}\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we define

(6.2.14)

V^{\sharp}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}\big(({\mathpzc G}_{\lambda}V)^{\circledast}\big).

Note, using (
4.2.17
) and (
3.3.33
), that for
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)

and
V_{1}\in C_{\lambda_{1}}\!\operatorname{-mod},\dots,V_{n}\in C_{\lambda_{n}}\!\operatorname{-mod}

we have

(6.2.15)

(V_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{n})^{\sharp}\simeq V_{1}^{\sharp}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{n}^{\sharp}.

Lemma 6.2.16
.

We have that
\sharp
defines a contravariant functor on the
finite dimensional graded
C_{d}
-supermodules. Moreover,
\sharp\circ\sharp
is equivalent to the identity functor and we have a functorial isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(V,W)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(W^{\sharp},V^{\sharp})

Proof.

This follows from the corresponding properties of
\circledast
and the fact that the functors
{\mathpzc F}_{d}
and
{\mathpzc G}_{d}
are quasi-inverse equivalences, see Corollary
6.2.9
(iii).
∎

Lemma 6.2.17
.

For
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
and
V\in C_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we have a functorial isomorphism
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}(V^{\sharp})\simeq({\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,V)^{\sharp}
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}(V^{\sharp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{ECircledAstGG})}}{{=}}\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,{\mathpzc F}_{d}(({\mathpzc G}_{d}V)^{\circledast})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{EGGIInd})}}{{\simeq}}\ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}\,{\mathrm{Res}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{d}{\mathpzc F}_{d}(({\mathpzc G}_{d}V)^{\circledast})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Corollary~\ref{CEquivFG}(ii)}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}\,{\mathrm{Res}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda\delta}\,({\mathpzc G}_{d}V)^{\circledast}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma~\ref{LDualInd}(i)}}}{{\simeq}}\

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}\Big(\big({\mathrm{Res}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda\delta}\,({\mathpzc G}_{d}V)\big)^{\circledast}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Corollary~\ref{CEquivFG}(ii)}}}{{\simeq}}

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}\Big(\big({\mathpzc G}_{\lambda}{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}\,{\mathrm{Res}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda\delta}\,({\mathpzc G}_{d}V)\big)^{\circledast}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{EGGIInd})}}{{\simeq}}\ \ \ \ \ \,

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}({\mathpzc G}_{\lambda}\,{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,V)^{\circledast}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{ECircledAstGGPar})}}{{=}}\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle({\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,V)^{\sharp}

as required.
∎

Lemma 6.2.18
.

For
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
,
V_{1}\in C_{\lambda_{1}}\!\operatorname{-mod},\dots,V_{n}\in C_{\lambda_{n}}\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we have functorial isomorphisms

{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}\big(V_{1}^{\sharp}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{n}^{\sharp}\big)\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}\big((V_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{n})^{\sharp}\big)\simeq\big({\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}(V_{n}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{1})\big)^{\sharp}.

Proof.

The first isomorphism comes from (
6.2.15
).
Now,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}\big((V_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{n})^{\sharp}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{EGGIInd}),(\ref{ECircledAstGGPar})}}{{=}}\ \ \

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda}{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}\Big(\big({\mathpzc G}_{\lambda}(V_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{n})\big)^{\circledast}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Corollary~\ref{CEquivFG}(ii)}}}{{\simeq}}\

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda\delta}\,\Big(\big({\mathpzc G}_{\lambda}(V_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{n})\big)^{\circledast}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{EFGFunctorsLaOuter})}}{{\simeq}}\ \ \ \,\,\ \ \,

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda\delta}\,\Big(\big({\mathpzc G}_{\lambda_{1}}(V_{1})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda_{n}}(V_{n})\big)^{\circledast}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{EBoxCircledAst})}}{{\simeq}}\ \ \ \ \ \,\,\,

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda\delta}\,\big({\mathpzc G}_{\lambda_{1}}(V_{1})^{\circledast}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda_{n}}(V_{n})^{\circledast}\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma~\ref{LDualInd}(ii)}}}{{\simeq}}\ \,

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,\Big(\big({\mathrm{Ind}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda_{n}}(V_{n})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda_{1}}(V_{1})\big)^{\circledast}\Big)

\displaystyle{\simeq}\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,\Big(\big({\mathrm{Ind}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}(V_{n}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{1})\big)^{\circledast}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Corollary~\ref{CEquivFG}(ii)}}}{{\simeq}}\

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,\Big(\big({\mathpzc G}_{d}{\mathpzc F}_{d}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{d\delta}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}(V_{n}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{1})\big)^{\circledast}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{EGGIInd})}}{{\simeq}}\ \ \ \ \ \ \,

\displaystyle{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,\Big(\big({\mathpzc G}_{d}({\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}\,V_{n}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{1})\big)^{\circledast}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{ECircledAstGG})}}{{=}}\ \ \ \ \ \,\,

\displaystyle({\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}\,V_{n}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes V_{1})^{\sharp},

as required.
∎

Lemma 6.2.19
.

Each isomorphism class of irreducible graded
C_{d}
-supermodules contains an irreducible graded supermodule
L
such that
L^{\sharp}\simeq\Uppi^{\varepsilon}L
for some
{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{Z}/2
(depending on
L
). In particular, for every irreducible graded
C_{d}
-supermodule
V
, we have
V^{\sharp}\cong V
.

Proof.

Let
L
be an irreducible graded
C_{d}
-supermodule.
By Corollary
6.2.9
,
{\mathpzc G}_{d}L
is an irreducible graded
\hat{C}_{d}
-supermodule. By Lemma
3.3.19
, we may assume that
({\mathpzc G}_{d}L)^{\circledast}\simeq\Uppi^{\varepsilon}{\mathpzc G}_{d}L
for some
{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{Z}/2
. So

L^{\sharp}={\mathpzc F}_{d}\big(({\mathpzc G}_{d}L)^{\circledast}\big)\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}(\Uppi^{\varepsilon}{\mathpzc G}_{d}(L))\simeq\Uppi^{\varepsilon}{\mathpzc F}_{d}{\mathpzc G}_{d}(L)\simeq\Uppi^{\varepsilon}L,

as required.
∎

Lemma 6.2.20
.

We have that

(\hat{L}_{j})^{\circledast}\simeq\Uppi\hat{L}_{j}
and
(L_{j})^{\sharp}\simeq\Uppi L_{j}
.

Proof.

We have
L_{j}={\mathpzc F}_{1}(\hat{L}_{j})={f}_{1}\hat{L}_{j}
. In particular, recalling (
3.2.7
), we have that
\dim_{q,\pi}{f}^{j}\hat{L}_{j}=q^{2j+1-2\ell}\pi^{j}
. Since
{f}^{j}=1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{j}}
, it now follows from Lemma
3.3.43
that

\dim_{q,\pi}1_{\hat{\text{\boldmath$l$}}^{j}}\hat{L}_{j}=\pi^{j}(q^{2}+q^{-2})^{\ell-1-j}(\pi q+q^{-1}).

We now deduce from Lemma
3.3.19
that
(\hat{L}_{j})^{\circledast}\simeq\Uppi\hat{L}_{j}
, which also implies that
(L_{j})^{\sharp}\simeq\Uppi L_{j}
.
∎

Lemma 6.2.21
.

We have that
\hat{M}_{d,j}^{\circledast}\simeq\Uppi^{d}\hat{M}_{d,j}
and
M_{d,j}^{\sharp}\simeq\Uppi M_{d,j}
. In particular, the socle and the head of
\hat{M}_{d,j}
(resp.
M_{d,j}
) are (not necessarily evenly) isomorphic to each other.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.2.20
, we have
(\hat{L}_{j})^{\circledast}\simeq\Uppi\hat{L}_{j}
. So, using Lemma
3.3.34
(ii), we get

\hat{M}_{d,j}^{\circledast}=({\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d})^{\circledast}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}(\Uppi\hat{L}_{j})^{\boxtimes d}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\Uppi^{d}\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}\simeq\Uppi^{d}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\delta^{d}}^{d\delta}\hat{L}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}=\Uppi^{d}\hat{M}_{d,j}.

Now, by (
6.2.13
) and Lemma
6.1.4
, we have

M_{d,j}^{\sharp}={\mathpzc F}_{d}\big(({\mathpzc G}_{d}\,M_{d,j})^{\circledast}\big)\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}\big((\hat{M}_{d,j})^{\circledast}\big)\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}(\Uppi^{d}\hat{M}_{d,j})\simeq\Uppi^{d}{\mathpzc F}_{d}(\hat{M}_{d,j})=\Uppi^{d}M_{d,j}.

The final statement now follows using Lemma
6.2.19
.
∎

Corollary 6.2.22
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
\hat{M}_{\lambda,j}^{\circledast}\cong\hat{M}_{\lambda,j}
and
M_{\lambda,j}^{\sharp}\cong M_{\lambda,j}
. In particular, the socle and the head of
\hat{M}_{\lambda,j}
(resp.
M_{\lambda,j}
) are (not necessarily evenly) isomorphic to each other.

Chapter 7
Imaginary Howe duality

As in Section
6
, we again fix
j\in J
. We can consider the imaginary tensor space
M_{d,j}
as a (faithful) graded supermodule over
imaginary graded Schur superalgebra

{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=C_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})
. In this chapter we will prove that this graded supermodule is projective and its endomorphism algebra is
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, see Theorem
7.1.6
. But this ‘imaginary Schur-Weyl duality’ is not sufficient to describe
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
up to Morita equivalence since in general
M_{d,j}
is not a projective generator for
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
. We will eventually construct the desired projective generator as a direct sum
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
of ‘imaginary divided powers’ modules (for
n\geq d
), and the endomorphism algebra of
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)
will turn out to be the classical Schur algebra
S(n,d)
. This will lead to a Morita equivalence between the imaginary and the classical Schur algebras.

7.1.
Imaginary Schur superalgebra

7.1a.
Imaginary Schur superalgebra
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
and its parabolic analogues
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}

We define the
imaginary graded Schur superalgebra

(7.1.1)

{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=C_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j}).

In particular,
M_{d,j}
can be considered as a faithful graded supermodule over
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
.
(Occasionally, we will also use the graded superalgebra

\hat{C}_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})

to which we do not assign any special name.)

More generally, for
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
, we set

{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=C_{\lambda}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{\lambda}}(M_{\lambda,j})\cong{\bar{C}}_{\lambda_{1},j}\otimes\dots\otimes{\bar{C}}_{\lambda_{n},j}.

In particular,
M_{\lambda,j}
can be considered as a faithful graded supermodule over
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
. Identifying

{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}={\bar{C}}_{\lambda_{1},j}\otimes\dots\otimes{\bar{C}}_{\lambda_{n},j}
, we have

M_{\lambda,j}\cong M_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{\lambda_{n},j}
as graded
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
-supermodules.

7.1b.
Imaginary tensor space
M_{d,j}
as a module over
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}

As in §
6.1c
, we can now consider

M_{d,j}
as a graded
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bisupermodule and, more generally,

M_{\lambda,j}
as a graded
({\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda})
-bisupermodule.
By functoriality of
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
, we have that
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{\lambda,j}
becomes a graded
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda})
-bisupermodule. Similarly, taking into account Lemma
6.1.15
(ii),
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{d,j}
becomes a graded
({\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bisupermodule.

Lemma 7.1.2
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then:

(i)

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{\lambda,j}\simeq M_{d,j}
as graded
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda})
-bisupermodules, where the right action of
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
on
M_{d,j}
is via restriction from
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.

(ii)

{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{d,j}\simeq M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
as graded
({\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bisupermodules.

Proof.

Follows immediately from Lemma
6.1.15
.
∎

Remark 7.1.3
.

Lemma
7.1.2
shows that the
C_{\lambda}
-action on
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{d,j}
factors through the quotient
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
and the
C_{d}
-action on
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{\lambda,j}
factors through the quotient
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
.
In Corollary
8.1.22
we will prove a stronger result that
the functor
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}
sends
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-modules to
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
-modules and the functor

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
sends
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
-modules to
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-modules.

Recall the graded superalgebra embedding
C_{\lambda}\to{f}_{\lambda}C_{d}{f}_{\lambda}

from (
4.2.13
).

Lemma 7.1.4
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Denote
\bar{f}_{\lambda}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}_{\lambda}+{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})\in{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
. The embedding
C_{\lambda}\to{f}_{\lambda}C_{d}{f}_{\lambda}
induces a graded superalgebra embedding
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\to\bar{f}_{\lambda}{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}_{\lambda}
.

Proof.

The surjection
C_{d}\to{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
with kernel
{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})
induces a surjection
{f}_{\lambda}C_{d}{f}_{\lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}\bar{f}_{\lambda}{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}_{\lambda}
with kernel
{f}_{\lambda}{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j}){f}_{\lambda}
,
so the composition
C_{\lambda}\to{f}_{\lambda}C_{d}{f}_{\lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}\bar{f}_{\lambda}{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}_{\lambda}
has kernel
C_{\lambda}\cap{f}_{\lambda}{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j}){f}_{\lambda}
. But by Lemma
6.1.15
(ii), the graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule
{f}_{\lambda}M_{d,j}
is a direct sum of copies of
M_{\lambda,j}
, so
C_{\lambda}\cap{f}_{\lambda}{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j}){f}_{\lambda}={\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{\lambda}}(M_{\lambda,j})
.
∎

We make use of the following
Schubert’s Criterion
(see
[
4
, §3.2]
for attributions):

Lemma 7.1.5
.

Let
A
be a graded
\Bbbk
-superalgebra and
P\in A\!\operatorname{-mod}
be projective. Let
Z\subseteq P
be a graded subsupermodule such that every homomorphism in
\operatorname{Hom}_{A}(P,P/Z)
annihilates
Z
. Then
P/Z
is a projective graded
(A/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{A}(P/Z))
-supermodule.

Proof.

The proof in
[
4
, Lemma 3.2a]
goes through in the setting of finitely generated graded supermodules.
∎

Theorem 7.1.6
.

We have that
M_{d,j}
is a projective graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodule and there is an isomorphism (of graded superalgebras)
\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(M_{d,j})\cong\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.

Proof.

The second statement comes from Theorem
6.1.9
.

For the first statement we will apply Lemma
7.1.5
. Let
P\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=C_{d}{f}^{j^{d}}
. By (
6.1.3
), we have
{f}^{j^{d}}v_{d,j}=v_{d,j}
, so, as
v_{d,j}
generates
M_{d,j}
, there is a surjective homomorphism

\pi\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}P{\twoheadrightarrow}M_{d,j},\ {f}^{j^{d}}\mapsto v_{d,j}.

Let
Z=\operatorname{Ker}\pi
. To verify the assumptions of Lemma
7.1.5
, it suffices to prove that any
F\in\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(P,M_{d,j})
can be written as
F={\varphi}\circ\pi
for some
{\varphi}\in\operatorname{End}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})
. Since
P
is generated by
{f}^{j^{d}}
, it suffices to show that
F({f}^{j^{d}})={\varphi}(\pi({f}^{j^{d}}))={\varphi}(v_{d,j})
for some
{\varphi}\in\operatorname{End}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})
. We have that
F({f}^{j^{d}})\in{f}^{j^{d}}M_{d,j}
, so by Lemma
6.1.6
(i), we can write

F({f}^{j^{d}})=\textstyle\sum_{w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}}c_{w}\dot{w}v_{d,j}\qquad(c_{w}\in\Bbbk).

In view of (
6.1.8
),
we can now take
{\varphi}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\sum_{w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}}(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}c_{w}{\varphi}_{w}
.
∎

Corollary 7.1.7
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
M_{\lambda,j}
is a projective graded
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
-supermodule and
\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}}(M_{d,j})\cong\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}

Recall that by Lemma
6.1.4
, we have
\hat{M}_{d,j}\simeq{\mathpzc G}_{d}(M_{d,j})=\hat{C}_{d}{f}_{d}\otimes_{C_{d}}M_{d,j}
, where
M_{d,j}
is a graded
(C_{d},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bisupermodule. So we have a right
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-module structure on
\hat{M}_{d,j}
or a homomorphism of algebras

(7.1.8)

\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}\to\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}=\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})}(\hat{M}_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}

which maps
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
to the unique endomorphism
\alpha_{w}
with

\alpha_{w}({f}_{d}\otimes v_{d,j})=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}{f}_{d}\otimes\dot{w}v_{d,j}.

Corollary 7.1.9
.

The homomorphism (
7.1.8
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This follows from
Theorem
6.1.9
and
the fact that
{\mathpzc G}_{d}
is an equivalence by Corollary
6.2.9
(ii).
∎

Theorem 7.1.10
.

We have that
\hat{M}_{d,j}
is a projective graded
\hat{C}_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})
-supermodule and
\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})}(\hat{M}_{d,j})\cong\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
7.1.6
, using Corollary
7.1.9
instead of Theorem
6.1.9
, and
Lemma
6.1.6
(ii) instead of Lemma
6.1.6
(i).
∎

7.2.
Imaginary symmetric and divided powers

7.2a.
The modules
S_{d,j}
and
\Gamma_{d,j}

Recall from (
2.1.21
) the elements
{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
and
{\mathtt{y}}_{d}
of
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
. Define the
imaginary symmetric
and
imaginary divided powers
as the following graded
C_{d}
-supermodules:

(7.2.1)

\displaystyle S_{d,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=M_{d,j}/\operatorname{span}_{\Bbbk}\{mg-m\mid g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d},\,m\in M_{d,j}\},

(7.2.2)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{d,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{m\in M_{d,j}\mid mg=m\ \text{for all}\ g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}.

An important role will also be played by the graded
C_{d}
-supermodules

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}\quad\text{and}\quad M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d},

the first of which referred to as the
imaginary exterior power
. Note that
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}\neq 0\neq M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
for example by Theorem
6.1.9
, and
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
is a graded subsupermodule of
\Gamma_{d,j}
.

The graded
C_{d}
-supermodules
S_{d,j},\,\Gamma_{d,j},\,M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d},\,M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
factor through the quotient
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
of
C_{d}
to yield graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodules.

To prove Lemma
7.2.9
below, we will also need the following
\hat{C}_{d}
-versions of
S_{d,j},\Gamma_{d,j}

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{d,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\hat{M}_{d,j}/\operatorname{span}_{\Bbbk}\{mg-m\mid g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d},\,m\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\},

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}_{d,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{m\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\mid mg=m\ \text{for all}\ g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\},

as well as the
\hat{C}_{d}
-modules
\hat{M}_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}
and
\hat{M}_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
.

Lemma 7.2.3
.

We have
S_{d,j}\simeq M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}

as graded left
C_{d}
-supermodules and

\hat{S}_{d,j}\simeq\hat{M}_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}

as graded left
\hat{C}_{d}
-supermodules.

Proof.

By definition,

M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\simeq(M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}})/\operatorname{span}_{\Bbbk}\{mg\otimes 1-m\otimes 1\mid g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d},\,m\in M_{d,j}\}.

Identifying
M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}=M_{d,j}
, this immediately gives the first claim. The second claim is proved similarly.
∎

Lemma 7.2.4
.

We have that
\{v_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}\}
is a basis of the
\Bbbk
-module
{f}^{j^{d}}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}
, and
\{v_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}\}
is a basis of the
\Bbbk
-module
{f}^{j^{d}}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
.

Proof.

By (
6.1.10
) and Lemma
6.1.6
(i), we have that
{f}^{j^{d}}M_{d,j}
is a free right
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-module with basis
v_{d,j}
. This implies the lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.2.5
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
.

(i)

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}
and
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
are irreducible
C_{d}
-modules, not isomorphic to each other if
\operatorname{char}\mathbb{F}\neq 2
.

(ii)

\hat{M}_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}
and
\hat{M}_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
are irreducible
\hat{C}_{d}
-modules, not isomorphic to each other if
\operatorname{char}\mathbb{F}\neq 2
.

Proof.

(i) By Theorem
7.1.6
,
M_{d,j}
is a projective graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodule with
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}=\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(M_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}
. Moreover, by Lemma
6.2.21
, every composition factor of the socle of
M_{d,j}
appears in its head. Also, left ideals
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
and
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}
are irreducible
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-modules, not isomorphic to each other if
\operatorname{char}\mathbb{F}\neq 2
.
Now the claim follows from Lemmas
2.2.29
,
2.2.30
.

(ii) The proof is similar to (i) using Theorem
7.1.10
instead of Theorem
7.1.6
.
∎

Lemma 7.2.6
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
.

(i)

S_{d,j}
has simple head isomorphic to
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
, and no other composition factor of
S_{d,j}
appears in the head of
M_{d,j}
.

(ii)

\hat{S}_{d,j}
has simple head isomorphic to
\hat{M}_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
, and no other composition factor of
\hat{S}_{d,j}
appears in the head of
\hat{M}_{d,j}
.

Proof.

(i) Let
{\mathpzc{f}}
,
{\mathpzc{g}}
and
{\mathpzc{q}}
be the functors defined in §
2.2e
, taking the projective module
P
to be the
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-module
M_{d,j}
, so that
H=\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, see Theorem
7.1.6
. By Lemma
7.2.3
, we have
S_{d,j}\simeq{\mathpzc{g}}(\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}})
. On the other hand, by Lemma
6.2.21
, every composition factor of the socle of
M_{d,j}
appears in its head, so by Lemma
2.2.30
, we have

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}\simeq{\mathpzc{q}}\circ{\mathpzc{g}}(\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}{\mathtt{x}}_{d})\simeq{\mathpzc{q}}\circ{\mathpzc{g}}(\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}})\simeq{\mathpzc{q}}(S_{d,j}).

By Lemma
7.2.5
(i), the graded supermodule
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
is irreducible. We deduce that
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
appears in the head of
S_{d,j}
and no other composition factor of
S_{d,j}
appear in the head of
M_{d,j}
. Since
S_{d,j}
is a quotient of
M_{d,j}
, this means that
S_{d,j}
has simple head.

(ii) is proved similarly using Theorem
7.1.10
instead of Theorem
7.1.6
, and Lemma
7.2.5
(ii) instead of Lemma
7.2.5
(i).
∎

Lemma 7.2.7
.

We have
{\mathpzc G}_{d}S_{d,j}\simeq\hat{S}_{d,j}
and
{\mathpzc F}_{d}\hat{\Gamma}_{d,j}\simeq\Gamma_{d,j}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.1.4
, we identify
{\mathpzc G}_{d}M_{d,j}=\hat{M}_{d,j}=\hat{C}_{d}{f}_{d}\otimes_{C_{d}}M_{d,j}
.

For the first isomorphism, since
S_{d,j}
is a quotient of
M_{d,j}
there is a
short exact sequence
0\to K\to M_{d,j}\to S_{d,j}\to 0.

Applying the exact functor
{\mathpzc G}_{d}
we get the exact sequence

0\to{\mathpzc G}_{d}K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\varphi}}}{{\to}}{\mathpzc G}_{d}M_{d,j}\to{\mathpzc G}_{d}S_{d,j}\to 0.

We need to prove that
\operatorname{Im}{\varphi}=\hat{K}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\operatorname{span}_{\Bbbk}\{mg-m\mid g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d},\,m\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\}
.
Note that
\operatorname{Im}{\varphi}
consists of all elements of the form
\sum_{k}r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes(m_{k}-m_{k}g_{k})
with
r_{k}\in\hat{C}_{d}
,
m_{k}\in M_{d,j}
and
g_{k}\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
. But

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum_{k}r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes(m_{k}-m_{k}g_{k})=\sum_{k}\big(r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes m_{k}-(r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes m_{k})g_{k}\big)\subseteq\hat{K}.

On the other hand,
\hat{K}
is a span of the elements of the form

\textstyle\sum_{k}r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes m_{k}-\Big(\sum_{k}r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes m_{k}\Big)g=\sum_{k}r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes(m_{k}-m_{k}g)\subseteq\operatorname{Im}{\varphi}.

For the second isomorphism, applying
{\mathpzc F}_{d}
to the embedding
\hat{\Gamma}_{d}\to\hat{M}_{d,j}
we get the embedding
{\mathpzc F}_{d}\hat{\Gamma}_{d}\to{\mathpzc F}_{d}\hat{M}_{d,j}=M_{d,j}
, whose image consists of all elements of the form
\sum_{k}{f}_{d}r_{k}{f}_{d}m_{k}
such that
\sum_{k}r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes m_{k}g=\sum_{k}r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes m_{k}
for all
g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, which is easily seen to be
\Gamma_{d,j}
.
∎

7.2b.
Duality between
\Gamma_{d,j}
and
S_{d,j}

Our next goal is to prove that
\Gamma_{d,j}\cong S_{d,j}^{\sharp}
when
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. We first establish the
\hat{C}_{d}
-version of this:

Lemma 7.2.8
.

If
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
then
\hat{\Gamma}_{d,j}\cong\hat{S}_{d,j}^{\circledast}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.2.21
, there exists a
\hat{C}_{d}
-isomorphism

{\varphi}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\hat{M}_{d,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Uppi^{d}\hat{M}_{d,j}^{\circledast}.

For
\theta\in\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})
, we define
\kappa(\theta)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\varphi}\circ\theta\circ{\varphi}^{-1}\in\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j}^{\circledast})
.
This defines an algebra isomorphism

\kappa\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}\to\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j}^{\circledast})^{\mathrm{sop}}.

Note by Corollary
7.1.9
that
\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}\cong\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
is concentrated in parity
{\bar{0}}
, so, recalling (
3.3.16
), we also have an algebra isomorphism

\circledast\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j}^{\circledast})^{\mathrm{sop}},\ \theta\mapsto\theta^{\circledast}.

Using the standard right
\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}
-module structure on
\hat{M}_{d,j}
and the standard right
\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j}^{\circledast})^{\mathrm{sop}}
-module structure on
\hat{M}_{d,j}^{\circledast}
, we can write:

v\cdot\kappa^{-1}(\theta^{\circledast})={\varphi}^{-1}({\varphi}(v)\cdot\theta^{\circledast})\qquad(v\in\hat{M}_{d,j},\,\theta\in\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}).

We have an algebra isomorphism

\sigma\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\kappa^{-1}\circ\circledast\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{End}_{\hat{C}_{d}}(\hat{M}_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}.

We consider a non-degenerate bilinear form
(\cdot,\cdot)
on
\hat{M}_{d,j}
defined from

(v,w)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\varphi}(v)(w)
. For any
x\in\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
and
v,w\in\hat{M}_{d,j}
, we have

\displaystyle(v\cdot\sigma(x),w)

\displaystyle=(v\cdot\kappa^{-1}(x^{\circledast}),w)

\displaystyle=({\varphi}^{-1}({\varphi}(v)\cdot x^{\circledast}),w)

\displaystyle=({\varphi}(v)\cdot x^{\circledast})(w)

\displaystyle=x^{\circledast}({\varphi}(v))(w)

\displaystyle={\varphi}(v)(x(w))

\displaystyle={\varphi}(v)(w\cdot x)

\displaystyle=(v,w\cdot x).

Let

\textstyle\operatorname{Aug}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}\to\mathbb{F},\ \sum_{g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}}c_{g}g\mapsto\sum_{g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}}c_{g}

be the augmentation homomorphism.
By definition,
\hat{S}_{d,j}=\hat{M}_{d,j}/K
where
K\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\operatorname{span}_{\Bbbk}\{w(x-\operatorname{Aug}(x))\mid w\in\hat{M}_{d,j},\,x\in\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}
. Now,

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{d,j}^{\circledast}

\displaystyle\cong\{v\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\mid(v,k)=0\ \text{for all $k\in K$}\}

\displaystyle\cong\{v\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\mid\big(v,w(x-\operatorname{Aug}(x))\big)=0\ \text{for all $w\in\hat{M}_{d,j}$ and $x\in\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}$}\}

\displaystyle=\{v\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\mid(v(\sigma(x)-\operatorname{Aug}(x)),w)=0\ \text{for all $w\in\hat{M}_{d,j}$ and $x\in\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}$}\}

\displaystyle=\{v\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\mid v(\sigma(x)-\operatorname{Aug}(x))=0\ \text{for all $x\in\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}$}\}

\displaystyle=\{v\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\mid vx=\operatorname{Aug}(\sigma^{-1}(x))v\ \text{for all $x\in\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}$}\}.

We have an algebra homomorphism
\operatorname{Aug}\circ\sigma^{-1}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbb{F}
, so either
\operatorname{Aug}\circ\sigma^{-1}
is trivial or a sign homomorphism. We need to make sure it is actually the trivial homomorphism. In particular, we may assume that
p\neq 2
.

If
\operatorname{Aug}\circ\sigma^{-1}
is a sign homomorphism then we have proved that
\hat{S}_{d,j}^{\circledast}\cong\{v\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\mid wg=\mathtt{sgn}(g)w\supseteq\hat{M}_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}
. Then by Lemmas
7.2.5
and
3.3.19
, the irreducible module
(\hat{M}_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d})^{\circledast}\cong\hat{M}_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}
appears in the head of
\hat{S}_{d,j}
. But by Lemma
7.2.6
(ii), the head of
\hat{S}_{d,j}
is isomorphic to
\hat{M}_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
. It remains to apply Lemma
7.2.5
(ii).
∎

Lemma 7.2.9
.

If
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
then
\Gamma_{d,j}\cong S_{d,j}^{\sharp}
.

Proof.

We have

S_{d,j}^{\sharp}={\mathpzc F}_{d}(({\mathpzc G}_{d}S_{d,j})^{\circledast})\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}(\hat{S}_{d,j}^{\circledast})\cong{\mathpzc F}_{d}(\hat{\Gamma}_{d,j})\simeq\Gamma_{d,j},

where we have used (
6.2.13
) and Lemmas
7.2.7
,
7.2.8
.
∎

Corollary 7.2.10
.

If
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
then
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}\subseteq\Gamma_{d,j}
is the simple socle of
\Gamma_{d,j}
and no composition factor of
\Gamma_{d,j}/M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{d}
is isomorphic to a submodule of
M_{d,j}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemmas
7.2.6
,
7.2.9
and
6.2.19
.
∎

7.2c.
Parabolic versions
S_{\lambda,j}
and
\Gamma_{\lambda,j}

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)

and recall the notation (
2.1.21
).
We consider the following graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodules:

\displaystyle S_{\lambda,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=M_{\lambda,j}/\operatorname{span}_{\Bbbk}\{mg-m\mid g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda},\,m\in M_{\lambda,j}\}\simeq S_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes S_{\lambda_{n},j},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\lambda,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{m\in M_{\lambda,j}\mid mg=m\ \text{for all}\ g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\}\simeq\Gamma_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\Gamma_{\lambda_{n},j}.

We will also use the graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodules

M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}\simeq M_{\lambda_{1},j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{\lambda_{n},j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda_{n}}\quad\text{and}\quad M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\simeq M_{\lambda_{1},j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{\lambda_{n},j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda_{n}}.

Note that
