M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\subseteq\Gamma_{\lambda,j}
.

The graded
C_{d}
-supermodules
S_{\lambda,j},\,\Gamma_{\lambda,j},\,M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda},\,M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
factor through the quotient
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
of
C_{\lambda}
to yield graded
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
-supermodules.

Since
M_{\lambda,j}\simeq M_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes M_{\lambda_{n},j}
,
S_{\lambda,j}\simeq S_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes S_{\lambda_{n},j}
and

\Gamma_{\lambda,j}\simeq\Gamma_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes\Gamma_{\lambda_{n},j}
, the following three lemma follow from Lemmas
7.2.10
,
7.2.3
and
7.2.9
, respectively.

Lemma 7.2.11
.

If
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\subseteq\Gamma_{\lambda,j}
is the simple socle of
\Gamma_{\lambda,j}
and no composition factor of
\Gamma_{\lambda,j}/M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
is isomorphic to a submodule of
M_{\lambda,j}
.

Lemma 7.2.12
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
S_{\lambda,j}\simeq M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}

as graded left
C_{\lambda}
-supermodules.

Lemma 7.2.13
.

If
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
then
\Gamma_{\lambda,j}\simeq S_{\lambda,j}^{\sharp}
for any
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
.

Lemma 7.2.14
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. We have:

(i)

{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}S_{d,j}\simeq S_{\lambda,j}
.

(ii)

If
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
then
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\Gamma_{d,j}\simeq\Gamma_{\lambda,j}
.

Proof.

(i) By Lemmas
7.2.3
and
7.2.12
, we have
S_{d,j}\simeq M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}
and
S_{\lambda,j}\simeq M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}
. Using Corollary
6.1.16
(ii), we now get

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}S_{d,j}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}(M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}})

\displaystyle\simeq M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}{\mathrm{Res}}^{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}

\displaystyle\simeq M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\simeq S_{\lambda,j}.

(ii) follows from (i) on dualizing using Lemmas
7.2.9
,
7.2.13
and
6.2.17
.
∎

7.2d.
Parabolic versions
S_{j}^{\lambda}
and
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}

We consider the following graded
C_{d}
-supermodules:

\displaystyle S_{j}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=M_{d,j}/\operatorname{span}_{\Bbbk}\{mg-m\mid g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda},\,m\in M_{d,j}\},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{m\in M_{d,j}\mid mg=m\ \text{for all}\ g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\}.

We will also use the graded
C_{d}
-supermodules

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}\quad\text{and}\quad M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}.

Note that

(7.2.15)

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\subseteq\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j}.

The graded
C_{d}
-supermodules
S_{j}^{\lambda},\,\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda},\,M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda},\,M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
factor through the quotient
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
of
C_{d}
to yield graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodules.

Lemma 7.2.16
.

For
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
, we have:

(i)

S_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,S_{\lambda,j}
;

(ii)

\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,\Gamma_{\lambda,j}
;

(iii)

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}(M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda})
;

(iv)

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}(M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda})
.

Proof.

(i), (iii), (iv) We identify
M_{d,j}
and
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,M_{\lambda,j}
as graded
(C_{d},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda})
-bisupermodules
via the isomorphism of Lemma
6.1.15
(i). Then the quotient
S_{j}^{\lambda}
of
M_{d,j}
is identified with the quotient
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,S_{\lambda,j}
of
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,M_{\lambda,j}
proving (i), the submodule
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}\subseteq M_{d,j}
is identified with the submodule
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}(M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda})\subseteq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,M_{\lambda,j}
proving (iii), and the submodule
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\subseteq M_{d,j}
is identified with the submodule
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}(M_{\lambda,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda})\subseteq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,M_{\lambda,j}
, proving (iv).

(ii)
Let

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}_{\lambda,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{m\in\hat{M}_{\lambda,j}\mid mg=m\ \text{for all}\ g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\}

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}_{j}^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{m\in\hat{M}_{d,j}\mid mg=m\ \text{for all}\ g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\}.

Note that

(7.2.17)

{\mathpzc F}_{d}\hat{\Gamma}_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}.

Indeed, use Lemma
6.1.4
to identify
\hat{M}_{d,j}=\hat{C}_{d}f_{d}\otimes_{C_{d}}M_{d,j}
and
{\mathpzc F}_{d}\hat{M}_{d,j}=M_{d,j}
. Then applying
{\mathpzc F}_{d}
to the embedding
\hat{\Gamma}^{\lambda}\to\hat{M}_{d,j}
, we get the embedding
\alpha\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathpzc F}_{d}\hat{\Gamma}^{\lambda}\to{\mathpzc F}_{d}\hat{M}_{d,j}=M_{d,j}
, whose image consists of all elements of the form
\sum_{k}{f}_{d}r_{k}{f}_{d}m_{k}
, with
r_{k}\in\hat{C}_{d}
,
m_{k}\in M_{d,j}
, such that in
\hat{M}_{d,j}
we have

\sum_{k}r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes m_{k}g=\sum_{k}r_{k}{f}_{d}\otimes m_{k}
for all
g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
. So
\operatorname{Im}\alpha=\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
.

Note next that

(7.2.18)

\hat{\Gamma}_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\lambda\delta}^{d\delta}\hat{\Gamma}_{\lambda,j}.

Indeed, use Lemma
4.1.9
to identify
\hat{M}_{d,j}={\mathrm{Ind}}_{\lambda\delta}^{d\delta}\hat{M}_{\lambda,j}
.
By Lemma
4.1.6
(ii), we then have
\hat{M}_{d,j}=\bigoplus_{w\in{\mathscr{D}}_{pd}^{p\lambda}}\psi_{w}1_{\lambda\delta}\otimes\hat{M}_{\lambda,j}
with all the natural maps
M_{\lambda,j}\to\psi_{w}1_{\lambda\delta}\otimes\hat{M}_{\lambda,j},\ m\mapsto\psi_{w}1_{\lambda\delta}\otimes m
being isomorphisms. Then the submodule
\hat{\Gamma}_{j}^{\lambda}
of
\hat{M}_{d,j}
is identified with the submodule

\bigoplus_{w\in{\mathscr{D}}_{pd}^{p\lambda}}\psi_{w}1_{\lambda\delta}\otimes\hat{\Gamma}_{\lambda,j}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\lambda\delta}^{d\delta}\hat{\Gamma}_{\lambda,j}\subseteq{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\lambda\delta}^{d\delta}\hat{M}_{\lambda,j}.

Now,

\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}\hat{\Gamma}_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\lambda\delta}^{d\delta}\,\hat{\Gamma}_{\lambda,j}\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\lambda\delta}^{d\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda}{\mathpzc F}_{\lambda}\hat{\Gamma}_{\lambda,j}\simeq{\mathpzc F}_{d}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\lambda\delta}^{d\delta}\,{\mathpzc G}_{\lambda}\Gamma_{\lambda,j}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,\Gamma_{\lambda,j},

where we have used (
7.2.17
) for the first isomorphism, (
7.2.18
) for the second isomorphism, Corollary
6.2.9
(ii) for the third isomorphism, Lemma
7.2.7
for the fourth isomorphism, and (
4.2.25
) for the last isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 7.2.19
.

For
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
, we have

S_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\otimes{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}
.

Proof.

We have

M_{d,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\otimes{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}(M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}})\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}S_{\lambda,j}\simeq S_{j}^{\lambda}.

where we have used Corollary
6.1.16
(i) for the first isomorphism, Lemma
7.2.12
for the second isomorphism, and Lemma
7.2.16
for the third isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 7.2.20
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
(S_{j}^{\lambda})^{\sharp}\simeq\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle(S_{j}^{\lambda})^{\sharp}\simeq({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}S_{\lambda,j})^{\sharp}\simeq({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}^{d}S_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}^{\sharp})\simeq({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}^{d}\Gamma_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}},j})\simeq\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}},

where we have used Lemma
7.2.16
(i), Lemma
6.2.18
for the second isomorphism, Lemma
7.2.13
for the third isomorphism and Lemma
7.2.16
(ii) for the fourth isomorphism.
∎

Lemma 7.2.21
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then no composition factor of
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}/M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
is isomorphic to a submodule of
M_{d,j}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathpzc{f}},{\mathpzc{g}}
and
{\mathpzc{q}}
be the functors defined in §
2.2e
corresponding to
P
being the projective
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}},j}
-module
M_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}},j}
, see Corollary
7.1.7
. Note that the
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-module
{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}}^{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}}
is isomorphic to the left ideal
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}
of
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
. By Corollary
6.2.22
and Lemma
2.2.30
, we have
{\mathpzc{q}}\circ{\mathpzc{g}}({\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}}^{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}})\simeq M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}
. But by Lemma
7.2.19
, we have

{\mathpzc{g}}({\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}}^{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}})=M_{d,j}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}}^{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}}\simeq S_{j}^{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}.

So there is a short exact sequence of the form
0\to V\to S_{j}^{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}\to W\to 0
where
W\simeq M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}
and
V
has no composition actors in common with the head of
M_{d,j}
. Dualizing and using Lemma
7.2.20
, we have a short exact sequence
0\to W^{\sharp}\to\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}\to V^{\sharp}\to 0
. Moreover, by Lemma
6.2.19
,
V^{\sharp}
has the same composition factors as
V
, so
V^{\sharp}
has no composition factors in common with the head of
M_{d,j}
. Finally,

\displaystyle W^{\sharp}\ \simeq\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle(M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}})^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma}~\ref{LUpperInd}\text{(iv)}}}{{\simeq}}\ \ \

\displaystyle\big({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}^{d}(M_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}},j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}})\big)^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\simeq\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \,

\displaystyle\big({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}^{d}(M_{\lambda_{n},j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda_{n}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{\lambda_{1},j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda_{1}})\big)^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma}~\ref{LSharpIndComm}}}{{\simeq}}\ \ \ \ \ \,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\big((M_{\lambda_{1},j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda_{1}})^{\sharp}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes(M_{\lambda_{n},j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda_{n}})^{\sharp}\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemmas~\ref{Lttx}(i),\ref{LGGIrrSelfD}}}}{{\cong}}\

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\big(M_{\lambda_{1},j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{\lambda_{n},j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda_{n}}\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma}~\ref{LUpperInd}\text{(iv)}}}{{\simeq}}\ \ \ \,

\displaystyle M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda},

which implies the result using the Jordan-Hölder theorem.
∎

7.3.
Classical Schur algebras as endomorphism algebras

Recall the classical Schur algebra
S(n,d)=\operatorname{End}_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}\big(\textstyle\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M^{\lambda}\big)
from §
3.1b

with the basis
\{\xi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}\mid\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(n,d),\,g\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\}
from Lemma
3.1.8
. The basis elements
\xi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
are closely related to the elements
X_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}\in\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
and
Y_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}\in\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
defined in (
3.1.2
).

7.3a.
The endomorphism algebras of the modules
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
and
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}

It follows from (
3.1.3
) that for any
g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
and
\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(d)
, we have

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\mu}X_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}=M_{d,j}\Big(\sum_{w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mu}g\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\cap{\mathscr{D}}^{\lambda}}w\Big)\,{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\subseteq M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda},

so the right multiplication by
X_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}
defines a homomorphism from
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\mu}
to
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
.
Denote by
\upxi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
the endomorphism of
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
which is zero on the summands
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\nu}
with
\nu\neq\mu
and sends
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\mu}
to
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
via the homomorphism given by the right multiplication by
X^{g}_{\mu,\lambda}
.

Theorem 7.3.1
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. Then there is a graded superalgebra isomorphism

\textstyle S(n,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}\big(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\big),\ \xi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}\mapsto\upxi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}.

Proof.

Let
{\mathpzc{f}}
,
{\mathpzc{g}}
and
{\mathpzc{q}}
be the functors defined in §
2.2e
, taking the projective module
P
to be the
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-module
M_{d,j}
, so that
H=\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, see Theorem
7.1.6
. We have an equivalence
{\mathpzc{q}}\circ{\mathpzc{g}}
of graded supercategories coming from Theorem
2.2.28
.
By Lemma
6.2.21
, every composition factor of the socle of
M_{d,j}
appears in its head, so by Lemma
2.2.30
, for every
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
, we have

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\simeq{\mathpzc{q}}\circ{\mathpzc{g}}(\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda})\simeq{\mathpzc{q}}\circ{\mathpzc{g}}(M^{\lambda}).

So

\textstyle\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\simeq{\mathpzc{q}}\circ{\mathpzc{g}}\big(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M^{\lambda}\big),

and the endomorphism algebras of
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
and
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M^{\lambda}
are isomorphic by Theorem
2.2.28
. The latter is
S(n,d)
by definition.
It remains to use Lemma
2.2.30
one more time to
check that the image of
\xi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
under the functor
{\mathpzc{q}}\circ{\mathpzc{g}}
is precisely the endomorphism
\upxi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
.
∎

It follows from (
3.1.4
) that for any
g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, we have

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\mu}Y_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}=M_{d,j}\Big(\sum_{w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mu}g\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\cap{\mathscr{D}}^{\lambda}}\mathtt{sgn}(w)w\Big)\,{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}\subseteq M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda},

so the right multiplication by
Y_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}
defines a homomorphism from
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\mu}
to
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}
.
Denote by
\upeta^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
the endomorphism of
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}
which is zero on the summands
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\nu}
with
\nu\neq\mu
and sends
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\mu}
to
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}
via the homomorphism induced by the right multiplication by
Y^{g}_{\mu,\lambda}
. The same argument as in the proof of Theorem
7.3.1
yields:

Theorem 7.3.2
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. Then there is a graded superalgebra isomorphism

S(n,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}\big(\textstyle\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}\big),\ \xi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}\mapsto\upeta^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}.

7.3b.
The endomorphism algebra of
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}

Our real object of interest is the endomorphism algebra of
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
. In this subsection we will use Theorem
7.3.1
to show that this is also the classical Schur algebra.

Lemma 7.3.3
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
,
\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(d)
and
g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
. Then
\Gamma_{j}^{\mu}X^{g}_{\mu,\lambda}\subseteq\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
, so the right multiplication by
X^{g}_{\mu,\lambda}
induces a homomorphism
\Gamma_{j}^{\mu}\to\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
of graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodules.

Proof.

It suffices to check that
\Gamma_{j}^{\mu}X^{g}_{\mu,\lambda}(u-1)=0
for all
u\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
.
But for any such
u
, we have using (
3.1.3
) that

M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\mu}X^{g}_{\mu,\lambda}(u-1)=M_{d,j}\Big(\sum_{w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\mu}g\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\cap{\mathscr{D}}^{\lambda}}w\Big){\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}(u-1)=0.

As the right multiplication by
X^{g}_{\mu,\lambda}(u-1)
yields a graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodule homomorphism
\Gamma_{j}^{\mu}\to M_{d,j}
, we deduce from Lemma
7.2.21
that
\Gamma_{j}^{\mu}X^{g}_{\mu,\lambda}(u-1)=0
.
∎

In view of the lemma, we denote by
\upzeta^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
the endomorphism of
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
which is zero on the summands
\Gamma_{j}^{\nu}
with
\nu\neq\mu
and sends
\Gamma_{j}^{\mu}
to
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
via the homomorphism given by the right multiplication by
X^{g}_{\mu,\lambda}
.

Theorem 7.3.4
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. Then there is a graded superalgebra isomorphism

\textstyle S(n,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}\big(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}\big),\ \xi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}\mapsto\upzeta^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}.

Proof.

We have

A\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}\subseteq B\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}.

Let
S
be the graded subsuperalgebra of
\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(B)
consisting of the endomorphisms which preserve
A
. Restriction yields a graded superalgebra homomorphism
S\to\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(A)
. This homomorphism is injective by Lemma
7.2.21
. Moreover, this homomorphism is surjective since by Theorem
7.3.1
, the elements
\upxi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
span
S
, and by definition
\upxi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
is the restriction of
\upzeta^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
. In particular,
\{\upzeta^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}\mid\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(n,d),\,g\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}_{d}^{\mu}\}
is a basis of
S
. It remains to prove using dimensions that
S=\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(B)
. On expanding the direct sums this will follow by dimensions if we can show that

\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(\Gamma_{j}^{\mu},\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda})=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}}(M^{\lambda},M^{\mu})

for all
\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(n,d)
. But

\displaystyle\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(\Gamma_{j}^{\mu},\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}((\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda})^{\sharp},(\Gamma_{j}^{\mu})^{\sharp})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(S_{j}^{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}},S_{j}^{\mu^{\mathrm{op}}})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}({\mathpzc{g}}M^{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}},{\mathpzc{g}}M^{\mu^{\mathrm{op}}})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(M^{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}},{\mathpzc{f}}{\mathpzc{g}}M^{\mu^{\mathrm{op}}})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(M^{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}},M^{\mu^{\mathrm{op}}})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(M^{\lambda},M^{\mu})

using consecutively
Lemma
6.2.16
, Lemma
7.2.20
, Lemma
7.2.19
, the fact that the functor
{\mathpzc{g}}
is left adjoint to the functor
{\mathpzc{f}}
(see §
2.2e
), Lemma
2.2.26
, and the isomorphism
M^{\nu^{\mathrm{op}}}\cong M^{\nu}
coming from the fact that the subgroups
\mathfrak{S}_{\nu^{\mathrm{op}}}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{\nu}
of
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
are conjugate.
∎

Chapter 8
Morita equivalence of imaginary and classical Schur algebras

We continue to work with a fixed
j\in J
.
In Theorem
7.3.4
, we have established the isomorphism
\textstyle S(n,d)\cong\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}\big(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}\big).
The main result of this chapter is that, under the assumption
n\geq d
, the graded supermodule
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
is a projective generator for the imaginary graded Schur superalgebra
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
, thus
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
is graded Morita superequivalent to the classical Schur algebra
S(n,d)
.

To establish projectivity of the graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodules
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
, we will compare them to certain projective graded
C_{d}
-supermodules which we call (imaginary) Gelfand-Grave modules.

8.1.
Imaginary Gelfand-Graev modules

8.1a.
The modules
G_{d,j}
and
G_{\lambda,j}

Recall from §§
4.2a
,
4.2b
the (divided power) Gelfand-Graev word

\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}
and the corresponding idempotent
{f}^{d,j}=1_{\text{\boldmath$l$}^{d,j}}
.
We define the
Gelfand-Graev module
to be
the projective graded
C_{d}
-supermodule

\displaystyle G_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=C_{d}{f}^{d,j}.

We identify
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}=\operatorname{End}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}

via the isomorphism of
Theorem
6.1.9
. Then
\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{d,j},M_{d,j})
is naturally a right
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-module.

Lemma 8.1.1
.

We have an isomorphism of right
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-modules

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{d,j},M_{d,j})\cong\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.1.18
,
{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}
has
\Bbbk
-basis
\{g_{d,j}v_{d,j}\}
.
So, as
\Bbbk
-modules,

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{d,j},M_{d,j})\cong{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}\cong\Bbbk.

Now, as
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-modules,
\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{d,j},M_{d,j})\cong\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}
thanks to Lemma
6.1.17
.
∎

Recall from Theorem
6.2.7
(ii) the composition factors
\{L_{j}(\lambda)\mid\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)\}
of
M_{d,j}
. We denote by
P_{j}(\lambda)
the projective cover of
L_{j}(\lambda)
as a graded
C_{d}
-supermodule.

Lemma 8.1.2
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. Then
G_{d,j}\cong P_{j}\big((d)\big)
.

Proof.

For any
V\in C_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{d,j},V)=\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(C_{d}{f}^{d,j},V)\cong{f}^{d,j}V.

So the lemma follows from Theorem
6.2.7
(ii) and (
6.2.4
).∎

More generally, for
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
, we consider
the projective graded supermodule over the parabolic
C_{\lambda}=C_{\lambda_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes C_{\lambda_{n}}

G_{\lambda,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=G_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes G_{\lambda_{n},j}.

and the projective graded
C_{d}
-supermodule

(8.1.3)

G_{j}^{\lambda}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}G_{\lambda,j}\simeq C_{n}{f}^{\lambda,j},

see Lemma
4.2.30
for the last isomorphism.

We identify
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}=\operatorname{End}_{C_{d}}(M_{\lambda,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}

via the isomorphism of
Corollary
6.1.11
, so
\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},M_{\lambda,j})
is naturally a right
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
-module.
From Lemma
8.1.1
, we get:

Lemma 8.1.4
.

We have an isomorphism of right
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
-modules

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},M_{\lambda,j})\cong\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}.

From Lemma
8.1.2
, we get:

Lemma 8.1.5
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
. Then

G_{\lambda,j}\cong P_{j}\big((\lambda_{1})\big)\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes P_{j}\big((\lambda_{n})\big).

Lemma 8.1.6
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
. Then
\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{j}^{\lambda},L_{j}(\lambda))=1
.

Proof.

We have

\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{j}^{\lambda},L_{j}(\lambda))=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(C_{n}{f}^{\lambda,j},L_{j}(\lambda))=\dim{f}^{\lambda,j}L_{j}(\lambda)=1

by (
8.1.3
) and (
6.2.4
).
∎

Proposition 8.1.7
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
. Then
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}G_{d,j}\cong G_{\lambda,j}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
6.2.11
,
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}G_{d,j}
is a projective graded supermodule over
C_{\lambda}
. Moreover, by Lemmas
4.2.29
and
8.1.1
, we have

\displaystyle\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}G_{d,j},M_{\lambda,j})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{d,j},{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}M_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}},j})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{d,j},M_{d,j})=1,

hence exactly one composition factor of
M_{\lambda,j}
appears in the head of
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}G_{d,j}
.
By Lemma
8.1.5
,
G_{\lambda,j}
is the projective cover of
L_{j}\big((\lambda_{1})\big)\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L_{j}\big((\lambda_{n})\big)
. So to complete the proof of the proposition it remains to show that for an irreducible graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule
L
, we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}G_{d,j},L)=0

unless
L\cong L_{j}((\lambda_{1}))\boxtimes\dots L_{j}((\lambda_{n}))
.

Recalling the classification of irreducible modules from Theorem
6.2.7
, an arbitrary irreducible graded supermodule over
C_{\lambda}
has form
L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(1)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(n)})
where
\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(r)}=(\mu_{(r)}^{(0)},\dots,\mu_{(r)}^{(\ell-1)})\in{\mathcal{P}}^{J}(\lambda_{r})
for
r=1,\dots,n
.
Now, by Lemma
4.2.29
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}\big({\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}G_{d,j},L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(1)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(n)})\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}\big(G_{d,j},{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}^{d}\big(L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(n)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(1)})\big)\big).

For
r=1,\dots,n
,
by definition of
L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(r)})
, we have

L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(r)})={\mathrm{GGI}}^{\lambda_{r}}_{|\mu_{(r)}^{(0)}|,\dots,|\mu_{(r)}^{(\ell-1)}|}\big(L(\mu_{(r)}^{(0)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L(\mu_{(r)}^{(\ell-1)})\big).

Note that for any
i\in J
, we have that all composition factors of

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{|\mu_{(1)}^{(i)}|,\dots,|\mu_{(n)}^{(i)}|}^{|\mu_{(1)}^{(i)}|+\dots+|\mu_{(n)}^{(i)}|}\big((L(\mu_{(1)}^{(i)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L(\mu_{(n)}^{(i)})\big)

are composition factors of
M_{|\mu_{(1)}^{(i)}|+\dots+|\mu_{(n)}^{(i)}|,i}
, i.e. have form
L_{i}(\nu^{(i)})
for a partition
\nu^{(i)}\in{\mathcal{P}}(|\mu_{(1)}^{(i)}|+\dots+|\mu_{(n)}^{(i)}|)
.
So, using Corollary
3.3.31
, we deduce that all composition factors of
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}^{d}\big(L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(n)})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes L(\text{\boldmath$\mu$}_{(1)})\big)
are of the form
L(\text{\boldmath$\nu$})
for
\text{\boldmath$\nu$}=(\nu^{(0)},\dots,\nu^{(\ell-1)})
with each
\nu^{(i)}\in{\mathcal{P}}(|\mu_{(1)}^{(i)}|+\dots+|\mu_{(n)}^{(i)}|)
.
By Lemma
8.1.2
, we deduce that for an irreducible
C_{d}
-module
L
, we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}G_{d,j},L)=0

unless
L\cong L_{j}(\mu^{(1)})\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes L_{j}(\mu^{(n)})
, where
\mu^{(r)}\in{\mathcal{P}}(\lambda_{r})
for
r=1,\dots,n
.

Moreover, again

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}G_{d,j},L_{j}(\mu^{(1)})\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes L_{j}(\mu^{(n)}))

\displaystyle\simeq\,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{d,j},{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}\big(L_{j}(\mu^{(n)})\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes L_{j}(\mu^{(1)})\big)).

Since
M_{d,j}\cong{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}(M_{\lambda_{n},j}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes M_{\lambda_{1},j})
, the multiset of the composition factors of
M_{d,j}
is the disjoint union of the multisets of the composition factors of the modules
{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}\big(L_{j}(\mu^{(n)})\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes L_{j}(\mu^{(1)})\big)
as
L_{j}(\mu^{(r)})
runs over the multiset of the composition factors of
M_{\lambda_{r},j}
for
r=1,\dots,n
.
Lemmas
8.1.2
and
8.1.1
now imply that for exactly one tuple
(\mu^{(1)},\dots,\mu^{(n)})
we have that the above Hom-space is
1
-dimensional and for all other tuples, it is trivial. But by Corollary
6.2.12
, we have that
L_{j}((d))
is a composition factor of
{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}L_{j}\big((\lambda_{n})\big)\boxtimes\dots L_{j}\big((\lambda_{1})\big)
, so the only tuple
(\mu^{(1)},\dots,\mu^{(n)})
for which the above Hom-space is non-trivial must be of the form
\big((\lambda_{1}),\dots,(\lambda_{n})\big)
.
∎

8.1b.
The modules
Y_{j}^{\lambda}

To compare
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
with
G_{j}^{\lambda}
we introduce the intermediate modules
Y_{j}^{\lambda}
.
First, we consider the graded
C_{d}
-subsupermodule of
M_{d,j}

(8.1.8)

Y_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=C_{d}{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}.

More generally, for
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
, we consider the graded

C_{\lambda}
-subsupermodule of
M_{\lambda,j}

(8.1.9)

Y_{\lambda,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=C_{\lambda}{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{\lambda,j}\simeq Y_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes Y_{\lambda_{n},j}

and the graded
C_{d}
-supermodule

(8.1.10)

Y_{j}^{\lambda}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}Y_{\lambda,j}.

By Lemma
6.1.18
,
{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}
has basis
\{g_{d,j}v_{d,j}\}
. Since
g_{d,j}v_{d,j}\in\Gamma_{d,j}
by Lemma
6.1.17
, we deduce that

(8.1.11)

Y_{d,j}\subseteq\Gamma_{d,j}.

So, more generally,

(8.1.12)

Y_{\lambda,j}\subseteq\Gamma_{\lambda,j}.

We will eventually prove that
Y_{d,j}=\Gamma_{d,j}
and
Y_{\lambda,j}=\Gamma_{\lambda,j}
.

Recall from Lemma
8.1.4
that
\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},M_{\lambda,j})\cong\Bbbk
, so we can always pick a homomorphism

\varphi_{\lambda}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},M_{\lambda,j})

which spans
\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},M_{\lambda,j})
as a
\Bbbk
-module.

Lemma 8.1.13
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
Y_{\lambda,j}
is the image of
\varphi_{\lambda}
, where
\varphi_{\lambda}
is a homomorphism which spans

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},M_{\lambda,j})
.

Proof.

We have

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},M_{\lambda,j})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(C_{\lambda}{f}^{\lambda,j},M_{\lambda,j})\cong{f}^{\lambda,j}M_{\lambda,j}

which implies the result.
∎

Lemma 8.1.14
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,Y_{d,j}\simeq Y_{\lambda,j}
.

Proof.

By definition,
Y_{d,j}\subseteq M_{d,j}
, so using Lemmas
6.1.15
(i) and
4.2.29
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\neq\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(Y_{d,j},M_{d,j})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(Y_{d,j},{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}}^{d}\,M_{\lambda^{\mathrm{op}},j})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,Y_{d,j},M_{\lambda,j}).

In particular,
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,Y_{d,j}\neq 0
.

By Lemma
8.1.13
, if
0\neq\varphi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G_{d,j},M_{d,j})
, then
\operatorname{Im}\varphi=Y_{d,j}
. Since

Y_{d,j}\subseteq\Gamma_{d,j}
by (
8.1.11
), we consider
\varphi
as a homomorphism
G_{d,j}\to\Gamma_{d,j}
. Applying the functor
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}
to
\varphi
, we get a homomorphism

\bar{\varphi}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,G_{d,j}\to{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,\Gamma_{d,j}
,
or, in view of Proposition
8.1.7
and Lemma
7.2.14
(ii), a homomorphism
\bar{\varphi}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}G_{\lambda,j}\to\Gamma_{\lambda,j}
. As the functor
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}
is exact,
\operatorname{Im}\bar{\varphi}={\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}Y_{d,j}
, which is non-zero by the previous paragraph. Now by Lemma
8.1.13
, we have
\operatorname{Im}\bar{\varphi}=Y_{\lambda,j}
, completing the proof.
∎

8.1c.
Projectivity of
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. By Lemma
8.1.13
, we have
Y_{\lambda,j}=\operatorname{Im}\varphi_{\lambda}
for a homomorphism
\varphi_{\lambda}
which spans

\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},M_{\lambda,j})\cong\Bbbk
. So we can consider
\varphi_{\lambda}
as a surjective homomorphism
G_{\lambda,j}\to Y_{\lambda,j}
. Applying the exact functor
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
, we get the surjective homomorphism of graded
C_{d}
-supermodules

(8.1.15)

{\varphi}^{\lambda}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}G^{\lambda}_{j}={\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,G_{\lambda,j}{\twoheadrightarrow}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,Y_{\lambda,j}=Y^{\lambda}_{j}.

Lemma 8.1.16
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. For any
g\in\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G^{\lambda}_{j},M_{d,j})
there exists a unique
\bar{g}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(Y^{\lambda}_{j},M_{d,j})
such that
g=\bar{g}\circ{\varphi}^{\lambda}
. In particular, for any submodule
V\subseteq M_{d,j}
, we have
\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G^{\lambda}_{j},V)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(Y^{\lambda}_{j},V)
.

Proof.

As
{\varphi}^{\lambda}
is surjective, the uniqueness of
\bar{g}
is clear.

Since
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
is left adjoint to
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}
, we have the natural (counit of adjunction) homomorphism

{\varepsilon}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,M_{d,j}\to M_{d,j}

which, for any graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule
W
, yields the adjunction isomorphism

\alpha_{W}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(W,{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,M_{d,j})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}({\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,W,M_{d,j}),\ h\mapsto{\varepsilon}\circ{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}(h).

Taking
W=G_{\lambda,j}
, let

g_{\lambda}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\alpha_{G_{\lambda,j}}^{-1}(g)\in\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,M_{d,j}).

By Lemma
7.1.2
(ii), we have
{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,M_{d,j}\simeq M_{\lambda,j}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, so using Lemma
8.1.13
, there exists a homomorphism
\bar{g}_{\lambda}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}Y_{\lambda,j}\to{\mathrm{GGR}}^{d}_{\lambda}\,M_{d,j}
such that
g_{\lambda}=\bar{g}_{\lambda}\circ\varphi_{\lambda}
. Hence

{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}(g_{\lambda})={\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}(\bar{g}_{\lambda})\circ{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}(\varphi_{\lambda})={\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}(\bar{g}_{\lambda})\circ{\varphi}^{\lambda}.

Therefore

g={\varepsilon}\circ{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}(g_{\lambda})={\varepsilon}\circ{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}(\bar{g}_{\lambda})\circ{\varphi}^{\lambda},

and we can take
\bar{g}={\varepsilon}\circ{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}(\bar{g}_{\lambda})
.
∎

Lemma 8.1.17
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. For any
\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(d)
, we have

(8.1.18)

\displaystyle\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(Y^{\lambda}_{j},Y^{\mu}_{j})=|{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu}|,

(8.1.19)

\displaystyle\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G^{\lambda}_{j},Y^{\mu}_{j})=|{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu}|,

(8.1.20)

\displaystyle\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G^{\lambda}_{j},\Gamma^{\mu}_{j})=|{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu}|.

Proof.

We first prove (
8.1.19
). By the adjointness of
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
and
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G^{\lambda}_{j},Y^{\mu}_{j})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,G_{\lambda,j},{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}\,Y_{\mu,j})

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}\,Y_{\mu,j}).

Since
G_{\lambda,j}
is projective, Theorem
4.2.34
and Lemma
8.1.14
give

\displaystyle\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}\,Y_{\mu,j})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu}}\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},{\mathrm{GGI}}^{\lambda}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}\,{}^{x}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{\mu}_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu}\,Y_{\mu,j})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu}}\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},{\mathrm{GGI}}^{\lambda}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}\,{}^{x}(Y_{x^{-1}\lambda\cap\mu,j})

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{x\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu}}\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},{\mathrm{GGI}}^{\lambda}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}\,Y_{\lambda\cap x\mu,j})

To complete the proof of (
8.1.19
), we show that each dimension in the sum above equals
1
. Note that the composition
\lambda\cap x\mu
is a refinement of
\lambda
. Denote by
\nu
the composition obtained by from
\lambda\cap x\mu
by taking the parts of this refinement within each part
\lambda_{r}
of
\lambda
in the opposite order. Using Lemma
4.2.29
, Proposition
8.1.7
and Lemma
8.1.13
,
we get

\displaystyle\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\lambda,j},{\mathrm{GGI}}^{\lambda}_{\lambda\cap x\mu}\,Y_{\lambda\cap x\mu,j})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\nu}}({\mathrm{GGR}}^{\lambda}_{\nu}\,G_{\lambda,j},Y_{\nu,j})

\displaystyle=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{\lambda}}(G_{\nu,j},Y_{\nu,j})=1.

The proof for (
8.1.20
) is similar to that for (
8.1.19
), using Lemma
7.2.14
(ii) instead of Lemma
8.1.14
.

For (
8.1.18
), as
Y_{\mu,j}\subseteq M_{\mu,j}
and
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}
is exact,
Y^{\mu}_{j}={\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}Y_{\mu,j}
is isomorphic to a submodule of
M_{d,j}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\mu}^{d}M_{\mu,j}
, so (
8.1.18
) follows from (
8.1.19
) by Lemma
8.1.16
.
∎

Theorem 8.1.21
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
.

(i)

We have
\Gamma_{d,j}=Y_{d,j}
, so
\Gamma_{d,j}
can be characterized as the image of any non-zero homomorphism from
G_{d,j}
to
M_{d,j}
.

(ii)

For each
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
, we have that
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
is a projective graded supermodule over
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
.

(iii)

If
n\geq d
then
\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
is a projective generator for
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
.

Proof.

Fix
n\geq d
and set

\displaystyle\textstyle\Gamma\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j},\quad Y\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}Y^{\lambda}_{j},\quad G\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}G^{\lambda}_{j}.

Let
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
. By (
8.1.3
), we have
C_{n}{f}^{\lambda,j}\simeq G^{\lambda}_{j}
. As
n\geq d
, we can consider
\lambda
as an element of
\Uplambda(n,d)
, and so
G^{\lambda}_{j}
is a direct summand of
G
. By (
6.2.4
), we have
\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{n}}(C_{n}{f}^{\lambda,j},L_{j}(\lambda))\simeq{f}^{\lambda,j}L_{j}(\lambda)\neq 0
, and so, by Theorem
6.2.7
(ii), every composition factor of
M_{d,j}
appears in the head of
G
. Hence
\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G,X)\neq 0
for any
X\in C_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}
all of whose composition factors are composition factors of
M_{d,j}
.

On the other hand, for each
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
, recalling (
8.1.12
) and
applying the exact functor
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
to the inclusions
Y_{\lambda,j}\subseteq\Gamma_{\lambda,j}\subseteq M_{\lambda,j}
, we get

Y^{\lambda}_{j}\subseteq\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j}\subseteq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\,M_{\lambda,j}\simeq M_{d,j}.

In particular,
Y\subseteq\Gamma
and all composition factors of
\Gamma/Y
are composition factors of
M_{d,j}
.
By Lemma
8.1.17
, we have

\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G,Y)=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G,\Gamma).

As
G
is projective this implies
\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G,\Gamma/Y)=0
, so
\Gamma/Y=0
by the previous paragraph. This proves (i).

Now,
Y=\Gamma
is a quotient of
G
, see (
8.1.15
), and so by Lemma
8.1.17
,

\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(\Gamma,\Gamma)=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{C_{d}}(G,\Gamma).

This verifies the conditions of Lemma
7.1.5
, so
\Gamma
is
projective when considered as a graded
C_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(\Gamma)
-supermodule. Hence so is every summand
\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j}
of
\Gamma
.
Since all summands of
\Gamma
are isomorhic to submodules of
M_{d,j}
, and one of the summands

\Gamma^{\omega_{d}}_{j}\simeq M_{d,j}
, we have
{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(\Gamma)={\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})
and
C_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(\Gamma)=C_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})={\bar{C}}_{d,j}
, proving (ii).

By Theorem
7.3.4
, we have

\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(\Gamma)\cong S(n,d)
. For
n\geq d
, the number of irreducible representations of
S(n,d)
is equal to
|{\mathcal{P}}(d)|
. So by Fitting’s Lemma
[
36
, 1.4]
, the graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodule
\Gamma
has the same amount of non-isomorphic irreducible modules appearing in its head. Taking into account Theorem
6.2.7
(ii), every composition factor of
M_{d,j}
appears in the head of
\Gamma
.
Since
M_{d,j}
is a faithful
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-module, every irreducible
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-module appears as some composition factor of
M_{d,j}
. Thus every irreducible
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-module appears in the head of
\Gamma
, so
\Gamma
is a projective generator, proving (iii).
∎

Recall Lemma
7.1.4
. In particular, we have a subalgebra
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\subseteq\bar{f}_{\lambda}{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}_{\lambda}
.

Corollary 8.1.22
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
.

(i)

The functor
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}

restricts to a functor
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
. Moreover, for
V\in{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we have
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}V\simeq{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}}V
.

(ii)

The functor
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}
restricts to a functor
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
.

Proof.

We prove (i), the proof of (ii) being similar. As the functor
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
is exact, it suffices to check the first statement on projective graded
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
-supermodules. By Theorem
8.1.21
, every indecomposable projective graded
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
-supermodule is a submodule of
M_{\lambda,j}
, so we just need to check that
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{\lambda,j}
is a
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-module. But
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}M_{\lambda,j}\simeq M_{d,j}
. The second statement follows from the first using adjointness of induction and restriction.
∎

Corollary 8.1.23
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
,
W\in{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
and
V\in{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
. Then

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}}(W,{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}V)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}W,V).

Proof.

To prove the claim, the adjoint functor property gives us an isomorphism
of functors
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}}(W,-)\circ{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}W,-).

Since
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}
is exact and sends injectives to injectives (being adjoint to the exact functor
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
), an application of
[
24
, I.4.1(3)]
completes the proof of the claim.
∎

Corollary 8.1.24
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
,
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
and
V\in{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
. Considering
V
as a graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule via inflation, we have the graded
C_{\lambda}
-supermodule
V^{\sharp}
. Then
V^{\sharp}
factors through to a
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
-module.

Proof.

By Theorem
8.1.21
(iii),
V
is a direct sum of subquotients of
M_{\lambda,j}
. Since
M_{\lambda,j}^{\sharp}\simeq M_{\lambda,j}
by Lemma
6.2.21
and (
6.2.15
),
V^{\sharp}
is also a direct sum of subquotients of
M_{\lambda,j}
. Hence
V^{\sharp}
factors through to a module over
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}
.
∎

Recall from (
6.1.21
), (
6.1.22
) that
\operatorname{bideg}(g_{\lambda,j}v_{\lambda,j})=(a_{\lambda,j},dj\text{ }(\text{\rm mod }2)\,)
.

Corollary 8.1.25
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
,
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
and set

\bar{f}^{\lambda,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}^{\lambda,j}+{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})\in C_{d}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d}}(M_{d,j})={\bar{C}}_{d,j}.

Then
{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{\lambda,j}}\,\Uppi^{dj}\,{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}^{\lambda,j}\simeq\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
as
graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodules.

Proof.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})
.
Using Corollary
8.1.22
(i), we get

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}\big({\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{\lambda_{1},j}}\,\Uppi^{\lambda_{1}j}\,{\bar{C}}_{\lambda_{1},j}\bar{f}^{\lambda_{1},j}\,\boxtimes\,\dots\,\boxtimes\,{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{\lambda_{n},j}}\,\Uppi^{\lambda_{1}j}\,{\bar{C}}_{\lambda_{n},j}\bar{f}^{\lambda_{n},j}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{\lambda_{1},j}+\dots+a_{\lambda_{n},j}}\,\Uppi^{\lambda_{1}j+\dots+\lambda_{n}j}\,{\mathrm{GGI}}^{d}_{\lambda}({\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\bar{f}^{\lambda,j})

\displaystyle\simeq\

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{\lambda,j}}\,\Uppi^{dj}\,{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}}{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j}\bar{f}^{\lambda,j}

\displaystyle\simeq\

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{\lambda,j}}\,\Uppi^{dj}\,{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}^{\lambda,j}.

Since by Lemma
7.2.16
, we have
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\big(\Gamma_{\lambda_{1},j}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\Gamma_{\lambda_{n},j}\big)
, this reduces the proof of the lemma to the
n=1
case, i.e. to proving that
{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{d,j}}\,\Uppi^{dj}\,{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}^{d,j}\simeq\Gamma_{d,j}
.

By Lemma
6.1.18
,
{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}
has basis
\{g_{d,j}v_{d,j}\}
, so, by (
8.1.8
), there is a bidegree
(0,{\bar{0}})
surjective homomorphism

{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{d,j}}\,\Uppi^{dj}\,{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}_{d}\to Y_{d,j},\ \bar{f}_{d}\mapsto g_{d,j}v_{d,j}
. But
Y_{d,j}=\Gamma_{d,j}
is projective by
Theorem
8.1.21
. Hence
\Gamma_{d,j}
is a direct summand of
{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{\lambda,j}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}_{d}
. But
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\bar{f}_{d}
is a quotient of
G_{d,j}
so it is indecomposable by Lemma
8.1.2
.
∎

8.2.
Morita equivalence functors

8.2a.
The functors
{\mathpzc{a}}_{n,d,j}
and
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}

In view of Theorem
7.3.4
we can consider

(8.2.1)

\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}

as a graded
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},S(n,d)^{\mathrm{op}})
-bisupermodule.
Identifying
S(n,d)^{\mathrm{op}}
with
S(n,d)
using the anti-automorphism
\tau
of Lemma
3.1.11
, we consider
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)
as a graded
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},S(n,d))
-bisupermodule.
This yields the functors

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{a}}_{n,d,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to S(n,d)\!\operatorname{-mod},\ V\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(\Gamma_{j}(n,d),V),

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}S(n,d)\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\!\operatorname{-mod},\ W\mapsto\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}W.

If
n\geq d
,
which we assume from now on
, and
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
, then by Theorem
8.1.21
(iii), these functors are mutually quasi-inverse (graded super)equivalences.

Recall the
S(n,d)
-modules
\Gamma^{\lambda}_{n,d}
and
\Lambda^{\lambda}_{n,d}
from §
3.1c
.

Lemma 8.2.2
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)
. Then

\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Gamma^{\lambda}_{n,d})\quad{and}\quad M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}\simeq{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Lambda^{\lambda}_{n,d}).

Proof.

For the first claim,
we have
\Gamma^{\lambda}_{n,d}\simeq S(n,d)\xi_{\lambda}
by Lemma
3.1.25
(i), so

{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Gamma^{\lambda}_{n,d})\simeq\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}S(n,d)\xi_{\lambda}\simeq\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\cdot\xi_{\lambda}=\tau(\xi_{\lambda})(\Gamma_{j}(n,d))=\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda},

where the last equality comes from the definition of the action of
S(n,d)
on
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)
from Theorem
7.3.4
.

For the second claim, we have
\Lambda^{\lambda}_{n,d}\simeq S(n,d)\kappa({\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda})
by Lemma
3.1.25
(ii), so

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Lambda^{\lambda}_{n,d})

\displaystyle\simeq\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}S(n,d)\kappa({\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\simeq\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\cdot\kappa({\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\tau(\kappa({\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}))(\Gamma_{j}(n,d)).

Note using Lemma
3.1.14
that

\tau(\kappa({\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}))=\tau\big(\sum_{g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\mathtt{sgn}(g)\xi^{g}_{\omega_{d},\omega_{d}}\big)=\sum_{g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\mathtt{sgn}(g)\xi^{g^{-1}}_{\omega_{d},\omega_{d}}=\sum_{g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\mathtt{sgn}(g)\xi^{g}_{\omega_{d},\omega_{d}}.

Note that
\Gamma^{\omega_{d}}_{j}=M_{d,j}
. So by the definition of the action of
S(n,d)
on
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)
from Theorem
7.3.4
, we now have

\tau(\kappa({\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda}))(\Gamma_{j}(n,d))=\sum_{g\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\mathtt{sgn}(g)\xi^{g}_{\omega_{d},\omega_{d}}(\Gamma_{j}(n,d))\simeq M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda},

as required.
∎

Recall the irreducible graded
C_{d}
-supermodules
\{L_{j}(\lambda)\mid\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)\}
from Theorem
6.2.7
(ii).
We consider these as irreducible graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodules.
We have the irreducible (graded)
S(n,d)
-(super)modues
\{L_{n,d}(\lambda)\mid\lambda\in\Uplambda_{+}(n,d)\}
from §
3.1c
.
Since
n\geq d
, we can identify the sets
{\mathcal{P}}(d)
and
\Uplambda_{+}(n,d)
. Then:

Lemma 8.2.3
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. For
\lambda\in\Uplambda_{+}(n,d)
, we have
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(L_{n,d}(\lambda))\simeq L_{j}(\lambda)
.

Proof.

Since
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}
is an equivalence,
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(L_{n,d}(\lambda))
is irreducible, and by definition, it is a composition factor of
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)
, hence of
M_{d,j}
. By Theorem
6.2.7
(ii), we must have
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(L_{n,d}(\lambda))\simeq L_{j}(f(\lambda))
for some bijection
f
on
{\mathcal{P}}(d)
. We prove that
f(\lambda)=\lambda
by induction on the dominance order on
{\mathcal{P}}(d)
. For the smallest partition
\omega_{d}
, we have

{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(L_{n,d}(\omega_{d}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{EExtDef})}}{{\simeq}}{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Lambda^{(d)}_{n,d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma \ref{L6.3.1}}}}{{\simeq}}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Corollary \ref{CCompFactorsMy}}}}{{\simeq}}L_{j}(\omega_{d}).

For an arbitrary
\lambda
, suppose we already know that
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(L_{n,d}(\mu))\simeq L_{j}(\mu)
for all
\mu\lhd\lambda
.
By Lemma
3.1.24
, the composition factors of
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Lambda_{n,d}^{\lambda^{\prime}})
are of the form
L_{j}(\mu)
with
\mu\lhd\lambda
, as well as
L_{j}(f(\lambda))
(appearing once). On the other hand, by Lemma
8.2.2
, we have
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Lambda_{n,d}^{\lambda^{\prime}})\simeq M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
, whose composition factors, by Corollary
6.2.5
, are of the form
L_{j}(\mu)
with
\mu\lhd\lambda
, as well as
L_{j}(\lambda)
(appearing once). It follows that
f(\lambda)=\lambda
.
∎

Recall from §
3.1c
that
S(n,d)
is a quasi-hereditary algebra with weight poset
\Uplambda_{+}(n,d)
partially ordered by the dominance order
\unlhd
. As
n\geq d
, we can identify
\Uplambda_{+}(n,d)
with the set
{\mathcal{P}}(d)
of partitions of
d
. So for
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
, the algebra
S(n,d)
has the standard module
\Delta_{n,d}(\lambda)
and the costandard module
\nabla_{n,d}(\lambda)
. For all
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
, define the (graded)
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodules

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}(\lambda)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Delta_{n,d}(\lambda))\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla_{j}(\lambda)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\nabla_{n,d}(\lambda)).

We need to explain why these definitions are independent of the choice of
n\geq d
. Take
N\geq n\geq d
and identify
S(n,d)
with the idempotent truncation
\xi(N,n;d)S(N,d)\xi(N,n;d)
using (
3.1.15
). Recalling the Morita equivalence from Lemma
3.1.27
, it suffices to prove the isomorphism of functors

(8.2.4)

{\mathpzc{b}}_{N,d,j}\circ{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S(n,d)}^{S(N,d)}\simeq{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}.

To see this, note that
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\simeq\Gamma_{j}(N,d)\cdot\xi(N,n;d)
. Now, for
W\in S(n,d)\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we have functorial isomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{b}}_{N,d,j}\circ{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S(n,d)}^{S(N,d)}(W)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{j}(N,d)\otimes_{S(N,d)}\otimes S(N,d)\xi(N,n;d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}(W)

\displaystyle\simeq\Gamma_{j}(N,d)\xi(N,n;d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}(W)

\displaystyle\simeq\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}(W)

\displaystyle={\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(W).

Finally, note using Lemmas
6.2.16
,
6.2.19
and general theory of quasi-hereditary algebras (see for example
[
9
, (1.2)]
) that

(8.2.5)

\Delta_{j}(\lambda)^{\sharp}\simeq\nabla_{j}(\lambda)\qquad(\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)).

Lemma 8.2.6
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. We have that
\dim{\bar{C}}_{d,j}=\sum_{\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)}(\dim\Delta_{j}(\lambda))^{2}
.

Proof.

This is a general fact on finite-dimensional quasi-hereditary algebras with duality. To give more details, let
\Delta_{j}^{\mathrm{op}}(\lambda)=\nabla_{j}(\lambda)^{*}
be the right
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-module with the action given by
(f\cdot s)(v)=f(sv)
for
f\in\nabla_{j}(\lambda)^{*},\,s\in{\bar{C}}_{d,j},\,v\in\nabla_{j}(\lambda)
.
Note by (
8.2.5
) that
\dim\Delta_{j}^{\mathrm{op}}(\lambda)=\dim\Delta_{j}(\lambda)
.
Finally, as an
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},{\bar{C}}_{d,j})
-bimodule,
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
has a filtration with subquotients
\{\Delta_{j}(\lambda)\otimes\Delta_{j}^{\mathrm{op}}(\lambda)\mid\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)\}
, see e.g.
[
35
, (2.20) and Proposition 3.5]
. ∎

Recall the right
S(n,d)
-modules
\tilde{\Delta}_{n,d}(\lambda)
from §
3.1c
and Lemma
3.1.21
.

Lemma 8.2.7
.

Let
\Bbbk=F
.
As a graded
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},S(n,d))
-bisuprmodule,
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)
has a filtration with subquotients
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{n,d}(\lambda)
appearing once for each
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
and ordered in any way refining the dominance order so that factors corresponding to more dominant
\lambda
appear lower in the filtration.

Proof.

The functor
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}-
can be viewed as an exact functor from the category of
(S(n,d),S(n,d))
-bimodules to the category of
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},S(n,d))
-bimodules. Then

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}(\Delta_{n,d}(\lambda)\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{n,d}(\lambda))

\displaystyle\simeq(\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}\Delta_{n,d}(\lambda))\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{n,d}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\simeq\Delta_{j}(\lambda)\otimes\tilde{\Delta}_{n,d}(\lambda).

So applying
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}-
to the filtration of Lemma
3.1.21
yields the result.
∎

We conclude this subsection with alternative descriptions of the standard modules
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)
for
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
without reference to the Morita equivalence
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\sim_{\mathrm{Mor}}S(n,d)
. This will be needed in §
8.3
.
Recall the element
u_{\lambda}
from Lemma
2.1.22
.

Theorem 8.2.8
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
.

(i)

We have that
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda},M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}})\cong\mathbb{F}
, and the image of any non-zero homomorphism from
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda},M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}})
is isomorphic to
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)
.

(ii)

We have that
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)\simeq\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}u_{\lambda^{\prime}}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.2.2
, we have
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}\simeq{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Gamma^{\lambda}_{n,d})
and
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\simeq{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Lambda^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{n,d})
. Since
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)={\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Delta_{n,d}(\lambda)
and
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}
is an equivalence, (i) now follows from Lemma
3.1.26
.

We have
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}\supseteq M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}
by (
7.2.15
). Moreover,
M_{d,j}
is a faithful right
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-module by Theorem
6.1.9
. Since
{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}u_{\lambda^{\prime}}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\neq 0
in
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
by Lemma
2.1.22
(with
\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}
), we conclude that
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda}u_{\lambda^{\prime}}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\neq 0
. Hence
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}u_{\lambda^{\prime}}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\neq 0
. Finally, observe that
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}u_{\lambda^{\prime}}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
is both a homomorphic image of
\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}
and a submodule of
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
. So (ii) follows from (i).
∎

8.2b.
Induction and Morita equivalence

Fix
n\geq d
. We now prove that the Morita equivalence

{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\sim_{\mathrm{Mor}}S(n,d)
of §
8.2a

intertwines Gelfand-Graev induction and the tensor product (
3.1.12
) for the classical Schur algebras.

Theorem 8.2.9
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
{\underline{d}}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})\in\Uplambda(m,d)
, and
W_{t}\in S(n,d_{t})\!\operatorname{-mod}
for
t=1,\dots,m
. Then there is a functorial isomorphism

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{d_{1},\dots,d_{m}}^{d}\big(({\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d_{1},j}W_{1})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes({\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d_{m},j}W_{m})\big)\simeq{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(W_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes W_{m}).

Proof.

Choose
{\underline{n}}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{m})\in\Uplambda(m,n)
with
n_{t}\geq d_{t}
for
t=1,\dots,m
, and recall the idempotents
\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\in S(n,d)
from (
3.1.18
) and the functors

{\mathrm{Res}}^{S(n,d)}_{S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}
and
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{S(n,d)}_{S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}

from (
3.1.20
).
For
t=1,\dots,m
, let
U_{t}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathrm{Res}}^{S(n,d_{t})}_{S(n_{t},d_{t})}W_{t}
. By Lemma
3.1.27
, we have
W_{t}\simeq{\mathrm{Ind}}^{S(n,d_{t})}_{S(n_{t},d_{t})}U_{t}
.

Recall the notation (
3.1.17
). So, for
\lambda\in\Uplambda({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})
we have that

\displaystyle\lambda^{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n_{1}})\in\Uplambda(n_{1},d_{1}),

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=(\lambda_{n_{1}+1},\dots,\lambda_{n_{1}+n_{2}})\in\Uplambda(n_{2},d_{2}),

\displaystyle\lambda^{m}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=(\lambda_{n_{1}+\dots+n_{m-1}+1},\dots,\lambda_{n})\in\Uplambda(n_{m},d_{m}).

Note that any composition in
\Uplambda({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})
is a refinement of the composition
{\underline{d}}
, and consider the set of triples

\Omega\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{(\lambda,\mu,g)\mid\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}}),\,g\in{}^{\lambda}{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu}_{\underline{d}}\}.

For
(\lambda,\mu,g)\in\Omega
, we have
\lambda^{t},\mu^{t}\in\Uplambda(n_{t},d_{t})
for
t=1,\dots,m
, and
g=(g_{1},\dots,g_{m})\in\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{d}}=\mathfrak{S}_{d_{1}}\times\dots\times\mathfrak{S}_{d_{m}}
, with each
g_{t}\in{}^{\lambda^{t}}{\mathscr{D}}^{\mu^{t}}_{d_{t}}
. So we have a basis

\{{\varphi}^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\varphi}^{g_{1}}_{\lambda^{1},\mu^{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes{\varphi}^{g_{1}}_{\lambda^{1},\mu^{1}}\mid(\lambda,\mu,g)\in\Omega\}

of
S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})=S(n_{1},d_{1})\otimes\dots\otimes S(n_{m},d_{m})
.
Recalling the Levi subalgebra embedding

S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\subseteq\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})S(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})

from (
3.1.19
), note that under this embedding the basis element
{\varphi}^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}\in S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})
maps to the element of
S(n,d)
with the same name (which makes sense since
\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{d}}\leq\mathfrak{S}_{d}
and
\Uplambda({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\subseteq\Uplambda(n,d)
).

Define the graded
({\bar{C}}_{{\underline{d}},j},S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}}))
-bisupermodule

\textstyle\Gamma_{{\underline{n}},{\underline{d}},j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{\underline{d}}\,\Gamma_{\lambda,j}\simeq\Gamma(n_{1},d_{1})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\Gamma(n_{m},d_{m}).

Then
\Gamma_{j}({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}\,\Gamma_{{\underline{n}},{\underline{d}},j}
is a graded
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}}))
-bisupermodule in a natural way, and by the transitivity of Gelfand-Graev induction we have

\Gamma_{j}({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\simeq\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}\Gamma_{j}^{\lambda}.

So
\Gamma_{j}({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})
can be identified with the direct summand
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})
of
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)
. Identifying
\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})S(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})
with
\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}}))^{\mathrm{sop}}
, we have a Levi subalgebra

S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\subseteq\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})S(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})=\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}}))^{\mathrm{sop}}.

In this way, we consider
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})
as a graded
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}}))
-bisupermodule. Moreover, it is clear that we have an isomorphism

\Gamma_{j}({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\simeq\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})
of graded
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}}))
-bisupermodules.

We now have

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(W_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes W_{m})

\displaystyle\simeq\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}\big(({\mathrm{Ind}}^{S(n,d_{1})}_{S(n_{1},d_{1})}U_{1})\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathrm{Ind}}^{S(n,d_{m})}_{S(n_{m},d_{m})}U_{m}\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lemma~\ref{1.5d}}}}{{\simeq}}\

\displaystyle{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}\big({\mathrm{Ind}}^{S(n,d)}_{S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}(U_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes U_{m})\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\otimes_{S(n,d)}S(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\otimes_{S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}(U_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes U_{m})

\displaystyle\simeq\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}(n,d)\xi({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\otimes_{S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}(U_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes U_{m})

\displaystyle\simeq\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})\otimes_{S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}(U_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes U_{m})

\displaystyle\simeq\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}\,\Gamma_{{\underline{n}},{\underline{d}},j})\otimes_{S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}(U_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes U_{m})

\displaystyle\simeq\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}\big(\Gamma_{{\underline{n}},{\underline{d}},j}\otimes_{S({\underline{n}},{\underline{d}})}(U_{1}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes U_{m})\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}\big({\mathpzc{b}}_{n_{1},d_{1},j}(U_{1})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathpzc{b}}_{n_{m},d_{m},j}(U_{m})\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{EIsoFunctors})}}{{\simeq}}\ \ \ \,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}\big({\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d_{1},j}({\mathrm{Ind}}^{S(n,d_{1})}_{S(n_{1},d_{1})}U_{1})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathpzc{b}}_{n_{m},d_{m},j}({\mathrm{Ind}}^{S(n,d_{m})}_{S(n_{m},d_{m})}U_{m})\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\ \ \ \ \ \

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}\big({\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d_{1},j}(W_{1})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathpzc{b}}_{n_{m},d_{m},j}(W_{m})\big),

as required.
∎

As a first application we obtain ‘commutativity’ of the Gelfand-Graev induction in the following sense:

Corollary 8.2.10
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
,
c,d\in\mathbb{N}
,
U\in{\bar{C}}_{c,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
and
W\in{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
. Then

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{c,d}^{c+d}(U\boxtimes W)\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{d,c}^{c+d}(W\boxtimes U).

Proof.

For
n\geq c+d
, we have using the theorem,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{GGI}}_{c,d}^{c+d}(U\boxtimes W)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{c,d}^{c+d}\big({\mathpzc{b}}_{n,c,j}({\mathpzc{a}}_{n,c,j}(U))\boxtimes{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}({\mathpzc{a}}_{n,d,j}(W))\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,c+d,j}\big({\mathpzc{a}}_{n,c,j}(U)\otimes{\mathpzc{a}}_{n,d,j}(W)\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,c+d,j}\big({\mathpzc{a}}_{n,d,j}(W)\otimes{\mathpzc{a}}_{n,c,j}(U)\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{d,c}^{c+d}\big({\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}({\mathpzc{a}}_{n,d,j}(W))\boxtimes({\mathpzc{b}}_{n,c,j}({\mathpzc{a}}_{n,c,j}(U))\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{GGI}}_{d,c}^{c+d}(W\boxtimes U),

as desired.
∎

As a second application, we show that Gelfand-Graev induction and restriction preserve the property of possessing (co)standard filtrations:

Theorem 8.2.11
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
{\underline{d}}=(d_{1},\dots,d_{m})\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then:

(i)

The functor
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}
sends
{\bar{C}}_{{\underline{d}},j}
-modules with standard (resp. costandard) filtrations to
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-modules with standard (resp. costandard) filtrations.

(ii)

The functor
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}
sends
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-modules with standard (resp. costandard) filtrations to
{\bar{C}}_{{\underline{d}},j}
-modules with standard (resp. costandard) filtrations.

Proof.

(i) For standard filtrations, it suffices to check that for arbitrary
\lambda^{1}\in{\mathcal{P}}(d_{1}),\dots,\lambda^{m}\in{\mathcal{P}}(d_{m})
the graded supermodule

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}(\Delta_{j}(\lambda^{1})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\Delta_{j}(\lambda^{m}))

has a standard filtration.
Let
n\geq d
.
By Theorem
8.2.9
, we have

{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}(\Delta_{j}(\lambda^{1})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes\Delta_{j}(\lambda^{m}))\cong{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(\Delta_{n,d_{1}}(\lambda^{1})\otimes\dots\otimes\Delta_{n,d_{m}}(\lambda^{m})).

So the result on standard filtrations follows since
\Delta_{n,d_{1}}(\lambda^{1})\otimes\dots\otimes\Delta_{n,d_{m}}(\lambda^{m})
has a standard filtration as an
S(n,d)
-module by Lemma
3.1.22
.

The argument for costandard
filtrations is similar or can be deduced from the result on standard filtrations using (
8.2.5
) and Lemma
6.2.18

(ii) We prove the result for costandard filtrations; the analogous result for standard
filtrations follows using (
8.2.5
) and Lemma
6.2.17
.
Take
V\in{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
with a costandard filtration. Using the cohomological criterion for costandard filtrations
[
13
, A2.2(iii)]
, we need to show that
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{{\bar{C}}_{{\underline{d}},j}}(W,{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}V)=0
for all
W\in{\bar{C}}_{{\underline{d}},j}\!\operatorname{-mod}
with a standard filtration. For such
W
, by (i) and the cohomological criterion for costandard filtrations, we have
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}({\mathrm{GGI}}_{\underline{d}}^{d}W,V)=0
. So the result follows from Corollary
8.1.23
.
∎

8.2c.
Double centralizer properties

Throughout the subsection we assume that
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
n\geq d
. In particular, we identify
{\mathcal{P}}(d)=\Uplambda_{+}(n,d)
.
Recall the theory of tilting modules and Ringel duality from §
3.1d
, in particular the tilting modules
T_{n,d}(\lambda)
for the classical Schur algebra
S(n,d)
.
For
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
, define

(8.2.12)

T_{j}(\lambda)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}(T_{n,d}(\lambda)).

Since
\beta_{n,d}
is an equivalence,
\{T_{j}(\lambda)\mid\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)\}
are the indecomposable tilting modules for
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
(independent of the choice of
n\geq d
for example thanks to (
8.2.4
)).

Lemma 8.2.13
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. The indecomposable tilting modules
\{T_{j}(\lambda)\mid\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)\}
are precisely the indecomposable summands of
\bigoplus_{\nu\in\Uplambda(d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\nu}
. Moreover, for
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
, the module
T_{j}(\lambda)
occurs exactly once as a summand of
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
, and if
T_{j}(\mu)
is a summand of
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
for some
\mu\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
, then
\mu\unlhd\lambda
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.1.30
, the indecomposable module

T_{n,d}(\lambda)
occurs exactly once as a summand of
\Lambda_{n,d}^{\lambda^{\prime}}
, and if
T_{n,d}(\mu)
is a summand of
\Lambda_{n,d}^{\lambda^{\prime}}
then
\mu\unlhd\lambda
.
Now the result follows on applying the functor
{\mathpzc{b}}_{n,d,j}
and using Lemma
8.2.2
.
∎

Corollary 8.2.14
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. For
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
, the graded supermodule
T_{j}(\lambda)
is the unique
indecomposable summand of
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
containing a graded subsupermodule isomorphic to
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
8.2.8
(i),
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
has a unique graded subsupermodule isomorphic to
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)
. By Lemma
8.2.13
,
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
has a unique summand isomorphic to
T_{j}(\lambda)
, and
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(\Delta_{j}(\lambda),V)=0

for any other summand
V
of
M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}
.
∎

Theorem 8.2.15
.

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
and
n\geq d
. The graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-supermodule

(8.2.16)

T_{j}(n,d)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\nu\in\Uplambda(n,d)}M_{d,j}{\mathtt{y}}_{\nu}

is a full tilting module.
Moreover, the Ringel dual
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}^{\prime}
of
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
relative to
T_{j}(n,d)
is precisely the algebra
S(n,d)^{\mathrm{op}}
where
S(n,d)
acts on
T_{j}(n,d)
as in Theorem
7.3.2
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.2.13
,
T_{j}(n,d)
is a full tilting module. The second statement is a restatement of Theorem
7.3.2
.
∎

In the following theorem,
T_{j}(n,d)
is as defined in (
8.2.16
) and the
S(n,d)
-action on
T_{j}(n,d)
is as in Theorem
7.3.2
, while the
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on
M_{d,j}
is as in Theorem
6.1.9
.

Theorem 8.2.17
.

(Double Centralizer Properties)

Let
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
.

(i)

If
n\geq d
then

\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}\big(T_{j}(n,d)\big)\cong S(n,d)\quad{and}\quad\operatorname{End}_{S(n,d)}\big(T_{j}(n,d))\cong{\bar{C}}_{d,j}.

(ii)

\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(M_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}\cong\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
and
\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}}(M_{d,j})\cong{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
.

Proof.

(i) follows from Theorem
8.2.15
and Lemma
3.1.29
.

(ii) By Theorem
6.1.9
, we already have
\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(M_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}\cong F\mathfrak{S}_{d}
.

Let
e\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\xi_{\omega_{d}}\in S(n,d)
, see (
3.1.13
).
By Lemma
3.1.31
, the composition factors of the socle and the head of
T_{n,d}(\lambda)
belong to the head of the projective
S(n,d)
-module
S(n,d)e
.

Now
T_{j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=T_{j}(n,d)
is a full tilting module for
S(n,d)=\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j}}(T_{j})
by Lemma
3.1.29
and Theorem
8.2.15
. So, by the previous paragraph, every composition factor of the socle and the head of
T_{j}
belongs to the head of
S(n,d)e
. By Lemma
2.2.27
, we deduce that
\operatorname{End}_{S(n,d)}(T_{j})\cong\operatorname{End}_{eS(n,d)e}(eT_{j})
. Switching to right actions via anti-automorphism
\tau
of Lemma
3.1.11
, and using (i), we have now shown that

{\bar{C}}_{d,j}\cong\operatorname{End}_{eS(n,d)e}(T_{j}e).

So, to prove (ii), it suffices to show that
\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}}(M_{d,j})\cong\operatorname{End}_{eS(n,d)e}(T_{j}e)
.

As left
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
-modules,
M_{d,j}\cong T_{j}e
. Recall the isomorphism
\kappa
from Lemma
3.1.14
and the explicit action of
S(n,d)
on
T_{j}
from Theorem
7.3.2
. Now it is easy to see that the
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},eS(n,d)e)
-bimodule
T_{j}e
is isomorphic to the
({\bar{C}}_{d,j},\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bimodule
M_{d,j}
, if we identify
\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
with
eS(n,d)e
via the isomoprhism
g\mapsto\mathtt{sgn}(g)\kappa(g)
for all
g\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
. In view of this, we have

\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{F}\mathfrak{S}_{d}}(M_{d,j})\cong\operatorname{End}_{eS(n,d)e}(T_{j}e)
. ∎

8.3.
Base change

In this section, we assume that the ground field
\mathbb{F}
is an
\mathcal{O}
-module. In fact, it will suffice to consider only the following two cases:

\bullet

\mathbb{F}=\mathbb{K}
, where
\mathbb{K}
is the (characteristic
0
) field of fractions
\mathbb{K}
of
\mathcal{O}
;

\bullet

\mathbb{F}=\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{m}
for a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of
\mathcal{O}
.

The algebras
S(n,d)
,
{\bar{C}}_{d,j}
, etc. as well as the modules
M_{d,j},\Gamma_{d,j}
, etc. were all defined for
\Bbbk=\mathcal{O}
or
\mathbb{F}
.
To specify the ground ring we will now use the notation like
S(n,d)_{\mathbb{F}}
,
S(n,d)_{\mathcal{O}}
,
M_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
,
M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
,
M_{d,j,\mathbb{K}}
, etc.
It is often possible to see that the base change
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}X_{\mathcal{O}}
for the object
X_{\mathcal{O}}
over
\mathcal{O}
is isomorphic to the corresponding object
X_{\mathbb{F}}
over
\mathbb{F}
. For example, we clearly have
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}R_{d\delta,\mathcal{O}}\cong R_{d\delta,\mathbb{F}}
and
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}C_{d,\mathcal{O}}\cong C_{d,\mathbb{F}}
.
What is often more difficult to see is that
X_{\mathcal{O}}
is
\mathcal{O}
-free, and so a priori the (graded) dimension of
X_{\mathbb{F}}
might depend on the characteristic of
\mathbb{F}
. For example, even though
R_{d\delta,\mathcal{O}}
is
\mathcal{O}
-free, we will not be able to prove the same for its quotient
\hat{C}_{d,\mathcal{O}}
or the idempotent truncation
C_{d,\mathcal{O}}
of
\hat{C}_{d,\mathcal{O}}
. However, we will establish the freeness for the quotient
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
of
C_{d,\mathcal{O}}
. Our main result in this direction is Theorem
8.3.8
below.

In what follows, we identify
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}C_{d,\mathcal{O}}=C_{d,\mathbb{F}}
, so given a graded
C_{d,\mathcal{O}}
-supermodule
V_{\mathcal{O}}
, the base change
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}V_{\mathcal{O}}
is a grade
C_{d,\mathbb{F}}
-supermodule.

8.3a.
Base change for modules

Lemma 8.3.1
.

As a graded
\mathcal{O}
-supermodule,
M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is finite rank free, and
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\simeq M_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
as graded
C_{d,\mathbb{F}}
-supermodules.

Proof.

This comes from Lemma
6.1.20
.
∎

We can identify
\mathcal{O}
with a subring of its field of fractions
\mathbb{K}
.
Given a finite dimensional graded
\mathbb{K}
-superspace
V_{\mathbb{K}}
, a
full rank lattice
in
V_{\mathbb{K}}
is a free graded
\mathcal{O}
-subsupermodule
V_{\mathcal{O}}
of
V_{\mathbb{K}}
such that
\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{O}}V_{\mathcal{O}}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}V_{\mathbb{K}}
. In this case
V_{\mathbb{K}}\simeq\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}V_{\mathcal{O}}
. Conversely, if
V_{\mathcal{O}}
is a finite rank free graded
\mathcal{O}
-supermodule then
V_{\mathcal{O}}
can be identified with a full rank lattice in
\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}V_{\mathcal{O}}
. The following result is standard.

Lemma 8.3.2
.

Suppose
V_{\mathcal{O}}
is a full rank lattice in a finite dimensional graded
\mathbb{K}
-superspace
V_{\mathbb{K}}
,
W_{\mathbb{K}}
is a graded
\mathbb{K}
-subsuperspace of
V_{\mathbb{K}}
and
W_{\mathcal{O}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=V_{\mathcal{O}}\cap W_{\mathbb{K}}
. Then:

(i)

W_{\mathcal{O}}
is a full rank lattice in
W_{\mathbb{K}}
;

(ii)

W_{\mathcal{O}}
is a direct summand of the graded
\mathcal{O}
-supermodule
V_{\mathcal{O}}
. In particular, the natural map
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}W_{\mathcal{O}}\to\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}V_{\mathcal{O}}
is injective.

Lemma 8.3.3
.

As a graded
\mathcal{O}
-supermodule,
\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is finite rank free, and
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\simeq\Gamma_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
as graded
C_{d,\mathbb{F}}
-supermodules. Moreover,

\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}=Y_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=C_{d,\mathcal{O}}{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.3.1
, we can identify
M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
with a full rank lattice in
M_{d,j,\mathbb{K}}
. Then by definition,
\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}=\Gamma_{d,j,\mathbb{K}}\cap M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
. So by Lemma
8.3.2
,
\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is a full rank lattice in
\Gamma_{d,j,\mathbb{K}}
and the natural map
\iota\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\to\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}=M_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
is injective. Since the action of
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
on
M_{d,j}
is compatible with base change, we have
\operatorname{Im}\iota\subseteq\Gamma_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
.
On the other hand, the natural homomorphism
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}Y_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\to M_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
has image
Y_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
. By (
8.1.11
), we have
Y_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\subseteq\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
, so
Y_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}\subseteq\operatorname{Im}\iota
. But
Y_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}=\Gamma_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
by Theorem
8.1.21
(i), so
\operatorname{Im}\iota=\Gamma_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
.

Finally, the embedding
Y_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\to\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
has to be an isomorphism since otherwise for some
\mathbb{F}
, the induced map
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}Y_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\to\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is not surjective, and so the composition
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}Y_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\to\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Gamma_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Gamma_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}=Y_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
is not surjective, giving a contradiction.
∎

Since induction commutes with base change, we deduce:

Corollary 8.3.4
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathcal{O}}
is finite rank free as a graded
\mathcal{O}
-supermodule, and
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathcal{O}}\simeq\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathbb{F}}
as graded
C_{d,\mathbb{F}}
-supermodules.

Let
\lambda\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
. By Theorem
8.2.8
(ii), we have

\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\Gamma_{j,\mathbb{F}}^{\lambda}u_{\lambda^{\prime}}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}.

So we define the corresponding ‘standard module over
\mathcal{O}
’ as follows:

\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathcal{O}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\Gamma_{j,\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}u_{\lambda^{\prime}}{\mathtt{x}}_{\lambda^{\prime}}.

Theorem 8.3.5
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
. Then
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathcal{O}}
is finite rank free as a graded
\mathcal{O}
-supermodule, and
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathcal{O}}\simeq\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{F}}
as graded
C_{d,\mathbb{F}}
-supermodules.

Proof.

By Corollary
8.3.4
, we have that

\Gamma_{j,\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}
is finite rank free as a graded
\mathcal{O}
-supermodule, and the embedding
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathcal{O}}\to\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathcal{O}}
induces a homomorphism of graded
C_{d,\mathbb{F}}
-supermodules
\iota\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathcal{O}}\to\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathcal{O}}=\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathbb{F}}
whose image is
\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{F}}
. In particular,
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathcal{O}})\geq\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{F}}
, and to establish that
\iota
is an isomorphism, it suffices to prove that
