\dim_{\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathcal{O}})=\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\Delta_{j}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{F}}
.

Let
n\geq d
. By (
8.2.1
) and Lemma
8.2.7
, we have

(8.3.6)

\begin{split}\sum_{\mu\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\Gamma^{\mu}_{j,\mathbb{F}}&=\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\Gamma_{j}(n,d)_{\mathbb{F}}\\
&=\sum_{\nu\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)}(\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathbb{F}})(\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\Delta_{n,d}(\nu)_{\mathbb{F}}),\end{split}

and

(8.3.7)

\begin{split}\sum_{\mu\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\Gamma^{\mu}_{j,\mathbb{K}}&=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\Gamma_{j}(n,d)_{\mathbb{K}}\\
&=\sum_{\nu\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)}(\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathbb{K}})(\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\Delta_{n,d}(\nu)_{\mathbb{K}}).\end{split}

By Corollary
8.3.4
, the left hand sides in (
8.3.6
) and (
8.3.7
) are equal to each other. Moreover, it is well known that
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\Delta_{n,d}(\nu)_{\mathbb{K}}=\dim_{\mathbb{F}}\Delta_{n,d}(\nu)_{\mathbb{F}}
for all
\nu\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
, see for example
[
19
, 5.4f]
. So it suffices to prove that
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathbb{K}}=\dim_{\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathcal{O}})
for all
\nu\in{\mathcal{P}}(d)
.
But the natural map
\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathcal{O}}\to\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Gamma^{\nu}_{j,\mathcal{O}}=\Gamma^{\nu}_{j,\mathbb{K}}
is injective and has image
\Gamma_{j,\mathbb{K}}^{\nu}u_{\nu^{\prime}}{\mathtt{x}}_{\nu^{\prime}}=\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathbb{K}}
, so
\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathcal{O}}
is a full rank lattice in
\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathbb{K}}
, which implies
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathbb{K}}=\dim_{\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Delta_{j}(\nu)_{\mathcal{O}})
.
∎

8.3b.
Base change for endomorphism algebras

Theorem 8.3.8
.

Let

\Gamma_{j}(n,d)_{\mathcal{O}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Gamma_{j,\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}
.

(i)

As a graded
\mathcal{O}
-supermodule,
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is finite rank free, and we have an isomorphism of graded superalgebras
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\cong{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
.

(ii)

For any
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
, the graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
-supermodule
\Gamma_{j,\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}
is projective.

(iii)

We have an isomorphism of graded superalgebras

\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}}(\Gamma_{j}(n,d)_{\mathcal{O}})\cong S(n,d)_{\mathcal{O}}.

(iv)

If
n\geq d
then
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)_{\mathcal{O}}
is a projective generator for
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
, and
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is graded Morita superequivalent to
S(n,d)_{\mathcal{O}}
.

Proof.

(i) By definition,
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is the
\mathcal{O}
-submodule of
\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}}(M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}})
consisting of the endomorphisms coming from

C_{d,\mathcal{O}}
acting on
M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
.
Taking into account Lemma
8.3.1
,
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is finite rank free as a graded
\mathcal{O}
-supermodule. Also, since
\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}C_{d,\mathcal{O}}\cong C_{d,\mathbb{K}}
, we have that
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is a full rank lattice in
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathbb{K}}
. In particular,
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}})=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}\nolimits_{\mathcal{O}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathbb{K}}
.

Moreover,
the natural inclusion

{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}{\hookrightarrow}\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}}(M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}})
yields a map

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\to\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{O}}(M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}})\simeq\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{F}}(M_{d,j,\mathbb{F}})

whose image is
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
.
Thus we have a surjective homomorphism
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\to{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
of graded superalgebras.
This map must be an isomorphism since the two algebras have the same dimension—indeed,
\dim_{\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}})=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathbb{K}}
by the previous paragraph, and
\dim_{\mathbb{K}}{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathbb{K}}=\dim{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
by Lemma
8.2.6
and Theorem
8.3.5
.

(ii) By Corollary
8.3.4
and Theorem
8.1.21
(ii),
for any field
\mathbb{F}
which is an
\mathcal{O}
-module,
we have that
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathcal{O}}\cong\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathbb{F}}
is a projective graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}
-supermodule. Therefore, in view of the Universal Coefficients Theorem,
\Gamma^{\nu}_{j,\mathcal{O}}
is a projective graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
-supermodule.

(iii) For
\Bbbk\in\{\mathcal{O},\mathbb{F}\}
, denote
E_{\Bbbk}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\Bbbk}}(\Gamma_{j}(n,d)_{\Bbbk}).

By Theorem
7.3.4
, we have
E_{\mathbb{F}}\cong S(n,d)_{\mathbb{F}}
.
By
[
36
, Lemma 14.5]
, we have a natural embedding

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E_{\mathcal{O}}{\hookrightarrow}E_{\mathbb{F}}
which is an isomorphism for
\mathbb{F}=\mathbb{K}
.
Moreover,
E_{\mathcal{O}}
is a
\mathcal{O}
-free, so

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{F}}(\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E_{\mathcal{O}})

\displaystyle=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}(\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E_{\mathcal{O}})=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}E_{\mathbb{K}}

\displaystyle=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}S(n,d)_{\mathbb{K}}=\dim_{\mathbb{F}}S(n,d)_{\mathbb{F}}=\dim_{\mathbb{F}}E_{\mathbb{F}}.

By dimensions, the natural embedding
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}E_{\mathcal{O}}\to E_{\mathbb{F}}
must be an isomorphism.

By Theorem
7.3.4
, the basis element
\xi_{\lambda,\mu}^{g}
of
E_{\mathbb{K}}=S(n,d)_{\mathbb{K}}
acts as zero on all summands except
\Gamma^{\mu}_{j,\mathbb{K}}
, on which the action is induced by the right multiplication by
X_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}
. By definition,
\Gamma^{\nu}_{j,\mathcal{O}}=\Gamma^{\nu}_{j,\mathbb{K}}\cap M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
for all
\nu
. Also,
X_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}\in\mathcal{O}\mathfrak{S}_{d}
, therefore the right multiplication by
X_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}
stabilizes
M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
. Hence
\Gamma^{\mu}_{j,\mathcal{O}}X_{\mu,\lambda}^{g}\subseteq\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathcal{O}}
, so each
\xi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}\in E_{\mathbb{K}}
restricts to give a well-defined element of
E_{\mathcal{O}}
. We have constructed an isomorphic copy
S_{\mathcal{O}}
of
S(n,d)_{\mathcal{O}}
in
E_{\mathcal{O}}
, namely, the
\mathcal{O}
-span of the standard basis elements
\xi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}\in S(n,d)_{\mathbb{K}}
.

It remains to show that
S_{\mathcal{O}}=E_{\mathcal{O}}
. We have a short exact sequence of
\mathcal{O}
-modules:

0\to S_{\mathcal{O}}\to E_{\mathcal{O}}\to Q_{\mathcal{O}}\to 0
, and we need to prove that
Q_{\mathcal{O}}=0
, for which it suffices to prove that
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}Q_{\mathcal{O}}=0
for any
\mathbb{F}
. Tensoring with
\mathbb{F}
, we have an exact sequence

\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}S_{\mathcal{O}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}E_{\mathbb{F}}\to\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}Q_{\mathcal{O}}\to 0.

The map
\iota
sends
1\otimes\xi^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
to the corresponding endomorphism
\upzeta^{g}_{\lambda,\mu}
defined as in Theorem
7.3.4
. Hence,
\iota
is injective, so
\iota
is an isomorphism by dimensions, and
\mathbb{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}Q_{\mathcal{O}}=0
, as required.

(iv) By (ii),
\Gamma_{j}(n,d)_{\mathcal{O}}
is a projective graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
-supermodule. For
n\geq d
, it is a projective generator, because this is so on tensoring with
\mathbb{F}
, using (i) and Theorem
8.1.21
(iii). ∎

Corollary 8.3.9
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
.

(i)

The functor
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}
restricts to a functor
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j,\mathcal{O}}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\!\operatorname{-mod}
. Furthermore, for
V\in{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j,\mathcal{O}}\!\operatorname{-mod}
, we have
{\mathrm{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}V\simeq{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\bar{f}_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j,\mathcal{O}}}V
.

(ii)

The functor
{\mathrm{GGR}}_{\lambda}^{d}
restricts to a functor
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j,\mathcal{O}}\!\operatorname{-mod}
.

Proof.

The proof of Corollary
8.1.22
now generalizes using the fact that by Theorem
8.3.8
, every indecomposable projective graded
{\bar{C}}_{\lambda,j,d}
-supermodule is a submodule of
M_{\lambda,j,\mathcal{O}}
.
∎

Lemma 8.3.10
.

The graded
C_{d,\mathcal{O}}
-module
G_{d,j}
is indecomposable.

Proof.

We have
G_{d,j,\mathbb{F}}=\mathbb{F}\otimes G_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is indecomposable by Lemma
8.1.2
, so
G_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
is also indecomposable.
∎

Corollary 8.3.11
.

Let
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
and set

\bar{f}^{\lambda,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={f}^{\lambda,j}+{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d,\mathcal{O}}}(M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}})\in C_{d,\mathcal{O}}/{\mathrm{Ann}}_{C_{d,\mathcal{O}}}(M_{d,j,\mathcal{O}})={\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}.

Then
{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{\lambda,j}}\,\Uppi^{dj}\,{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}\bar{f}^{\lambda,j}\simeq\Gamma^{\lambda}_{j,\mathcal{O}}
as graded
{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\mathcal{O}}
-supermodules.

Proof.

The same proof as for Corollary
8.1.25
works, using Corollary
8.3.9
(i) instead of Corollary
8.1.22
(i) to reduce to the special case
\lambda=(d)
, Theorem
8.3.8
instead of Theorem
8.1.21
, and Lemma
8.3.10
instead of Lemma
8.1.2
.
∎

Corollary
8.3.11
and Theorem
8.3.8
(iii) now imply:

Corollary 8.3.12
.

We have an isomorphism of graded superalgebras

\textstyle\operatorname{End}_{{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\Bbbk}}\Big(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}{\mathpzc{Q}}^{a_{\lambda,j}}\,\Uppi^{dj}\,{\bar{C}}_{d,j,\Bbbk}\bar{f}^{\lambda,j}\Big)\cong S(n,d)_{\Bbbk}.

Chapter 9
Regrading imaginary Schur-Weyl duality

Recall from §
5.1a
the regrading
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
of
C_{d}
. By Theorem
5.2.23
,
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
is non-negatively graded. For
\nu\in\Uplambda(n,d)
, recalling (
5.1.11
), we have a parabolic subalgebra

{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}={\mathsf{C}}_{\nu_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu_{n}}\subseteq\mathsf{f}_{\nu}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\nu}

and the functors

{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\nu}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathsf{GGR}}_{\nu}^{d}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\!\operatorname{-mod}\to{\mathsf{C}}_{\nu}\!\operatorname{-mod}

defined in (
5.1.12
).

For
j\in J
, identifying
{\mathsf{C}}_{1}
with
H_{1}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})={\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[{\mathsf{z}}]
via the isomorphism of Theorem
5.1.16
and recalling the graded
{\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}[{\mathsf{z}}]
-supermodules
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}=\Bbbk{\mathsf{v}}_{j}
from §
3.2b
, we obtain the graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{1}
-supermodule
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}
. Recalling the
C_{1}
-module
L_{j}
from §
4.2h
and the functors
{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}
from
§
5.1b
, note that
{\mathsf{L}}_{j}\simeq{\mathpzc{h}}_{1}L_{j}
.
Moreover, by (
3.2.12
), we have

(9.0.1)

{\mathsf{L}}_{j}\simeq{\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j}/({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j})^{>0}.

9.1.
Regraded imaginary tensor spaces

9.1a.
Mixed imaginary tensor space
{\mathsf{M}}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}

Let
\text{\boldmath$j$}\in J^{d}
. The corresponding (mixed) imaginary tensor space is

{\mathsf{M}}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathsf{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}({\mathsf{L}}_{j_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{L}}_{j_{d}})={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}}({\mathsf{L}}_{j_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{L}}_{j_{d}})

with generator

{\mathsf{v}}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes{\mathsf{v}}_{j_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathsf{v}}_{j_{n}}.

We consider the graded left
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
-subsupermodule of
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}
generated by
({\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})^{>0}
:

(9.1.1)

{\mathsf{N}}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathsf{C}}_{d}({\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})^{>0}.

Lemma 9.1.2
.

For
\text{\boldmath$j$}=j_{1}\cdots j_{d}\in J^{d}
we have an isomorphism of graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
-supermodules

{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}/{\mathsf{N}}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathsf{M}}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}}({\mathsf{L}}_{j_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{L}}_{j_{d}}),\ \mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}+{\mathsf{N}}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\mapsto\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes{\mathsf{v}}_{j_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathsf{v}}_{j_{d}}.

Proof.

Since the algebra
{\mathsf{C}}_{1}
is non-negatively graded, we have for
{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}={\mathsf{C}}_{1}^{\otimes d}
:

{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}^{>0}=\sum_{r=1}^{d}{\mathsf{C}}_{1}^{\otimes(r-1)}\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{1}^{>0}\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{1}^{\otimes(d-r)}.

So

({\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})^{>0}=\sum_{r=1}^{d}{\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{r-1}}\otimes({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{r}})^{>0}\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{r+1}}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{d}},

and

{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}/({\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})^{>0}\simeq({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{1}}/({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{1}})^{>0})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{d}}/({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{d}})^{>0}).

Now, using (
9.0.1
) and exactness of Gelfand-Graev induction, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}({\mathsf{L}}_{j_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{L}}_{j_{d}})

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}\big(({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{1}}/({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{1}})^{>0})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{d}}/({\mathsf{C}}_{1}\mathsf{f}^{j_{d}})^{>0})\big)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}\big({\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}/({\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})^{>0}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\big({\mathsf{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\big)/\big({\mathsf{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}({\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})^{>0}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq\big({\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}\big)/\big({\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}}({\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})^{>0}\big)

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})/({\mathsf{C}}_{d}({\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}})^{>0})

\displaystyle={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$j$}}/{\mathsf{N}}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}},

which implies the claim.
∎

9.1b.
Regraded imaginary tensor space
{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}

Let
j\in J
. As a special case of the construction of §
9.1a
,
we consider the graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
-supermodule

{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathsf{M}}_{j^{d}}={\mathsf{GGI}}_{\omega_{d}}^{d}\,{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\omega_{d}}}{\mathsf{L}}_{j}^{\boxtimes d}.

with generator
{\mathsf{v}}_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathsf{v}}_{j^{d}}=\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}\otimes{\mathsf{v}}_{j}^{\otimes d}
.

By Lemma
9.1.2
,

(9.1.3)

{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}\simeq{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}/{\mathsf{N}}_{j^{d}}.

We identify
{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}/{\mathsf{N}}_{j^{d}}
using this isomorphism.

There is a natural right action of the graded superalgebra
\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}
on
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}
via right multiplication. Identifying
\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}
with
{\mathsf{e}}^{j^{d}}H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}){\mathsf{e}}^{j^{d}}
via the isomorphism of Theorem
5.1.16
, we get the right action of
{\mathsf{e}}^{j^{d}}H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}){\mathsf{e}}^{j^{d}}
on
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}
.
Recalling Theorem
3.2.18
, we have an embedding

\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathsf{e}}^{j^{d}}H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}){\mathsf{e}}^{j^{d}},\ w\mapsto{\mathsf{e}}^{j^{d}}w{\mathsf{e}}^{j^{d}}=w{\mathsf{e}}^{j^{d}}.

This gives the right action of
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
on
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}
. Taking into account the formula for the image of
s_{r}
under the isomorphism of Theorem
5.1.16
, we have for this action:

({\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}})\cdot s_{r}=(-1)^{j+1}{\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}\dot{\mathsf{s}}_{r}=(-1)^{j+1}{\mathsf{c}}\dot{\mathsf{s}}_{r}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}\qquad({\mathsf{c}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d},\ 1\leq r<d).

By Lemma
5.1.19
, recalling (
9.1.3
), this action factors through to the right action of
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
on the module
{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{j^{d}}/{\mathsf{N}}_{j^{d}}
such that

(9.1.4)

({\mathsf{c}}{\mathsf{v}}_{d,j})\cdot s_{r}=(-1)^{j+1}{\mathsf{c}}\dot{\mathsf{s}}_{r}{\mathsf{v}}_{d,j}\qquad({\mathsf{c}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d},\ 1\leq r<d),

hence

(9.1.5)

({\mathsf{c}}{\mathsf{v}}_{d,j})\cdot w=(-1)^{{\mathtt{l}}(w)(j+1)}{\mathsf{c}}\dot{\mathsf{w}}{\mathsf{v}}_{d,j}\qquad({\mathsf{c}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d},\ w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}),

making
{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}
into a graded
({\mathsf{C}}_{d},\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bisupermodule.

Recalling the graded
C_{d}
-supermodule
M_{d,j}
from (
6.1.1
) and using

{\mathsf{L}}_{j}\simeq{\mathpzc{h}}_{1}L_{j}
, we deduce using Lemma
5.1.13
that

(9.1.6)

{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}\simeq{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}M_{d,j}.

We identify

{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}
with
{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}M_{d,j}
via the isomorphism of (
9.1.6
).
Recalling the terminology of §
5.1b
,
we can now speak of the element of
{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}
corresponding to an element of
M_{d,j}
under the functor
{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}
.
Then the functor
{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}
intertwines the
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on
{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}
just defined and the
\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-action on
M_{d,j}
from §
6.1c
in the following sense:

Lemma 9.1.7
.

Let
m\in M_{d,j}
,
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}
and
{\mathsf{m}}
be the elements of
{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}
corresponding to
m
under the functor
{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}
. Then
{\mathsf{m}}\cdot w
corresponds to
m\cdot w
under the functor
{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}
.

Proof.

This follows from
(
9.1.5
), (
6.1.10
) and
(
5.1.9
).
∎

Proposition 9.1.8
.

The right action of
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
on
{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}
is faithful and induces the graded superalgebra isomorphism
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}\cong\operatorname{End}_{{\mathsf{C}}_{d}}({\mathsf{M}}_{d,j})^{\mathrm{sop}}
.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
9.1.7
and Theorem
6.1.9
.
∎

Proposition 9.1.9
.

We have:

(i)

For each
\lambda\in\Uplambda(d)
,
there is a bidegree
(0,{\bar{0}})
isomorphism of right
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-modules

\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}

which maps
\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{v}}_{d,j}
to
1\otimes 1_{\mathfrak{S}_{d}}
.

(ii)

{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda_{0}(d)}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}
.

Proof.

(i) follows from Lemmas
9.1.7
and
6.1.23
.

(ii) follows from (
9.1.6
) and Lemma
6.1.19
.
∎

Recalling the general set-up of Example
2.2.21
, we consider the graded superalgebra

(9.1.10)

{\mathsf{C}}_{j}(n,d)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\operatorname{End}_{{\mathsf{C}}_{d}}\Big(\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}\Big)^{\mathrm{sop}}\simeq\bigoplus_{\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}.

We identify
{\mathsf{C}}_{j}(n,d)
with
\bigoplus_{\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}
via the isomorphism above.
Then the action of
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
on
{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}
yields the action of the graded superalgebra
{\mathsf{C}}_{j}(n,d)=\bigoplus_{\lambda,\mu\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\mathsf{f}^{\mu,j}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}
on

{\mathsf{M}}_{j}(n,d)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,j}{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}.

In view of Proposition
9.1.9
, we have a graded
({\mathsf{C}}_{j}(n,d),\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d})
-bisupermodule structure on
{\mathsf{M}}_{j}(n,d)
, and as, a graded right
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}
-supermodule,

{\mathsf{M}}_{j}(n,d)\simeq\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\Uplambda(n,d)}\Bbbk_{\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\otimes_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}}\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}.

So

\operatorname{End}_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}({\mathsf{M}}_{j}(n,d))\cong S(n,d),

the classical Schur algebra, see (
3.1.9
).
The natural graded superalgebra homomorphism
{\varphi}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathsf{C}}_{j}(n,d)\to\operatorname{End}_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}({\mathsf{M}}_{j}(n,d))
restricts to the graded superalgebra homomorphism

\bar{\varphi}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\mathsf{C}}_{j}(n,d)^{0}\to\operatorname{End}_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}({\mathsf{M}}_{j}(n,d)).

Theorem 9.1.11
.

Let
j\in J
. Then
\bar{\varphi}
is an isomorphism. In particular,
{\mathsf{C}}_{j}(n,d)^{0}\cong S(n,d)
.

Proof.

If
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
, then by Theorem
8.2.17
(ii), the natural homomorphism
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\to\operatorname{End}_{\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{d}}({\mathsf{M}}_{d,j})
is surjective, which implies that
{\varphi}
is surjective. But
S(n,d)
is concentrated in degree
0
, so we deduce that
\bar{\varphi}
must be surjective. On the other hand, by Lemmas
3.1.8
and
5.2.28
, we have
\dim{\mathsf{C}}_{j}(n,d)^{0}\leq\dim S(n,d)
, so
{\varphi}
is an isomorphism by dimensions.

The result over
\Bbbk=\mathcal{O}
follows from that over the quotient fields
\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{m}
upon extension of scalars, since
S(n,d)
is torsion-free as an
\mathcal{O}
-module.
∎

9.1c.
The algebra
{\mathsf{C}}(n,d)

Recall from §
5.2e
and (
2.1.16
) how we consider the multicompositions from
\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)
as colored compositions from
\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n\ell,d)
via the embedding
{\mathtt{b}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)\to\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n\ell,d)
. For
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)
, we have the idempotents

\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
and the composition
{\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})\in\Uplambda(J,d)
, see (
5.2.26
).

We consider the
graded superalgebra

{\mathsf{C}}(n,d)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\operatorname{End}_{{\mathsf{C}}_{d}}\Big(\bigoplus_{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}\Big)^{\mathrm{sop}}\simeq\bigoplus_{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$},\text{\boldmath$\mu$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}},

where we have used the isomorphism (
2.2.22
).

If
n\geq d
,
this
is just a Morita equivalent version of
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
(which is sometimes a little bit more convenient to work with):

Lemma 9.1.12
.

If
n\geq d
then the graded superalgebras
{\mathsf{C}}(n,d)
and
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
are graded Morita superequivalent.

Proof.

We have to show that
{\mathsf{P}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\bigoplus_{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}
is a projective generator for
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
. If
\Bbbk=\mathcal{O}
, it suffices to prove this on tensoring with
\mathbb{F}
. So we may assume that
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
. Then it suffices to see that
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathsf{C}}_{d}}({\mathsf{P}},{\mathsf{L}})\neq 0
for any
{\mathsf{L}}\in{\mathrm{Irr}}({\mathsf{C}}_{d})
. Recalling (
5.1.7
), by Theorem
6.2.7
(i), we may assume that
{\mathsf{L}}={\mathsf{L}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathpzc{h}}_{d}(L(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}))
for some
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{J}(d)
. Since
n\geq d
, we may assume that
\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d)
, and then

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathsf{C}}_{d}}({\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}},{\mathsf{L}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}))\simeq\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}{\mathsf{L}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})\neq 0
by Corollary
6.2.3
and (
6.2.6
).
∎

For a composition
{\underline{d}}\in\Uplambda(J,d)
, we also have the graded superalgebra

{\mathsf{C}}(n,{\underline{d}})\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\operatorname{End}_{{\mathsf{C}}_{d}}\Big(\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d),\\
{\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})={\underline{d}}\end{subarray}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}\Big)^{\mathrm{sop}}\simeq\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{\boldmath$\lambda$},\text{\boldmath$\mu$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d),\\
{\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})={\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})={\underline{d}}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}.

For degree
0
components, from Lemma
5.2.27
, we immediately have:

Lemma 9.1.13
.

We have the decomposition of
{\mathsf{C}}(n,d)^{0}
as a direct sum of graded superalgebras:

{\mathsf{C}}(n,d)^{0}=\bigoplus_{{\underline{d}}\in\Uplambda(J,d)}{\mathsf{C}}(n,{\underline{d}})^{0}.

For
j\in J
, recall the graded superalgebra
{\mathsf{C}}_{j}(n,d)
from (
9.1.10
).

Lemma 9.1.14
.

Let
{\underline{d}}\in\Uplambda(J,d)
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathsf{C}}(n,{\underline{d}})^{0}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{\boldmath$\lambda$},\text{\boldmath$\mu$}\in\Uplambda^{J}(n,d),\\
{\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\lambda$})={\underline{d}}(\text{\boldmath$\mu$})={\underline{d}}\end{subarray}}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\mu$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{{\underline{d}}}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\text{\boldmath$\lambda$}}\,\cong\,{\mathsf{C}}_{0}(n,d_{0})^{0}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathsf{C}}_{\ell-1}(n,d_{\ell-1})^{0}.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Proposition
5.2.29
.
∎

Corollary 9.1.15
.

We have

{\mathsf{C}}(n,d)^{0}\cong\bigoplus_{{\underline{d}}=(d_{0},\dots,d_{\ell-1})\in\Uplambda(J,d)}S(n,d_{0})\otimes\dots\otimes S(n,d_{\ell-1}).

Proof.

This follows from Lemmas
9.1.13
,
9.1.14
and Theorem
9.1.11
.
∎

9.2.
Regrading divided powers

9.2a.
Mixed imaginary tensor spaces
{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}

Given a colored composition
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
as in §
2.1b
, we have the corresponding (mixed) imaginary tensor space

{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}({\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}}({\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})

with generator

{\mathsf{v}}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}\otimes{\mathsf{v}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes{\mathsf{v}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}.

By transitivity of the Gelfand-Graev induction, we have an isomorphism of graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
-supermodules

{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq{\mathsf{M}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}

under which
{\mathsf{v}}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
corresponds to
{\mathsf{v}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
. So the mixed tensor spaces
{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
are no different from the mixed tensor spaces
{\mathsf{M}}_{\text{\boldmath$j$}}
defined in §
9.1a
, but the notation
{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
is convenient for dealing with the right action of
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
on
{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
.

By the
n=1
case considered in §
9.1b
, for
t=1,\dots,n
, we have the right action of

\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda_{t}}
on
{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{t},i_{t}}
which commutes with the left
{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{t}}
-action. Hence we have the action of

\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}=\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\times\dots\times\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda_{n}}
on
{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}
which commutes with the left
{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}
-action. This induces the right action of
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
on
{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
which commutes with the left
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
-action.

9.2b.
Mixed divided powers

Define

{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{{\mathsf{m}}\in{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mid{\mathsf{m}}\cdot w=w\ \,\text{for all}\ \,w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\}.

As a special case, we define the graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
-supermodules

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{d,j}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{{\mathsf{m}}\in{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}\mid{\mathsf{m}}\cdot w={\mathsf{m}}\ \text{for all}\ w\in\mathfrak{S}_{d}\}.

Comparing with (
7.2.2
), it follows immediately from Lemma
9.1.7
that
{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{d,j}\simeq{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}\Gamma_{d,j}
.

Lemma 9.2.1
.

We have
{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{d,j}={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{d,j}{\mathsf{M}}_{d,j}
.

Proof.

Since
{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{d,j}\simeq{\mathpzc{h}}_{d}\Gamma_{d,j}
, it suffices to prove that

\Gamma_{d,j}=C_{d}{f}^{d,j}M_{d,j}
.
When
\Bbbk=\mathbb{F}
, this follows from (
8.1.8
) and Theorem
8.1.21
(i). When
\Bbbk=\mathcal{O}
this follows from Lemma
8.3.3
.
∎

Lemma 9.2.2
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. Then:

(i)

{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\big({\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}\big)
;

(ii)

{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\big(\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\otimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}\big)
.

Proof.

(i) By Corollary
6.2.10
,
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}
is a finitely generated projective right graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}
-supermodule, so
there is a right graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}
-supermodule
{\mathsf{W}}
such that

{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}\oplus{\mathsf{W}}
is a free right graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}
-supermodule. It is easy to see using the freeness that then the
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
-invariants of
({\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}\oplus{\mathsf{W}})\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}}({\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})

are exactly

\displaystyle({\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}\oplus{\mathsf{W}})\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}}({\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})

\displaystyle\simeq\,

\displaystyle\big({\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}({\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})\big)\,\oplus\,\big({\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}}({\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})\big).

But

\displaystyle({\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}\oplus{\mathsf{W}})\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}}({\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})

\displaystyle\simeq\,

\displaystyle\big({\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}({\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})\big)\,\oplus\,\big({\mathsf{W}}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}}({\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})\big),

and it now follows that the
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
-invariants of
{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}({\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})

are exactly
{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}({\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})
.

(ii) By (i) and Lemma
9.2.1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathsf{GGI}}_{\lambda}^{d}\big({\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}\big)

\displaystyle={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}\otimes_{{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}}\big(({\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{1}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}})\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes({\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda_{n}}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}})\big)

\displaystyle={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\big(\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\otimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}\big),

as required.
∎

For
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
, we have the word
i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}\in J^{d}
.
By Lemma
9.1.2
, we have an isomorphism of
graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
-supermodules

(9.2.3)

{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}/{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}},\ {\mathsf{v}}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mapsto\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}+{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}.

The graded superalgebra
\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
acts on
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
on the right by right multiplication. Identifying
\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
with
{\mathsf{e}}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}){\mathsf{e}}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
via the isomorphism of Theorem
5.1.16
, we get the right action of
{\mathsf{e}}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}){\mathsf{e}}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
on
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
.
Recalling Theorem
3.2.18
and the defining relations of
H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell})
, we have an embedding

\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathsf{e}}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}H_{d}({\mathsf{A}}_{\ell}){\mathsf{e}}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}},\ w\mapsto{\mathsf{e}}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}w\,{\mathsf{e}}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}=w\,{\mathsf{e}}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}.

This gives the right action of
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
on
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
. Taking into account the formula for the image of
s_{r}
under the isomorphism of Theorem
5.1.16
, we have for this action:

({\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}})\cdot s_{r}=(-1)^{i_{t}+1}{\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}\dot{\mathsf{s}}_{r}=(-1)^{i_{t}+1}{\mathsf{c}}\dot{\mathsf{s}}_{r}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}\qquad({\mathsf{c}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d})

if
s_{r}
belongs to the
t
th component
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda_{t}}
of
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\times\dots\times\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda_{n}}=\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
.
By Lemma
5.1.19
, this action factors through to the right action of
\Bbbk\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
on the module
{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\simeq{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}/{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
such that

(9.2.4)

({\mathsf{c}}{\mathsf{v}}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})\cdot s_{r}=(-1)^{j_{t}+1}{\mathsf{c}}\dot{\mathsf{s}}_{r}{\mathsf{v}}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\qquad({\mathsf{c}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d})

if
s_{r}
belongs to the
t
th component
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda_{t}}
of
\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda_{1}}\times\dots\times\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda_{n}}=\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
.

Define the left graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
-supermodule

\hat{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{{\mathsf{m}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}\mid{\mathsf{m}}\cdot w={\mathsf{m}}\ \text{for all}\ w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\}.

Lemma 9.2.5
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. Then
\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\in\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\hat{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
.

Proof.

By (
5.2.32
), we have
\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\in\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
. Moreover,
\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\cdot w=\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
for all
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}
by Proposition
5.2.36
(ii) and (
9.2.4
).
∎

Define the left graded
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}
-supermodule

\tilde{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{{\mathsf{m}}\in{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}\mid{\mathsf{m}}\cdot w-{\mathsf{m}}\in{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}\ \text{for all}\ w\in\mathfrak{S}_{\lambda}\}.

Lemma 9.2.6
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. Then

\tilde{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}={\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}+{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we identify
{\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
with
{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}/{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}
via the isomorphism (
9.2.3
) so

\mathsf{f}_{\lambda}\otimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{1},i_{1}}\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes{\mathsf{M}}_{\lambda_{n},i_{n}}=({\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}+{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}})/{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}.

Hence by Lemma
9.2.2
(ii), under this identification, we have

{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}=({\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}+{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}})/{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}.

By definition,
\tilde{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
is the preimage of
{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
under the canonical surjection

{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}{\twoheadrightarrow}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}/{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}={\mathsf{M}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}.

So the result follows.
∎

9.2c.
On the idempotent truncation
\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}_{\omega_{d}}

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
.
By Lemmas
5.2.33
and
9.2.5
,

g_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\in\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}(\hat{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{0}\subseteq\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}(\tilde{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{0}.

Lemma 9.2.7
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}),(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
and
{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}(\tilde{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{0}
. Then
{\mathsf{v}}={\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
for some
{\mathsf{c}}\in\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.2.6
, we have

{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}+\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}.

Since all algebras are non-negatively graded, it follows from the definition (
9.1.1
) that
{\mathsf{N}}_{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}}^{0}=0
, and taking into account the assumption
\deg({\mathsf{v}})=0
, we actually have

{\mathsf{v}}\in(\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{0}(\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}})^{0}.

But

(\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{\lambda}\mathsf{f}^{i_{1}^{\lambda_{1}}\cdots i_{n}^{\lambda_{n}}})^{0}

is spanned by
\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
thanks to Lemma
5.2.34
.
∎

Corollary 9.2.8
.

Let
(\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}),(\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$})\in\Uplambda^{\operatorname{col}}(n,d)
. If
{\mathsf{v}}\in\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}(\hat{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}})^{0}
then
{\mathsf{v}}={\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g}_{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
for some
{\mathsf{c}}\in\mathsf{f}^{\mu,\text{\boldmath$j$}}{\mathsf{C}}_{d}^{0}\mathsf{f}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
.

Proof.

Follows from Lemma
9.2.7
using
\hat{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}\subseteq\tilde{\mathsf{\Gamma}}^{\lambda,\text{\boldmath$i$}}
.
∎
